**2-NĀMA-KAPPA**  
 **2-NOUN CHAPTER**

**PAṬHAMA-KAṆḌA**  
**FIRST SECTION**

**52\. 60\. Jinavacanayuttaṃ hi.** \[Jinavacanayuttaṃ \+ hi, 2\]

“Jinavacanayuttaṃ hi” icc etaṃ adhikāratthaṃ veditabbaṃ.

---

Conforming to the Word of the Victorious One.

“Conforming to the Word of the Victorious One (Buddha)”. This should be understood as a governing aphorism,[^1] i.e., as applying to all the suttas backward and forward.

---

**53\. 61\. Liṅgañ ca nippajjate (192).** \[Liṅgaṃ \+ ca \+ nippajjate, 3\]

Yathā yatthā jinavacanayuttaṃ hi liṅgaṃ, tathā tathā idha liṅgañ ca nippajjate.

Taṃ yathā? Eso no satthā, brahmā, attā, sakhā, rājā.

---

And the base should be established.[^2]

Here the base should be established in such a way that it conforms to the Word of the Victorious One.

As what? Eso no Satthā (this is our Teacher), brahmā (Brahmā), attā (the self; oneself), sakhā (friend), rājā (king).

---

**54\. 62\. Tato ca vibhattiyo (202-3).** \[Tato \+ ca \+ vibhattiyo, 3\]

Tato jinavacanayuttehi liṅgehi vibhattiyo parā honti.

---

And after those, the \[nominal\] inflections.[^3]

Inflections are placed after the bases that conform to the Word of the Victorious One.

---

**55\. 63\. Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su (200).** \[Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su, 1\]

Kā ca pana tāyo vibhattiyo? Si, yo iti paṭhamā; aṃ, yo iti dutiyā; nā, hi iti tatiyā; sa, naṃ iti catutthī; smā, hi iti pañcamī; sa, naṃ iti chaṭṭhī; smiṃ, su iti sattamī.

**Vibhatti** icc anena kvattho? Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se.

---

‘Si, yo’, ‘aṃ, yo’, ‘nā, hi’, ‘sa, naṃ’, ‘smā, hi’, ‘sa, naṃ’, ‘smiṃ, su’.[^4]

And which are those inflections? ‘Si, yo’ are the first \[pair\]; ‘aṃ, yo’ the second; ‘nā, hi’ the third; ‘sa, naṃ’ the fourth; ‘smā, hi’ the fifth; ‘sa, naṃ’ the sixth; ‘smiṃ, su’ the seventh.

What is the use of saying “inflection”? \[To use it in suttas such as\] “Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se” (§120), etc. When ‘sa’ follows, “amha” together with the inflection \[is substituted by\] “mamaṃ”.

---

**56\. 64\. Tadanuparodhena.** \[Taṃ-an-uparodhena, 1\]

Yathā yathā tesaṃ jinavacanānaṃ anuparodho, tathā tathā idha liṅgañ ca nippajjate.

---

By not contradicting those.[^5]

Here the gender \[and the completed form\] should be established in such a way that it does not contradict the Words of the Victorious One.

---

**57\. 71\. Ālapane si gasañño (204).** \[Ālapane \+ si \+ gasañño, 3\]

Ālapanatthe **si gas**añño hoti. 

Bhoti ayye (Vin. ii, 280); bhoti kaññe; bhoti Kharādiye (Khu. v, 4). 

**Ālapane** ti kimatthaṃ? Sā ayyā (Vin. ii, 307). 

**Sī** ti kimatthaṃ? Bhotiyo ayyāyo (Vin. ii, 288). 

**Ga** icc anena kvattho? Ghat’ e ca.

---

In the vocative, ‘si’ is named ‘ga’.

In the meaning of vocative, ‘si’ has the name ‘ga’.

---

Examples:

1. **Bhoti ayye** \= Dear lady\![^6]  
2. **Bhoti kaññe** \= Dear girl\!  
3. **Bhoti Kharādiye** \= Dear Kharādiyā\!

What is the purpose of saying “vocative”? \[To prevent the operation of this rule in other cases such as\] “Sā ayyā” (that lady) –here the case is not vocative but nominative–.

What is the purpose of saying ‘si’? \[To prevent the operation of this rule in the example\] “Bhotiyo ayyāyo” –here the inflection is ‘yo’, not ‘si’–.[^7]

What is the use of the name ‘ga’? \[For the use of the name ‘ga’ in suttas such as\] “Ghat’ e ca” (§114). After ‘gha’ there is \[substitution of ‘ga’\] by ‘e’.[^8]

---

**58\. 29\. Ivaṇṇ’-uvaṇṇā jha-lā (205).** \[Ivaṇṇa-uvaṇṇā \+ jha-lā, 2\]

**Ivaṇṇ’-uvaṇṇā** icc ete **jha-la**saññā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Isino, aggino, gahapatino, daṇḍino. Setuno, ketuno, bhikkhuno. Sayambhuno, abhibhuno. 

**Jha la** icc anena kvattho? Jha-lato sassa no vā.

---

‘I’, ‘ī’ and ‘u’, ‘ū’ \[are named\] ‘jha’, ‘la’.

The letters ‘i’ and ‘ī’, and ‘u’ and ‘ū’, are respectively named ‘jha’ and ‘la’. 

The examples are “isino”, etc. When forming the word “isino”, first the base is put down as “isi”, then the sixth inflection ‘sa’ is employed; then the second ‘i’ of “isi” is given the name ‘jha’ by this sutta. The same should be done with the nouns whose bases end in ‘ī’, ‘u’, ‘ū’.

What is the use of the names ‘jha’ and ‘la’? \[For the use of the names ‘jha’ and ‘la’ in suttas such as\] “Jha-lato sassa no vā” (§117). Optionally, after ‘jha’ and ‘la’, ‘sa’ \[is substituted by ‘no’\].

---

**59\. 182\. Te itthikhyā po (206).** \[Te \+ itthikhyā \+ po, 3\]

Te **ivaṇṇ’-uvaṇṇā** yadā itthikhyā, tadā **pa**saññā honti. 

Rattiyā, itthiyā, dhenuyā, vadhuyā. 

**Itthikhyā** ti kimatthaṃ? Isinā, bhikkhunā. 

**Pa** icc anena kvattho? Pato yā.

---

Those that mean feminine \[gender\] \[are named\] ‘pa’.

Those letters ‘i’, ‘ī’ and ‘u’, ‘ū’, when they mean feminine \[gender\], are named ‘pa’. 

Examples: **rattiyā** (by night), **itthiyā** (by the woman), **dhenuyā** (by the cow), **vadhuyā** (by the daughter-in-law).[^9]

What is the purpose of saying “mean feminine \[gender\]”? \[To prevent the operation of this rule in examples such as\] “isinā” (by the sage), “bhikkhunā” (by the bhikkhu) – \[these are not named ‘pa’ since they are masculine\].

What is the use of the name ‘pa’? \[For the use of the name ‘pa’ in suttas such as\] “Pato yā” (§112). After ‘pa’, \[‘nā’, etc., are substituted by\] ‘ya’.

---

**60\. 177\. Ā gho (207).** \[Ā \+ gho, 2\]

**Ā**kāro yadā itthikhyo, tadā **gha**sañño hoti. 

Saddhāya, kaññāya, vīṇāya, gaṅgāya, disāya, sālāya, mālāya, tulāya, dolāya, pabhāya, sobhāya, paññāya, karuṇāya, nāvāya, kapālikāya. 

**Ā** ti kimatthaṃ? Rattiyā, itthiyā. 

**Itthikhyo** ti kimatthaṃ? Satthārā desito ayaṃ dhammo. 

**Gha** icc anena kvattho? Ghato nādīnaṃ.

---

‘Ā’ \[is named\] ‘gha’.

The letter ‘ā’, when it means feminine \[gender\], is named ‘gha’. 

Examples: **saddhāya** (by/with confidence), **kaññāya** (by/with the girl), **vīṇāya** (by/with the lute), **gaṅgāya** (by/with the river Ganges), **disāya** (by/with the direction), **sālāya** (by/with the hall), **mālāya** (by/with the garland), **tulāya** (by/with the scale), **dolāya** (by the cradle), **pabhāya** (by/with the light), **sobhāya** (by/with the beauty), **paññāya** (by/with wisdom), **karuṇāya** (by/with compassion), **nāvāya** (by/with the boat), **kapālikāya** (by/with the small bowl).

When forming “saddhāya” (by/with confidence), etc., the base is put down as “saddhā”, etc. The inflection ‘nā’ is employed; then the ‘ā’ of “saddhā”, etc., is named ‘gha’, and the application of the other suttas follows.

What is the purpose of saying ‘ā’? \[There is no application of this sutta in\] “rattiyā”, “itthiyā” \[because in these cases the letter is ‘i’ or ‘ī’, not ‘ā’\].

What is the purpose of saying “means feminine \[gender\]”? \[In the example\] “Satthārā desito ayaṃ dhammo” (this Dhamma taught by the Teacher) \[there is no application of this sutta because the word is masculine\].

What is the use of the name ‘gha’? \[For the use of the name ‘gha’ in suttas such as\] “Ghato nādīnaṃ” (§111). After ‘ga’, \[there is substitution by ‘āya’\] of those \[singular inflections\] beginning with ‘nā’, etc.

---

**61\. 86\. Sāgamo se (208).** \[Sa-āgamo \+ se, 2\]

**Sa**kārāgamo hoti **se** vibhattimhi.

Purisassa, aggissa, isissa, daṇḍissa, bhikkhussa, sayambhussa, abhibhussa. 

**Se** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisasmiṃ.

---

When ‘sa’ \[follows\], \[there is\] insertion of ‘s’.

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, there is insertion of ‘s’. 

Example: **purisassa** \= purisa \+ sa. By this sutta ‘s’ is inserted after the base. The rest are to be understood similarly.

What is the purpose of saying ‘se’? \[There is no application of this sutta in the example\] “purisasmiṃ” (in the man), \[since the inflection is not ‘sa’ but ‘smiṃ’\].

---

**62\. 206\. Saṃ-sāsv ekavacanesu ca (209).** \[Saṃ-sāsu \+ ekavacanesu \+ ca, 3\]

**Saṃ-sā**su ekavacanesu vibhattādesesu **sa**kārāgamo hoti. 

Etissaṃ, etissā; imissaṃ, imissā; tissaṃ, tissā; tassaṃ, tassā; yassaṃ, yassā; amussaṃ, amussā. 

**Saṃ-sāsv** iti kimatthaṃ? Agginā, pāṇinā. 

**Ekavacanesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Tāsaṃ, sabbāsaṃ. 

**Vibhattādesesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Manasā, vacasā, thāmasā.

---

And when the singular ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’ \[follow\].

When ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’, which are substitutions of the singular inflections \[‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’\], follow, there is insertion of ‘s’.[^10]

---

Examples:

1. **Etissaṃ** \= etā \+ smiṃ (‘ā’ of “etā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§63); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ (§179) and ‘s’ is inserted (§62)). Meaning: “in that”. \[Feminine gender.\]  
2. **Etissā** \= etā \+ sa (‘ā’ of “etā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§63); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘sā’ (§179) and ‘s’ is inserted (§62)). Meaning: “of that”. \[Feminine gender.\]  
3. The rest (**imissaṃ**, **imissā**; **tissaṃ**, **tissā**; **tassaṃ**, **tassā**; **yassaṃ**, **yassā**; **amussaṃ**, **amussā**) are to be understood similarly.

What is the purpose of saying “‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘saṃ’ or ‘sā’, such as in\] “agginā” (with fire) and “pāṇinā” (with the hand).

What is the purpose of saying “singular”? \[This sutta does not apply when the words are not singular, such as in\] **tāsaṃ** (of those) and **sabbāsaṃ** (of all).

What is the purpose of saying “which are substitutions of inflections”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no substitutions of inflections, such as in\] **manasā** (with the mind), **vacasā** (with speech) and **thāmasā** (with strength).

---

**63\. 217\. Et’-imāsam i (210).** \[Etā-imāsaṃ \+ i, 2\]

**Etā-imā** icc etesam anto saro **i**kāro hoti **saṃ-sā**su ekavacanesu vibhattādesesu. 

Etissaṃ, etissā; imissaṃ, imissā. 

**Saṃ-sāsv** iti kimatthaṃ? Etāya, imāya. 

**Ekavacanesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Etāsaṃ, imāsaṃ.

---

‘I’ of “etā” and “imā”.

When ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’, which are substitutions of the singular inflections \[‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’\], follow, the end vowel ‘ā’ of “etā” and “imā” is substituted by ‘i’. 

Examples: the same as the previous sutta.

What is the purpose of saying “‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘saṃ’ or ‘sā’, such as in\] **etāya** and **imāya**.

What is the purpose of saying “singular”? \[This sutta does not apply when the words are not singular, such as in\] **etāsaṃ** and **imāsaṃ**.

---

**64\. 216\. Tassā vā (211).** \[Tassā \+ vā, 2\]

Tassā itthiyaṃ vattamānāya ākārassa ikāro hoti vā saṃ-sāsu ekavacanesu vibhattādesesu. 

Tissaṃ, tissā; tassaṃ, tassā.

---

Optionally, \[‘ā’\] of “tā”.

Optionally, when ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’, which are substitutions of the singular inflections \[‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’\], follow, the \[end vowel\] ‘ā’ of “tā”, in the feminine \[gender\], is substituted by ‘i’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tissaṃ** \= tā \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘ā’ of “tā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§64) and ‘s’ is inserted (§62)).  
2. **Tissā** \= tā \+ sa (‘sa’ is substituted by ‘sā’ (§179); ‘ā’ of “tā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§64) and ‘s’ is inserted (§62)).

---

**65\. 215\. Tato sassa ssāya (212).** \[Tato \+ sassa \+ ssāya, 3\]

Tato **tā-etā-imā**to **sa**ssa vibhattissa **ssāyā**deso hoti vā. 

Tissāya, etissāya, imissāya. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Tissā, etissā, imissā.

---

After ‘tā’, ‘ssāya’ of ‘sa’.

Optionally, after “tā”, “etā” and “imā”, the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘ssāya’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tissāya** \= tā \+ sa (‘ā’ of “tā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§64); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘ssāya’ (§65)).  
2. **Etissāya** \= etā \+ sa (‘ā’ of “etā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§63); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘ssāya’ (§65)).  
3. **Imissāya** \= imā \+ sa (‘ā’ of “imā” is substituted by ‘i’ (§63); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘ssāya’ (§65)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[In the following examples ‘sa’ is not substituted by ‘ssāya’:\] tissā, etissā, imissā.

---

**66\. 205\. Gho rassaṃ (213).** \[Gho \+ rassaṃ, 2\]

**Gho** rassam āpajjate **saṃ-sā**su ekavacanesu vibhattādesesu. 

Tassaṃ, tassā; yassaṃ, yassā; sabbassaṃ, sabbassā. 

**Saṃ-sāsv** iti kimatthaṃ? Tāya, sabbāya. 

**Ekavacanesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Tāsaṃ, sabbāsaṃ.

---

‘Gha’ \[becomes short\].

When ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’, which are substitutions of the singular inflections \[‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’\], follow, ‘gha’ becomes short.

---

Examples:

1. **Tassaṃ** \= tā \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘ā’ of “tā” is shortened (§66)).  
2. **Tassā** \= tā \+ sa (‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘ā’ of “tā” is shortened (§66))  
3. **Yassaṃ** \= yā \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘ā’ of “yā” is shortened (§66)).  
4. **Yassā** \= yā \+ sa (‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘ā’ of “yā” is shortened (§66))   
5. **Sabbassaṃ** \= sabbā \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘ā’ of “sabbā” is shortened (§66)).  
6. **Sabbassā** \= sabbā \+ sa (‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘ā’ of “sabbā” is shortened (§66))

What is the purpose of saying “‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘saṃ’ or ‘sā’, such as in\] tāya, sabbāya.

What is the purpose of saying “singular”? \[This sutta does not apply when the words are not singular, such as in\] tāsaṃ, sabbāsaṃ.

---

**67\. 229\. No ca dvādito naṃmhi (214).** \[No \+ ca \+ dvi-ādito \+ naṃmhi, 4\]

**Dvi** icc evamādito saṅkhyāto **na**kārāgamo hoti **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Dvinnaṃ, tinnaṃ, catunnaṃ, pañcannaṃ, channaṃ, sattannaṃ, aṭṭhannaṃ, navannaṃ, dasannaṃ. 

**Dvādito** ti kimatthaṃ? Sahassānaṃ. 

**Naṃmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Dvīsu, tīsu. 

**Caggahaṇena ssa**ñ c’ āgamo hoti. Catassannaṃ itthīnaṃ; tissannaṃ vedanānaṃ.

---

And when ‘naṃ’ \[follows\], after “dvi” and others, \[there is insertion of\] ‘n’.

After the numerals “dvi” (two) and others, the letter ‘n’ is inserted when the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows.

---

Examples:

1. **Dvinnaṃ** (of two) \= dvi \+ naṃ (by this sutta ‘n’ is inserted).  
2. **Tinnaṃ** (of three), **catunnaṃ** (of four), **pañcannaṃ** (of five), **channaṃ** (of six), **sattannaṃ** (of seven), **aṭṭhannaṃ** (of eight), **navannaṃ** (of nine), **dasannaṃ** (of ten). The formation of these numerals is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “‘dvi’ and others”? \[This sutta does not apply to higher numbers such as\] sahassānaṃ (of thousands).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘naṃ’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘naṃ’ does not follow, such as in\] dvīsu (in two), tīsu (in three).

And by taking “ca”, ‘ssa’ is also inserted. 

Examples:

1. **Catassannaṃ** \= catu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “catu” is substituted by ‘a’ (§404); by “ca” of this sutta ‘ssa’ is inserted). Catassannaṃ itthīnaṃ (of four women).  
2. **Tissannaṃ** \= ti \+ naṃ (by “ca” of this sutta ‘ssa’ is inserted). Tissannaṃ vedanānaṃ (of three feelings).

---

**68\. 184\. Am-ā pato smiṃ-smānaṃ vā (216).** \[Aṃ-ā \+ pato \+ smiṃ-smānaṃ \+ vā, 4\]

**Pa** icc etasmā **smiṃ smā** icc etesaṃ **aṃ-ā** ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Matyaṃ, matiyaṃ, matyā (Khu. vi, 39), matiyā. Nikatyaṃ, nikatiyaṃ (Khu. vi, 39), nikatyā (Khu. v, 9), nikatiyā. Vikatyaṃ, vikatiyaṃ, vikatyā, vikatiyā. Viratyaṃ, viratiyaṃ, viratyā, viratiyā. Ratyaṃ, ratiyaṃ, ratyā (Khu. vi, 315), ratiyā. Puthabyaṃ, puthaviyaṃ, puthabyā, puthaviyā. Pavatyaṃ, pavatyā, pavattiyaṃ, pavattiyā.

---

Optionally, after ‘pa’, ‘aṃ’ and ‘ā’ of ‘smiṃ’ and ‘smā’.

Optionally, after ‘pa’, ‘smiṃ’ and ‘smā’ are substituted by ‘aṃ’ and ‘ā’ respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Matyaṃ** \= mati \+ smiṃ (‘i’ of “mati” is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§68); ‘i’ of “mati” is substituted by ‘y’ (§72)).  
2. **Matyā** \= mati \+ smā (‘i’ of “mati” is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§68); ‘i’ of “mati” is substituted by ‘y’ (§72)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous ones.

---

**69\. 186\. Ādito o ca (218).** \[Ādito \+ o \+ ca, 3\]

Ādi icc etasmā smiṃ-vacanassa aṃ-o ādesā honti vā. 

Ādiṃ, ādo. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Ādismiṃ, ādimhi nāthaṃ namassitvāna. 

Caggahaṇena aññasmā pi smiṃvacanassa ā-o-aṃ ādesā honti. Divā ca ratto ca haranti ye baliṃ (Khu. i, 312). Bārāṇasiṃ ahu rājā (Khu. v, 380).

---

And ‘o’ after “ādi”.

Optionally, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ after “ādi” is substituted by ‘aṃ’ and ‘o’.

---

Examples:

1. **Ādiṃ** \= ādi \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§69); ‘i’ of “ādi” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Ādo** \= ādi \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§69); ‘i’ of “ādi” is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not apply in:\] ādismiṃ, ādimhi nāthaṃ namassitvāna (having paid homage to the Lord, in the beginning).

And by taking “ca”, also after other words ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ā’, ‘o’ and ‘aṃ’. 

Examples:

1. **Divā** \= divā \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§69); ‘ā’ of “divā” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Ratto** \= ratti \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§69); ‘i’ of “ratti” is elided).  
3. **Bārāṇasiṃ** \= Bārāṇasī \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§69); ‘a’ of ‘aṃ’ is elided).

“Divā ca ratto ca haranti ye baliṃ” (those who by day and by night bring offerings). “Bārāṇasiṃ ahu rājā” (there was a king in Vārāṇasī).

---

**70\. 30\. Jha-lānam iy’-uvā sare vā (220).** \[Jha-lānaṃ \+ iya-uvā \+ sare \+ vā, 4\]

Jha la icc etesaṃ iya uva icc ete ādesā honti vā sare pare yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Tiyantaṃ pacchiyāgāre, aggiyāgāre, bhikkhuvāsane nisīdati, puthuvāsane nisīdati. 

**Sare** ti kimatthaṃ? Timalaṃ, tiphalaṃ, ticatukkaṃ, tidaṇḍaṃ, tilokaṃ, tinayanaṃ, tipāsaṃ, tihaṃsaṃ, tibhavaṃ, tikhandhaṃ, tipiṭakaṃ, tivedanaṃ, catuddisaṃ, puthubhūtaṃ. 

Vā ti kimatthaṃ? Pañcah’ aṅgehi (Vin. v, 343), tīhākārehi. Cakkh’ āyatanaṃ (Abhi. iii, 5). 

Vā ti vikappanatthaṃ? Ikārassa ayādeso hoti. Vatthuttayaṃ.

---

When a vowel follows, optionally, \[there are\] ‘iya’ and ‘uva’ of ‘jha’ and ‘la’.

Optionally, when a vowel follows, ‘jha’ and ‘la’ are substituted by ‘iya’ and ‘uva’ respectively. i ī — iya u ū — uva 

---

Examples:

1. **Tiyantaṃ** \= ti \+ antaṃ (‘i’ of ‘ti’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘i’ is substituted by ‘iya’ (§70); ‘a’ of ‘iya’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Bhikkhuvāsane** \= bhikkhu \+ āsane (‘u’ of “bhikkhu” is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘u’ is substituted by ‘uva’ (§70); ‘a’ of ‘uva’ is elided (§83)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous ones.

What is the purpose of saying “when a vowel follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when a vowel does not follow, such as in\] timalaṃ, tiphalaṃ, ticatukkaṃ, tidaṇḍaṃ, tilokaṃ, tinayanaṃ, tipāsaṃ, tihaṃsaṃ, tibhavaṃ, tikhandhaṃ, tipiṭakaṃ, tivedanaṃ, catuddisaṃ, puthubhūtaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not apply in\] pañcah’ aṅgehi, tīhākārehi, cakkh’ āyatanaṃ.

“Vā” is used in the meaning of alternative (*vikappana*). \[By that “vā”\] the letter ‘i’ is substituted by ‘aya’. **Vatthuttayaṃ** \= vatthutti \+ si (by “vā” of this sutta, ‘i’ of ‘ti’ is substituted by ‘aya’; ‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’).

---

**71\. 505\. Ya-vakārā ca (221).** \[Ya-vakārā \+ ca, 2\]

**Jha-lā**naṃ **ya**kāra-**vā**kārādesā honti sare pare yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Agyāgāraṃ (Vin. iii, 33); cakkh’ āyatanaṃ (Abhi. iii, 5); svāgataṃ (Khu. v, 300); te mahāvīra. 

**Caggahaṇaṃ** sampiṇḍanatthaṃ.

---

And the letters ‘ya’ and ‘va’.

When a vowel follows, ‘jha’ and ‘la’ are substituted by ‘ya’ and ‘va’ respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Agyāgāraṃ** \= aggi \+ āgāraṃ (‘i’ of “aggi” is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘i’ is substituted by ‘ya’ (§71); ‘a’ of ‘ya’ is elided (§83); ‘g’ of “aggi” is also elided (§41)).  
2. **Svāgataṃ** \= su \+ āgataṃ (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘u’ is substituted by ‘va’ (§71); ‘a’ of ‘va’ is elided (§83)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous ones.

“Ca” is used in the meaning of collecting (*sampiṇḍana*).[^11]

---

**72\. 185\. Pasaññassa ca (222).** \[Pa-saññassa \+ ca, 2\]

**Pa**saññassa ca **i**vaṇṇassa vibhattādese sare pare **ya**kārādeso hoti. 

Puthabyā; ratyā (Khu. vi, 315); matyā (Khu. vi, 39). 

**Sare** ti kimatthaṃ? Puthaviyaṃ.

---

And of those named ‘pa’.

And when the vowel \[‘ā’\] which is the substitution of the inflection \[‘smā’\] follows, ‘i’ and ‘ī’, named ‘pa’, are substituted by ‘ya’.

---

Examples:

1. **Puthabyā** \= puthavī \+ smā (‘ī’ of “puthavī” is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§68); ‘ī’ is substituted by ‘ya’ (§72); ‘v’ is substituted by ‘b’ (§20)).  
2. The formation of **ratyā** and **matyā** is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “when a vowel follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when a vowel does not follow, such as in\] puthaviyaṃ.

---

**73\. 174\. Gāva se (224).** \[Go \+ āva \+ se, 3\]

**Go** icc etassa **o**kārassa **āvā**deso hoti **se** vibhattimhi. 

Gāvassa.

---

When ‘sa’ \[follows\], ‘āva’ of \[‘o’ of\] “go”.

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’.

---

Example:

1. **Gāvassa** \= go \+ sa (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ (§73) and ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).

---

**74\. 169\. Yosu ca (224).** \[Yosu \+ ca, 2\]

Go icc etassa okārassa āvādeso hoti yo icc etesu paresu. 

Gāvo gacchanti; gāvo passanti; gāvī gacchanti; gāvī passanti. 

**Caggahaṇaṃ** kimatthaṃ? **Nā-smā-smiṃ-su**vacanesu **āvā**deso hoti. 

Gāvena, gāvā, gāve, gāvesu.

---

And when \[any of the three\][^12] ‘yo’ follow.

When \[any of the three\] ‘yo’ follow, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’.

---

Examples:

1. **Gāvo** \= go \+ yo (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ (§74); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (“tu” in §205); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of taking “ca”? \[By taking “ca”, there is the substitution ‘āva’\] also when ‘nā’, ‘smā’, ‘smiṃ’ and ‘su’ follow.

Examples:

1. **Gāvena** \= go \+ nā (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ by “ca” of this sutta (§74); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ena’ (§103); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
2. **Gāvā** \= go \+ smā (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ by “ca” of this sutta (§74); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§108); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
3. **Gāve** \= go \+ smiṃ (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ by “ca” of this sutta (§74); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§108); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
4. **Gāvesu** \= go \+ su (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ by “ca” of this sutta (§74); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).

---

**75\. 170\. Av’ aṃmhi ca (224).** \[Ava \+ aṃ-mhi \+ ca, 3\]

Go icc etassa okārassa āva ava icc ete ādesā honti aṃ-mhi vibhattimhi. 

**Caggahaṇena sā**disesesu pubbuttavacanesu **go** icc etassa **o**kārassa **avā**deso hoti. 

Gāvaṃ, gavaṃ. Gavassa, gavo, gavena, gavā, gave, gavesu.

---

And when ‘aṃ’ follows, ‘ava’.

When the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ and ‘ava’.

---

Examples:

1. **Gāvaṃ** \= go \+ aṃ (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ (§75); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
2. **Gavaṃ** \= go \+ aṃ (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).

By taking “ca”, when the remaining inflections beginning with ‘sa’ mentioned in the previous \[two\] suttas follow, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’.

Examples:

1. **Gavassa** \= go \+ sa (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).  
2. **Gavo** \= go \+ yo (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (“tu” in §205); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
3. **Gavena** \= go \+ nā (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ena’ (§103); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
4. **Gavā** \= go \+ smā (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§108); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
5. **Gave** \= go \+ smiṃ (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§108); ‘a’ is elided (§12)).  
6. **Gavesu** \= go \+ su (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§75); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).

---

**76\. 171\. Āvass’ u vā (226).** \[Āvassa \+ u \+ vā, 3\]

**Āva** icc etassa **gāvā**desassa antasarassa **u**kārādeso hoti vā **aṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Gāvuṃ, gāvaṃ. 

**Āvasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Gavaṃ. 

**Aṃmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Gāvo tiṭṭhanti.

---

Optionally, ‘u’ of ‘āva’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows, the end vowel of ‘āva’ –which is the substitution of “go”– is substituted by ‘u’.

---

Examples:

1. **Gāvuṃ** \= go \+ aṃ (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘āva’ (§73); ‘a’ of ‘āva’ is substituted by ‘u’ (§76); ‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘ṃ’ (§82)).  
2. **Gāvaṃ** \= no application of this rule.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘āva’”? \[This sutta does not apply in\] gavaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘aṃ’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘aṃ’ does not follow, such as in\] gāvo tiṭṭhanti.

---

**77\. 175\. Tato naṃ aṃ patimh’ ālutte ca samāse (227-8).** \[Tato \+ naṃ \+ aṃ \+ patimhi \+ ālutte \+ ca \+ samāse, 7\]

Tato gosaddato naṃvacanassa aṃādeso hoti, go icc etassa okārassa avādeso hoti **pati**mhi pare ālutte ca samāse. 

Gavaṃpati (S. iii, 382). 

**Alutte** ti kimatthaṃ? Gopati. 

**Caggahaṇena** asamāse pi **naṃ**vacanassa **aṃ**ādeso hoti, **go** icc etassa **o**kārassa **avā**deso hoti. 

Gavaṃ.

---

After that \[“go”\], when ‘pati’ follows in a compound with \[the inflection\] not elided, ‘aṃ’ of ‘naṃ’.

When there is a compound with \[the inflection\] not elided (*alutta-samāsa*), and when ‘pati’ follows, after the word “go” the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ and ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’.

---

Example:

1. **Gavaṃpati** \= gonaṃ \+ pati (‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§77); ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§77); ‘a’ of ‘ava’ is elided (§12)).

What is the purpose of saying “in a compound with \[the inflection\] not elided”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is a compound with elision of the inflection, such as in\] gopati.

And by taking “ca”, also when there is no compound, ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ and ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’.

Example:

1. **Gavaṃ** \= go \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§77); ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§77)).

---

**78\. 31\. O sare ca (229).** \[O \+ sare \+ ca, 3\]

**Go** icc etassa **o**kārassa **avā**deso hoti samāse ca sare pare. 

Gavassakaṃ, gaveḷakaṃ, gavājinaṃ. 

**Caggahaṇena u**vaṇṇa icc evam antānaṃ liṅgānaṃ **uva-ava-urā**desā honti **smiṃ-yo** icc etesu kvaci. 

Bhuvi, pasavo, guravo, caturo (Khu. i, 293). 

**Sare** ti kimatthaṃ? Godhano, govindho (D. ii, 186).

---

And when a vowel follows, ‘o’.

When there is a compound and a vowel follows, ‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’.

---

Example:

1. **Gavassakaṃ** \= go \+ assakaṃ (‘o’ of “go” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§78); ‘a’ of ‘ava’ is elided (§12)).

And by taking “ca”, when ‘smiṃ’ and ‘yo’ follow, sometimes the bases ending in ‘u’ and ‘ū’ are substituted by ‘uva’, ‘ava’ and ‘ura’.

Examples:

1. **Bhuvi** \= bhū \+ smiṃ (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘ū’ of “bhū” is substituted by ‘uva’ (§78); by “tato” of §206 ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’; ‘a’ of ‘uva’ is elided (§12)).  
2. **Pasavo** \= pasu \+ yo (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘u’ of “pasu” is substituted by ‘ava’ (§78); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (“tu” in §205); ‘a’ of ‘ava’ is elided (§12)).  
3. **Caturo** \= catu \+ yo (by “ca” of this sutta, ‘u’ is substituted by ‘ura’ (§78); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (“tu” in §205); ‘a’ of ‘ura’ is elided (§12)).

What is the purpose of saying “when a vowel follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when a vowel does not follow, such as in\] godhano, govindho.

---

**79\. 46\. Tabbiparītūpapade byañjane ca.** \[Tabbiparīto \+ upapade \+ byañjane \+ ca, 4\]

Tassa **ava**saddassa yadā upapade tiṭṭhamānassa tassa **o**kārassa viparīto hoti byañjane pare. 

Uggate sūriye; uggacchati; uggahetvā. 

**Caggahaṇam** avadhāraṇatthaṃ. Avasāne, avakiraṇe, avakirati.

---

And when \[‘ava’\] is the preceding member (*upapada*) and a consonant follows, \[there is\] reversion of that.

When the word ‘ava’ is the preceding member (*upapada*) and a consonant follows, ‘o’ of ‘ava’ is reversed \[into ‘u’\].[^13]

---

Example:

1. **Uggate** \= ava \+ gate (‘ava’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§50); then ‘o’ becomes ‘u’ (§79); ‘g’ of “gate” is doubled (§20)).[^14]

“Ca” is for \[delimiting the application of\] this rule, \[which does not apply in\] avasāne, avakiraṇe, avakirati.

---

**80\. 173\. Goṇa naṃmhi vā (231).** \[Goṇa \+ naṃmhi \+ vā, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **go**saddassa **goṇā**deso hoti vā **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Goṇānaṃ sattannaṃ. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Gonañ ce taramānānaṃ, ujuṃ gacchati puṅgavo. Sabbā gāvī ujuṃ yanti, nette ujuṃ gate sati. 

**Yogavibhāgena** aññatrā pi **goṇā**deso hoti. 

Goṇabhūtānaṃ.

---

Optionally, when ‘naṃ’ follows, “goṇa”.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows, the whole word “go” is substituted by “goṇa”.

---

Example:

1. **Goṇānaṃ** \= go \+ naṃ (“go” is substituted by “goṇa” (§80); ‘a’ of “goṇa” is lengthened (§89)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not apply in:\] “Gonañ ce taramānānaṃ, ujuṃ gacchati puṅgavo. Sabbā gāvī ujuṃ yanti, nette ujuṃ gate sati”. (When the leader of the crossing cattle goes straight, all the cows go straight when the leader goes straight.)

By dividing the sutta, in other contexts there is also the substitution by “goṇa”: goṇabhūtānaṃ.

---

**81\. 172\. Su-hi-nāsu ca (231-2).** \[Su-hi-nāsu \+ ca, 2\]

**Su hi nā** icc etesu sabbassa **go**saddassa **goṇā**deso hoti vā. 

Goṇesu, goṇehi, goṇebhi, goṇena. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Gosu, gohi, gobhi, gavena. 

**Caggahaṇena syā**disesesu pubbuttaravacanesu pi **goṇa-gu-gavayā**desā honti. Goṇo, goṇā, goṇaṃ, goṇe, goṇassa, goṇamhā. Goṇamhi, gunnaṃ, gavayehi, gavayebhi.

---

And when ‘su’, ‘hi’ and ‘nā’ follow.

Optionally, when ‘su’, ‘hi’ and ‘nā’ follow, the whole word “go” is substituted by “goṇa”.

---

Example:

1. **Goṇesu** \= go \+ su (“go” is substituted by “goṇa” (§81); ‘a’ of “goṇa” is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not apply in\] gosu, gohi, gobhi, gavena.

And by taking “ca”, when the remaining inflections, ‘si’ and others, mentioned before and after, follow, there are also the substitutions by “goṇa”, “gu” and “gavaya”.

Examples:

1. **Goṇo** \= go \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); “go” is substituted by “goṇa” (§81); ‘a’ of “goṇa” is elided (§12)).  
2. **Gunnaṃ** \= go \+ naṃ (“go” is substituted by “gu” (§81); ‘n’ is inserted (§28)).  
3. **Gavayehi** \= go \+ hi (“go” is substituted by “gavaya” (§81); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§108)).

---

**82\. 149\. Aṃ-mo niggahitaṃ jha-la-pehi (235).** \[Aṃ-mo \+ niggahitaṃ \+ jha-la-pehi, 3\]

**Aṃ**vacanassa, **ma**kārassa ca **jha la pa** icc etehi niggahitaṃ hoti. 

Aggiṃ, isiṃ, gahapatiṃ, daṇḍiṃ, mahesiṃ, bhikkhuṃ, paṭuṃ, sayambhuṃ, abhibhuṃ, rattiṃ, itthiṃ, vadhuṃ, pulliṅgaṃ, pumbhāvo, puṅkokilo. 

**Aṃ-mo** ti kimatthaṃ? Agginā, pāṇinā, bhikkhunā, rattiyā, itthiyā, vadhuyā. 

**Jha-la-pehī** ti kimatthaṃ? Sukhaṃ, dukkhaṃ. 

**Pun’ ārambhaggahaṇaṃ** vibhāsanivattanatthaṃ. Aggiṃ, paṭuṃ, buddhiṃ, vadhuṃ.

---

After ‘jha’, ‘la’ and ‘pa’, niggahita \[of\] ‘aṃ’ and ‘ma’.

After ‘jha’, ‘la’ and ‘pa’, ‘aṃ’ and ‘ma’ become niggahita (ṃ).

---

Examples:

1. **Aggiṃ** \= aggi \+ aṃ (‘i’ of “aggi” is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘ṃ’ (§82)).  
2. **Rattiṃ** \= ratti \+ aṃ (‘i’ of “ratti” is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘ṃ’ (§82)).  
3. **Pulliṅgaṃ** \= puma \+ liṅgaṃ (‘ma’ of “puma” becomes ‘ṃ’ (§82); ‘ṃ’ becomes ‘l’ (§31); ‘l’ is doubled (§28)).

What is the purpose of saying “‘aṃ’ and ‘ma’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘aṃ’ or ‘ma’, such as in\] agginā, pāṇinā, bhikkhunā, rattiyā, itthiyā, vadhuyā.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘jha’, ‘la’ and ‘pa’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘jha’, ‘la’ or ‘pa’, such as in\] sukhaṃ, dukkhaṃ.

Taking again “jha-la-pehi” is to prevent “vā” from following up to here.[^15] Examples: aggiṃ, paṭuṃ, buddhiṃ, vadhuṃ.

---

**83\. 67\. Saralopo ’mādesa-paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati (236).** \[Sara-lopo \+ aṃ-ādesa-paccaya-ādimhi \+ saralope \+ tu \+ pakati, 5\]

Saralopo hoti **amādesa-paccayādi**mhi, saralope tu pakati hoti. 

Purisaṃ, purise, pāpaṃ, pāpe, pāpiyo, pāpiṭṭho. 

**Amādesapaccayādimhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Appamādo amataṃ padaṃ (Khu. i, 16). 

**Saralope** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisassa, daṇḍinaṃ. 

**Tuggahaṇam** avadhāraṇatthaṃ? Bhikkhunī, gahapatānī. 

**Pakatiggahaṇasāmatthena** puna sandhibhāvo ca hoti. Seyyo (Khu. i, 29); seṭṭho (Vin. i, 
4); jeyyo; jeṭṭho (D. ii, 13).

---

When ‘aṃ’, a substitution, a suffix, etc., follow, \[there is\] elision of the vowel; when there is elision of the vowel, \[the remaining vowel remains\] natural.

When \[the inflection\] ‘aṃ’, a substitution, a suffix, etc., follow, there is elision of the vowel; and when there is elision of the vowel, \[the remaining vowel\] remains natural.[^16][^17]

---

Examples:

1. **Purisaṃ** \= purisa \+ aṃ (because of ‘aṃ’, there is elision of the vowel ‘a’ of “purisa” (§83); ‘a’ of ‘aṃ’ does not change (§83)).  
2. **Pāpiyo** \= pāpa \+ iya (‘a’ of “pāpa” is elided (§83); ‘i’ of ‘iya’ does not change (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘aṃ’, a substitution, a suffix, etc., \[follow\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when that does not happen, such as in\] “Appamādo amataṃ padaṃ”.[^18]

What is the purpose of saying “when there is elision of the vowel”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no vowel to elide, such as in\] purisassa, daṇḍinaṃ.

‘Tu’ is for preventing the operation of this rule in “bhikkhunī”, “gahapatānī”.

Examples:

1. **Bhikkhunī** \= bhikkhu \+ inī (here ‘i’ of ‘inī’ is elided (§13)).  
2. **Gahapatānī** \= gahapati \+ inī (‘i’ of ‘pati’ is substituted by ‘a’ (§91); ‘i’ of ‘inī’ is elided (§13); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§16)).

Taking again “pakati”, there is also sandhi: seyyo, seṭṭho, jeyyo, jeṭṭho.[^19]

---

**84\. 144\. Agho rassam ekavacanayosv api ca (237-8).** \[Agho \+ rassaṃ \+ ekavacana-yosu \+ api \+ ca, 5\]

**Agho** saro rassam āpajjate **ekavacana**-**yo** icc etesu. 

Itthiṃ, itthiyo, itthiyā. Vadhuṃ, vadhuyo, vadhuyā. Daṇḍiṃ, daṇḍino, daṇḍinā. Sayambhuṃ, sayambhuvo, sayambhunā. 

**Agho** ti kimatthaṃ? Kaññaṃ, kaññāyo, kaññāya. 

**Ekavacanayosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Itthīhi, sayambhūhi. 

Caggahaṇam avadhāraṇatthaṃ? Nadiṃ, nadīyo, nadiyā. 

**Apiggahaṇena** na rassam āpajjate. Itthī, bhikkhunī.

---

And also when the singular \[number\] and ‘yo’ follow, non-‘gha’ \[is shortened\].

When the singular \[inflection\] and ‘yo’ follow, the vowel that is not ‘gha’ is shortened.

---

Examples:

1. **Itthiṃ** \= itthī \+ aṃ (‘ī’ of “itthī” is shortened (§84); ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘ṃ’ (§82)).  
2. **Itthiyo** \= itthī \+ yo (‘ī’ of “itthī” is shortened (§84)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous ones.

What is the purpose of saying “non-‘gha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is ‘gha’, such as in\] kaññaṃ, kaññāyo, kaññāya.

What is the purpose of saying “when the singular \[number\] and ‘yo’ \[follow\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when that is not the case, such as in\] itthīhi, sayambhūhi.

“Ca” is for emphasis.

By taking “api”, there is no shortening. 

Examples:

1. **Itthī** \= itthī \+ si (‘ī’ is not shortened because of “api” in this sutta (§84); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

---

**85\. 150\. Na sismim anapuṃsakāni (239-48).** \[Na \+ sismim \+ anapuṃsakāni, 3\]

**Si**smiṃ anapuṃsakāni liṅgāni na rassam āpajjante. 

Itthī, bhikkhunī, vadhū, daṇḍī, sayambhū. 

**Sismiṃ** ti kimatthaṃ? Bhoti itthi, bothi vadhu, bho daṇḍi, bho sayambhu. 

**Anapuṃsakānī** ti kimatthaṃ? Sukhakāri dānaṃ, sukhakāri sīlaṃ, sīghayāyi cittaṃ.

---

When ‘si’ follows, the non-neuters, not \[shortened\].

When ‘si’ follows, the non-neuter bases are not shortened.

---

Examples:

1. **Itthī** \= itthī \+ si (‘ī’ is not shortened; ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ \[follows\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in\] “bhoti itthi”, etc.[^20]

Example:

1. **Itthi** \= itthī \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘gha’ (§57); ‘ī’ is shortened (§245); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

What is the purpose of saying “non-neuters”? \[This sutta does not apply when they are neuter, such as in\] “sukhakāri dānaṃ”, etc.

Examples:

1. **Sukhakāri** \= sukhakārī \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened by this sutta (§85); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

---

**86\. 227\. Ubhādito nam innaṃ (341).** \[Ubha-ādito \+ naṃ \+ innaṃ, 3\]

**Ubha** icc evam ādito saṅkhyāto **naṃ**vacanassa **innaṃ** hoti. 

Ubhinnaṃ, duvinnaṃ. 

**Ubhādito** ti kimatthaṃ? Ubhayesaṃ.

---

After “ubha” and others, ‘innaṃ’ \[of\] ‘naṃ’.

After the numerals “ubha” and others, the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘innaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Ubhinnaṃ** \= ubha \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘innaṃ’ (§86); ‘a’ of “ubha” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Duvinnaṃ** \= dvi \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘innaṃ’ (§86); by “ca” (§132) “dvi” is substituted by “duvi”; ‘i’ of “duvi” is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘ubha’ and others”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “ubha” and others, such as in\] ubhayesaṃ.[^21]

---

**87\. 231\. Iṇṇam-iṇṇannaṃ tīhi saṅkhyāhi (243).** \[Iṇṇaṃ-iṇṇannaṃ \+ tīhi \+ saṅkhyāhi, 3\]

**Naṃ**vacanassa **iṇṇaṃ iṇṇannaṃ** icc ete ādesā honti tīhi saṅkhyāhi. 

Tiṇṇaṃ, tiṇṇannaṃ. 

**Tīhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Dvinnaṃ.

---

After the numeral “ti”, ‘iṇṇaṃ’ and ‘iṇṇannaṃ’ \[of ‘naṃ’\].

After the numeral “ti”, the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘iṇṇaṃ’ and ‘iṇṇannaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tiṇṇaṃ**, **tiṇṇannaṃ** \= ti \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘iṇṇaṃ’ and ‘iṇṇannaṃ’ (§87); ‘i’ of “ti” is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “after the numeral ‘ti’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘ti’, such as in\] dvinnaṃ.

---

**88\. 147\. Yosu katanikāra-lopesu dīghaṃ (245).** \[Yosu \+ kata-nikāra-lopesu \+ dīghaṃ, 3\]

Sabbe sarā **yo**su katanikāra-lopesu dīgham āpajjante. 

Aggī, bhikkhū, rattī, yāgū, aṭṭhī, aṭṭhīni, āyū, āyūni, sabbāni, yāni, tāni, kāni, katamāni, etāni, amūni, imāni. 

**Yosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Aggi, bhikkhu, ratti, yāgu, sabbo, yo, so, ko, amuko. 

**Katanikāralopesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Itthiyo, vadhuyo, sayambhuvo. 

**Pun’ ārambhaggahaṇaṃ** kimatthaṃ? Niccadīpanatthaṃ. Aggī, bhikkhū, rattī, yāni, tāni, katamāni.

---

When ‘yo’ becomes ‘ni’ or is elided, long.

When ‘yo’ becomes ‘ni’ or is elided, all the vowels are lengthened.

---

Examples:

1. **Aggī** \= aggi \+ yo (‘yo’ is elided (§118); ‘i’ is lengthened (§88)).  
2. **Āyūni** \= āyu \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§217); ‘u’ is lengthened (§88)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘yo’ \[follows\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘yo’, such as in\] aggi, bhikkhu, ratti, yāgu, sabbo, yo, so, ko, amuko.

What is the purpose of saying “when it becomes ‘ni’ or is elided”? \[This sutta does not apply when it neither becomes ‘ni’ nor is elided, such as in\] itthiyo, vadhuyo, sayambhuvo.

What is the purpose of taking this sutta again? To show that it is a fixed operation. Examples: aggī, bhikkhū, rattī, yāni, tāni, katamāni.

---

**89\. 87\. Su-naṃ-hisu ca (246).** \[Su-naṃ-hisu \+ ca, 2\]

**Su naṃ hi** icc etesu sabbe sarā dīgham āpajjante. 

Aggīsu, aggīnaṃ, aggīhi; rattīsu, rattīnaṃ, rattīhi; bhikkhūsu, bhikkhūnaṃ, bhikkhūhi. Purisānaṃ. 

**Etesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Agginā, pāṇinā, daṇḍinā. 

**Caggahaṇam** avadhāraṇatthaṃ. Sukhettesu brahmacārisu (A. i, 352); dhammam akkhāsi Bhagavā; bhikkhunaṃ datvā sakehi pāṇibhi (Khu. ii, 138).

---

And when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow.

When ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow, all the vowels are lengthened.

---

Examples:

1. **Aggīsu** \= aggi \+ su (‘i’ is lengthened (§89)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “when these follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ do not follow, such as in\] agginā, pāṇinā, daṇḍinā.

“Ca” is for preventing \[the application of this rule in the following cases\].

Examples: 

1. **Brahmacārisu** \= brahmacāri \+ su (here ‘i’ is not lengthened, by “ca” (§89)).   
2. **Bhikkhunaṃ** \= bhikkhu \+ naṃ (here ‘u’ is not lengthened).  
3. **Pāṇibhi** \= pāṇi \+ hi (‘hi’ is substituted by ‘bhi’ (§99); ‘i’ is not lengthened, by “ca” (§89)).

---

**90\. 252\. Pañcādīnam attaṃ (247).** \[Pañca-ādīnaṃ \+ attaṃ, 2\]

**Pañcā**dīnaṃ saṅkhyānaṃ anto **a**ttam āpajjate **su naṃ hi** icc etesu. 

Pañcasu, pañcannaṃ, pañcahi; chasu, channaṃ, chahi; sattasu, sattannaṃ, sattahi; aṭṭhasu, aṭṭhannaṃ, aṭṭhahi; navasu, navannaṃ, navahi; dasasu, dasannaṃ, dasahi. 

**Pañcādīnamī** ti kimatthaṃ? Dvīsu, dvinnaṃ, dvīhi. 

**Attam iti** bhāvaniddeso ubhayassāgamanatthaṃ, anto **u**kāro **a**ttam āpajjate. Catassannaṃ itthīnaṃ. Tissannaṃ vedanānaṃ.

---

The state of ‘a’ of “pañca” and others.

When ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow, the end vowel of the numerals “pañca” and others remains ‘a’.

---

Example:

1. **Pañcasu** \= pañca \+ su (‘a’ remains ‘a’ (§90)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘pañca’ and others”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “pañca” and others, such as in\] dvīsu, dvinnaṃ, dvīhi.

“Attaṃ”, which is an expression that indicates a state (*bhāvaniddesa*), has the purpose of inserting ‘ssa’ and changing the end ‘u’ into ‘a’. 

Example:

1. **Catassannaṃ** \= catu \+ naṃ (‘ssa’ is inserted; ‘u’ becomes ‘a’ (§90)).  
2. **Tissannaṃ** \= ti \+ naṃ (‘ssa’ is inserted (§90); ‘n’ is doubled (§28)).[^22]

---

**91\. 194\. Patiss’ inīmhi (248).** \[Patissa \+ inīmhi, 2\]

**Pati**ss’ anto **a**ttam āpajjate **inī**mhi paccaye pare. 

Gahapatānī. 

**Inīmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Gahapati.

---

‘Inī’ of “pati”.

When the suffix ‘inī’ follows, the end vowel of “pati” becomes ‘a’.

---

Example:

1. **Gahapatānī** \= gahapati \+ inī (‘i’ of “gahapati” becomes ‘a’ (§91); ‘i’ of ‘inī’ is elided (§13); ‘a’ is lengthened (§16)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘inī’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘inī’ does not follow, such as in\] gahapati.

---

**92\. 100\. Ntuss’ anto yosu ca (249).** \[Ntussa \+ anto \+ yosu \+ ca, 4\]

**Ntu**paccayassa anto **a**ttam āpajjate **su naṃ hi yo** icc etesu paresu. 

Guṇavantesu, guṇavantānaṃ, guṇavantehi, guṇavantā, guṇavante. 

**Ntusse** ti kimatthaṃ? Isīnaṃ. 

Etesv iti kimatthaṃ? Guṇavā. 

**Caggahaṇena** aññesu vacanesu **a**ttañ ca hoti. Guṇavantasmiṃ, guṇavantena. 

**Antaggahaṇena ntu**paccayassa anto **a**ttam āpajjate, **yo**nañ ca **i**kāro hoti. Guṇavanti.

---

And when ‘yo’ \[and others\] follow, the end of ‘ntu’.

When ‘su’, ‘naṃ’, ‘hi’ and ‘yo’ follow, the end vowel of the suffix ‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Guṇavantesu** \= guṇavantu \+ su (‘u’ of ‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’ (§92); ‘a’ becomes ‘e’ (§89)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘ntu’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘ntu’, such as in\] isīnaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “when these follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’, ‘hi’ and ‘yo’ do not follow, such as in\] guṇavā.

By taking “ca”, when other inflections follow, there is also ‘a’. Examples: guṇavantasmiṃ, guṇavantena.

By taking “anta”, the end vowel of the suffix ‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’, and ‘yo’ becomes ‘i’. Example:

1. **Guṇavanti** \= guṇavantu \+ yo (‘u’ of ‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’ by “anta” (§92); ‘yo’ becomes ‘i’ (§92); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**93\. 106\. Sabbassa vā aṃ-sesu (251).** \[Sabbassa \+ vā \+ aṃ-sesu, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa **a**ttaṃ hoti vā **aṃ sa** icc etesu. 

Satimaṃ bhikkhuṃ, satimantaṃ bhikkhuṃ vā. Bandhumaṃ rājānaṃ, bandhumantaṃ rājānaṃ vā (D. ii, 14). Satimassa bhikkhuno, satimato bhikkhuno vā. Bandhumassa rañño (D. ii, 6) suṅkaṃ. Bandhumato rañño (D. ii, 13) vā suṅkaṃ deti.

**Etesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Satimā bhikkhu. Bandhumā rājā.

---

Optionally, when ‘aṃ’ and ‘sa’ follow, \[there is ‘a’\] of the whole \[‘ntu’\].

Optionally, when ‘aṃ’ and ‘sa’ follow, there is ‘a’ of the whole suffix ‘ntu’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satimaṃ** \= satimantu \+ aṃ (‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’ (§93); ‘a’ is elided (§83); ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘ṃ’ (§82)).  
2. **Satimassa** \= satimantu \+ sa (‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’ (§93); ‘a’ is elided (§83); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).

What is the purpose of saying “when these follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘aṃ’ and ‘sa’ do not follow, such as in\] satimā bhikkhu, bandhumā rājā.

---

**94\. 105\. Simhi vā (252).** \[Simhi \+ vā, 2\]

**Ntu**paccayassa antassa **a**ttaṃ hoti vā **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Himavanto pabbato (Khu. i, 56). 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Himavā pabbato.

---

Optionally, when the inflection ‘si’ follows.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘si’ follows, the end vowel of the suffix ‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’.

---

Example:

1. **Himavanto** \= himavantu \+ si (‘u’ of ‘ntu’ becomes ‘a’ (§94); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] himavā pabbato.

---

**95\. 145\. Aggiss’ ini (254).** \[Aggissa \+ ini, 2\]

**Aggi**ss’ antassa **ini** hoti vā **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Purato aggini. Pacchato aggini. Akkhiṇato aggini. Vāmato aggini. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Aggi.

---

‘Ini’ of \[the end vowel of\] “aggi”.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘si’ follows, there is ‘ini’ of the end vowel of “aggi”.

---

Examples:

1. **Aggini** \= aggi \+ si (‘i’ of “aggi” becomes ‘ini’ (§95); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] aggi.

---

**96\. 148\. Yosv akatarasso jho (259).** \[Yosu \+ akatarasso \+ jho, 3\]

**Yo**su akatarasso **jho a**ttam āpajjate. 

Aggayo, munayo, isayo (Khu. ii, 88), gahapatayo (Khu. vi, 423). 

**Yosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Aggīsu. 

**Akatarasso** ti kimatthaṃ? Daṇḍino. 

**Jho** ti kimatthaṃ? Rattiyo.

---

When ‘yo’ follows, ‘jha’ not shortened \[becomes ‘a’\].

When ‘yo’ follows, ‘jha’, which has not been shortened,[^23] becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Aggayo** \= aggi \+ yo (‘i’ is named ‘jha’ (§58) and it has not been shortened; ‘i’ becomes ‘a’ (§96)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘yo’ \[follows\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ does not follow, such as in\] aggīsu.

What is the purpose of saying “not shortened”? \[This sutta does not apply when it has been shortened, such as in\] daṇḍino. 

Example: 

**Daṇḍino** \= daṇḍī \+ yo (‘ī’ of “daṇḍī” is shortened (§84); therefore this sutta does not operate).

What is the purpose of saying “‘jha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘jha’, such as in\] rattiyo.

---

**97\. 156\. Ve-vosu lo ca (260).** \[Ve-vosu \+ lo \+ ca, 3\]

**Ve-vo** icc etesu akatarasso **lo a**ttam āpajjate. 

Bhikkhave, bhikkhavo; hetave, hetavo. 

**Akatarasso** ti kimatthaṃ? Sayambhuvo, vessabhuvo, parābhibhuvo. 

**Ve-vosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Hetunā, ketunā, setunā. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And when ‘ve’ and ‘vo’ follow, ‘la’ \[not shortened\].

When ‘ve’ and ‘vo’ follow, ‘la’, which has not been shortened, becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Bhikkhave** \= bhikkhu \+ yo (‘u’ of “bhikkhu” is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ve’ (§116); ‘u’, which has not been shortened, becomes ‘a’ (§97)).  
2. **Bhikkhavo** \= bhikkhu \+ yo (‘u’ of “bhikkhu” is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘vo’ (§119); ‘u’, which has not been shortened, becomes ‘a’ (§97)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “not shortened”? \[This sutta does not apply when it has been shortened, such as in\] sayambhuvo, vessabhuvo, parābhibhuvo.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘ve’ and ‘vo’ follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘ve’ and ‘vo’ do not follow, such as in\] hetunā, ketunā, setunā.

“Ca” is for dragging (*anukaḍḍhana*).

---

**98\. 189\. Mātulādīnam ānattam īkāre (261).** \[Mātulādīnaṃ \+ ānattaṃ \+ īkāre, 3\]

**Mātula** icc evam ādīnaṃ anto **āna**ttam āpajjate **ī**kāre paccaye pare. 

Mātulānī (Khu. i, 219), ayyakānī, varuṇānī. 

**Īkāre** ti kimatthaṃ? Bhikkhunī, rājinī, jālinī, gahapatānī (Vin. i, 314). 

Ānattaggahaṇena nadī icc etassa dīsaddassa jjo-jjā-ādesā honti saha vibhattiyā yo nā sa icc etesu. Najjo sandanti (S. ii, 178); najjā kataṃ taraṅgaṃ; najjā nerañjarāya tīre (Vin. 
iii, 1).

---

When ‘ī’ follows, ‘āna’ of “mātula”, etc.

When the suffix ‘ī’ follows, the end vowel of “mātula”, etc., becomes ‘āna’.

---

Examples:

1. **Mātulānī** \= mātula \+ ī (‘a’ of “mātula” becomes ‘āna’ (§98); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘ī’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘ī’ does not follow, such as in\] bhikkhunī, rājinī, jālinī, gahapatānī.

By taking “ānatta”, when ‘yo’, ‘nā’ and ‘sa’ follow, ‘dī’ of “nadī” is substituted by ‘jjo’ and ‘jjā’ together with the inflections.

Example:

1. **Najjo** \= nadī \+ yo (‘dī’ together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘jjo’ (§98)).

---

**99\. 81\. Smā-hi-smiṃnaṃ mhā-bhi-mhi vā (265-6).** \[Smā-hi-smiṃnaṃ \+ mhā-bhi-mhi \+ vā, 3\]

Sabbato liṅgato **smā hi smiṃ** icc etesaṃ **mhā bhi mhi** icc ete ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Purisamhā, purisasmā; purisebhi, purisehi; purisamhi, purisasmiṃ. 

**Smā-hi-smiṃnam** iti kimatthaṃ? Vaṇṇavantaṃ (Khu. i, 20) agandhakaṃ viruḷhapupphaṃ; mahantaṃ chattaṃ mahāchattaṃ; mahantaṃ dhajaṃ mahādhajaṃ.

---

Optionally, ‘mhā’, ‘bhi’ and ‘mhi’ of ‘smā’, ‘hi’ and ‘smiṃ’.

Optionally, after all the bases, ‘smā’, ‘hi’ and ‘smiṃ’ are substituted by ‘mhā’, ‘bhi’ and ‘mhi’ respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Purisamhā** \= purisa \+ smā (‘smā’ is substituted by ‘mhā’ (§99)).  
2. **Purisebhi** \= purisa \+ hi (‘hi’ is substituted by ‘bhi’ (§99); ‘a’ of “purisa” becomes ‘e’ (§101)).  
3. **Purisamhi** \= purisa \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘mhi’ (§99)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘smā’, ‘hi’ and ‘smiṃ’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘smā’, ‘hi’ or ‘smiṃ’, such as in\] vaṇṇavantaṃ agandhakaṃ viruḷhapupphaṃ, mahantaṃ chattaṃ mahāchattaṃ, mahantaṃ dhajaṃ mahādhajaṃ.

---

**100\. 214\. Na t’-imehi katākārehi (267).** \[Na \+ ta-imehi \+ kata-ākārehi, 3\]

**Ta ima** icc etehi katākārehi **smā-smiṃ**naṃ **mhā-mhi** icc ete ādesā n’ eva honti. 

Asmā ṭhāna bhayaṃ uppajjati; asmiṃ ṭhāne bhayaṃ tiṭṭhati; asmā; asmiṃ. 

**Katākārehī** ti kimatthaṃ? Tamhā, tamhi, imamhā, imamhi.

---

When “ta” and “ima” become ‘a’, no \[substitution\].

When “ta” and “ima” become ‘a’, ‘smā’ and ‘smiṃ’ are not substituted by ‘mhā’ and ‘mhi’.

---

Examples:

1. **Asmā** \= ta \+ smā (‘ta’ becomes ‘a’ (§176); ‘smā’ does not become ‘mhā’ (§100)).  
2. **Asmiṃ** \= ta \+ smiṃ (‘ta’ becomes ‘a’ (§176); ‘smiṃ’ does not become ‘mhi’ (§100)).[^24]

Examples:

1. **Asmā** \= ima \+ smā (“ima” becomes ‘a’ (§177); ‘smā’ does not become ‘mhā’ (§100)).  
2. **Asmiṃ** \= ima \+ smiṃ (“ima” becomes ‘a’ (§177); ‘smiṃ’ does not become ‘mhi’ (§100)).

What is the purpose of saying “become ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘ta’ and ‘ima’ do not become ‘a’, such as in\] tamhā, tamhi, imamhā, imamhi.

---

**101\. 80\. Su-hisv akāro e (268).** \[Su-hisu \+ akāro \+ e, 3\]

Su hi icc etesu akāro ettam āpajjate. 

Sabbesu, yesu, tesu, kesu, purisesu, imesu, kusalesu, tumhesu, amhesu; sabbehi, yehi, tehi, kehi, purisehi, imehi, kusalehi, tumhehi, amhehi.

---

When ‘su’ and ‘hi’ follow, ‘a’ \[becomes\] ‘e’.

When ‘su’ and ‘hi’ follow, ‘a’ becomes ‘e’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbesu** \= sabba \+ su (‘a’ becomes ‘e’ (§101)).  
2. **Sabbehi** \= sabba \+ hi (‘a’ becomes ‘e’ (§101)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

---

**102\. 202\. Sabbanāmānaṃ naṃmhi ca (270).** \[Sabbanāmānaṃ \+ naṃmhi \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ sabbanāmānaṃ anto **a**kāro **e**ttam āpajjate **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Sabbesaṃ, sabbesānaṃ; yesaṃ, yesānaṃ; tesaṃ, tesānaṃ; imesaṃ, imesānaṃ; kesaṃ, kesānaṃ; itaresaṃ, itaresānaṃ; katamesaṃ, katamesānaṃ. 

**Sabbanāmānam** iti kimatthaṃ? Buddhānaṃ Bhagavantānaṃ āciṇṇasamāciṇṇo (Vin. i, 
114). 

**Akāro** ti kimatthaṃ? Amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ. 

**Naṃmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Sabbe, ime. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And when ‘naṃ’ follows, of all the pronouns.

When the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows, the letter ‘a’, which is the end vowel of all the pronouns, becomes ‘e’.

---

Example:

1. **Sabbesaṃ** \= sabba \+ naṃ (‘a’ becomes ‘e’ (§102); ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ (§168)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “of \[all the\] pronouns”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no pronouns, such as in\] “Buddhānaṃ Bhagavantānaṃ āciṇṇasamāciṇṇo”.

What is the purpose of saying “the letter ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in\] amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘naṃ’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘naṃ’ does not follow, such as in\] sabbe, ime. 

Example: 

**Sabbe** \= sabba \+ yo (here ‘a’ does not become ‘e’; rather, ‘yo’ becomes ‘e’ by §164).

“Ca” is for dragging.[^25]

---

**103\. 79\. Ato n’ ena (271).** \[Ato \+ nā \+ ena, 3\]

Tasmā **a**kārato **nā**vacanassa **enā**deso hoti. 

Sabbena, yena, tena, kena, anena, purisena, rūpena. 

**Ato** ti kimatthaṃ? Muninā, amunā, bhikkhunā. 

**Nā** ti kimatthaṃ? Tasmā.

---

After ‘a’, ‘ena’ \[of\] ‘nā’.

After the letter ‘a’, the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ena’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbena** \= sabba \+ nā (‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ena’ (§103); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in\] muninā, amunā, bhikkhunā.

What is the purpose of saying ‘nā’? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘nā’, such as in\] tasmā.

---

**104\. 66\. S’ o (272).** \[Si \+ o, 2\]

Tasmā **a**kārato **si**vacanassa **o**kārādeso hoti. 

Sabbo, yo, so, ko, amuko, puriso. 

**Sī** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisānaṃ. 

**Ato** ti kimatthaṃ? Sayambhū.

---

‘Si’ \[becomes\] ‘o’.

After the letter ‘a’, the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbo** \= sabba \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying ‘si’? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘si’, such as in\] purisānaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in\] sayambhū.

---

**105\. 0\. So vā (273).** \[So \+ vā, 2\]

Tasmā **a**kārato **nā**vacanassa **so-**ādeso hoti vā. 

Atthaso dhammaṃ jānāti. Byañjanaso atthaṃ jānāti. Akkharaso. Suttaso (A. ii, 207). Padaso (Vin. ii, 25). Yasaso. Upāyaso. Sabbaso (A. i, 556). Thāmaso. Ṭhānaso. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Pādena vā pādārahena vā atirekapādena vā yo bhikkhu theyyacittena parassa bhaṇḍaṃ gaṇhāti, so bhikkhu pārājiko hoti asaṃvāso.

---

Optionally, ‘so’.

Optionally, after the letter ‘a’, the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘so’.

---

Example:

1. **Atthaso** \= attha \+ nā (‘nā’ is substituted by ‘so’ (§105)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] “Pādena vā pādārahena vā atirekapādena vā yo bhikkhu theyyacittena parassa bhaṇḍaṃ gaṇhāti, so bhikkhu pārājiko hoti asaṃvāso”. Translation: whatever bhikkhu, with the intention of stealing, takes the property of another –whether a quarter of a *pāda* \[coin\], or the equivalent of a quarter of a *pāda*, or more than a quarter of a *pāda*– that bhikkhu is one who has committed an offence entailing expulsion and can no longer live in communion.

---

**106\. 313\. Dīgh’-orehi (274).** \[Dīgha-orehi, 1\]

**Dīgha ora** icc etehi **smā**vacanassa **so-**ādeso hoti vā. 

Dīghaso (Vin. i, 227), oraso; dīghamhā, oramhā. 

**Dīgh’-ore**hi ti kimatthaṃ? Saramhā, vacanamhā.

---

After “dīgha” and “ora”.

Optionally, after “dīgha” and “ora”, the inflection ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘so’.

---

Example:

1. **Dīghaso** \= dīgha \+ smā (‘smā’ is substituted by ‘so’ (§106)).  
2. **Oraso** \= ora \+ smā (‘smā’ is substituted by ‘so’ (§106)).

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘dīgha’ and ‘ora’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “dīgha” or “ora”, such as in\] saramhā, vacanamhā.

---

**107\. 69\. Sabbayo-nīnam ā e (275, 277).** \[Sabba-yo-nīnaṃ \+ ā \+ e, 3\]

Tasmā **a**kārato sabbesaṃ **yo-nī**naṃ **ā-e-**ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Purisā, purise; rūpā, rūpe. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Aggayo, munayo, isayo. 

**Yo-nī**nan ti kimatthaṃ? Purisassa, rūpassa. 

**Akārato** ti kimatthaṃ? Daṇḍino, aṭṭhīni, aggī, pajjalanti, munī caranti.

---

‘Ā’ and ‘e’ of the whole ‘yo’ and ‘ni’.

Optionally, after the letter ‘a’, the whole ‘yo’ and ‘ni’ are substituted by ‘ā’ and ‘e’ respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Purisā** \= purisa \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§107); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Purise** \= purisa \+ ni (‘ni’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§107); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] aggayo, munayo, isayo.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘yo’ and ‘ni’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘yo’ or ‘ni’, such as in\] purisassa, rūpassa.

What is the purpose of saying “after the letter ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in\] daṇḍino, aṭṭhīni, aggī, pajjalanti, munī caranti.

---

**108\. 90\. Smā-smiṃnaṃ vā (276).** \[Smā-smiṃnaṃ \+ vā, 2\]

Tasmā **a**kārato sabbesaṃ **smā smiṃ** icc etesaṃ **ā-e-**ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Purisā, purisasmā, purise, purisasmiṃ. 

**Akārato** ti kimatthaṃ? Daṇḍinā, daṇḍismiṃ; bhikkhunā, bhikkhusmiṃ.

---

Optionally, \[there are ‘ā’ and ‘e’\] of ‘smā’ and ‘smiṃ’.

Optionally, after the letter ‘a’, ‘smā’ and ‘smiṃ’ are substituted by ‘ā’ and ‘e’ respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Purisā** \= purisa \+ smā (‘smā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§108); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Purise** \= purisa \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§108); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “after the letter ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in\] daṇḍinā, daṇḍismiṃ; bhikkhunā, bhikkhusmiṃ.

---

**109\. 304\. Āya catutthekavacanassa tu (279-80).** \[Āya \+ catuttha-ekavacanassa \+ tu, 3\]

Tasmā akārato catutthekavacanassa āyādeso hoti vā. 

Atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ Buddho loke uppajjati (A. i, 21). 

**Ato** ti kimatthaṃ? Isissa. 

**Catutthī** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisassa mukhaṃ. 

**Ekavacanasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisānaṃ dadāti. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Dātā hoti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā. 

**Tuggahaṇen’ atthañ** ca hoti. Atthatthaṃ, hitatthaṃ, sukhatthaṃ.

---

‘Āya’ of the fourth singular.

Optionally, after the letter ‘a’, the fourth singular \[inflection\] is substituted by ‘āya’.

---

Example:

1. **Atthāya** \= attha \+ sa (‘sa’ is substituted by ‘āya’ (§109); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in\] isissa.

What is the purpose of saying “the fourth \[inflection\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no fourth inflection, such as in\] purisassa mukhaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “of the singular”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no singular, such as in\] purisānaṃ dadāti.

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] “Dātā hoti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā”.

By taking ‘tu’, there is also substitution by ‘atthaṃ’. 

Example:

1. **Atthatthaṃ**[^26] \= attha \+ sa (by ‘tu’ of this sutta, ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘atthaṃ’ (§109); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Hitatthaṃ**[^27] \= hita \+ sa (by ‘tu’ of this sutta, ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘atthaṃ’ (§109); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Sukhatthaṃ**[^28] \= sukha \+ sa (by ‘tu’ of this sutta, ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘atthaṃ’ (§109); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**110\. 201\. Tayo n’ eva ca sabbanāmehi (281).** \[Tayo \+ na \+ eva \+ ca \+ sabbanāmehi, 5\]

Tehi sabbanāmehi akārantehi smā smiṃ sa icc etesaṃ[^29] tayo ā-e-āyādesā n’ eva honti. 

Sabbasmā, sabbasmiṃ, sabbassa. Yasmā, yasmiṃ, yassa. Tasmā, tasmiṃ, tassa. Kasmā, kasmiṃ, kassa. Imasmā, imasmiṃ, imassa. 

**Sabbanāmehi** ti kimatthaṃ? Pāpā, pāpe, pāpāya. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And after the pronouns, the three are not \[substituted\].

After the pronouns ending in ‘a’, ‘smā’, ‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’ are not substituted by the following three: ‘ā’, ‘e’ and ‘āya’.

---

Example:

1. **Sabbasmā** \= sabba \+ smā (‘smā’ is not substituted by ‘ā’ (§110)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “after pronouns”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no pronouns, such as in\] pāpā, pāpe, pāpāya.

“Ca” is for dragging.

---

**111\. 179\. Ghato nādīnaṃ (283).** \[Ghato \+ nādīnaṃ, 2\]

Tasmā **gha**to **nā**dīnam ekavacanānaṃ vibhattigaṇānaṃ **āyā**deso hoti. 

Kaññāya kataṃ kammaṃ, kaññāya dīyate, kaññāya nissaṭaṃ vatthaṃ. Kaññāya pariggaho, kaññāya patiṭṭhitaṃ sīlaṃ. 

**Ghato** ti kimatthaṃ? Rattiyā, itthiyā, dhenuyā, vadhuyā. 

**Nādīnam** iti kimatthaṃ? Kaññaṃ passati; vijjaṃ, vīṇaṃ, gaṅgaṃ. 

**Ekavacanānam** iti kimatthaṃ? Sabbāsu, yāsu, tāsu, kāsu, imāsu, pabhāsu.

---

After ‘gha’, \[there is ‘āya’\] of ‘nā’ and others.

After ‘gha’, the group of singular inflections beginning with ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘āya’.

---

Example:

1. **Kaññāya** \= kaññā \+ nā (‘ā’ of “kaññā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘āya’ (§111); ‘ā’ is elided (§83)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘gha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘gha’, such as in\] rattiyā, itthiyā, dhenuyā, vadhuyā.

What is the purpose of saying “‘nā’, etc.”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘nā’, etc., such as in\] kaññaṃ passati; vijjaṃ, vīṇaṃ, gaṅgaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “singular”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no singular, such as in\] sabbāsu, yāsu, tāsu, kāsu, imāsu, pabhāsu.

---

**112\. 183\. Pato yā (284).** \[Pato \+ yā, 2\]

Tasmā pato nādīnam ekavacanānaṃ vibhattigaṇānaṃ yādeso hoti. 

Rattiyā, itthiyā, deviyā, dhenuyā, yāguyā, vadhuyā. 

**Nādīnam** iti kimatthaṃ? Rattī, rattiṃ; itthī, itthiṃ. 

**Pato** ti kimatthaṃ? Kaññāya, vīṇāya, gaṅgāya, pabhāya, sobhāya. 

**Ekavacanānam** iti kimatthaṃ? Rattīnaṃ, itthīnaṃ.

---

After ‘pa’, ‘yā’.

After ‘pa’, the group of singular inflections beginning with ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘yā’.

---

Example:

1. **Rattiyā** \= ratti \+ nā (‘i’ of “ratti” is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘yā’ (§112)).  
2. **Dhenuyā** \= dhenu \+ nā (‘u’ of “dhenu” is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘yā’ (§112)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “of those beginning with ‘nā’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘nā’, etc., such as in\] rattī, rattiṃ; itthī, itthiṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘pa’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘pa’, such as in\] kaññāya, vīṇāya, gaṅgāya, pabhāya, sobhāya.

What is the purpose of saying “singular”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no singular, such as in\] rattīnaṃ, itthīnaṃ.

---

**113\. 132\. Sakhato gass’ e vā (285-6).** \[Sakhato \+ gassa \+ e \+ vā, 4\]

Tasmā **sakha**to **ga**ssa **a**kāra-**ā**kāra-**i**kāra-**ī**kāra-**e**kārādesā honti vā. 

Bho sakha, bho sakhā, bho sakhi, bho sakhī, bho sakhe.

---

Optionally, ‘e’ of ‘ga’ after “sakha”.

Optionally, after “sakha”, there are the substitutions of ‘ga’ by ‘a’, ‘ā’, ‘i’, ‘ī’ and ‘e’.

---

Example:

1. **Sakha** \= sakha \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ga’ is substituted by ‘a’ (§113); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

---

**114\. 178\. Ghat’ e ca (288).** \[Ghato \+ e \+ ca, 3\]

Tasmā **gha**to **ga**ssa **e**kārādeso hoti. 

Bhoti ayye, bhoti kaññe, bhoti Kharādiye (Khu. v, 4). 

**Caggahaṇam** avadhāraṇatthaṃ. Sanniṭṭhānaṃ.

---

And ‘e’ after ‘gha’.

After ‘gha’, ‘ga’ is substituted by ‘e’.

---

Example:

1. **Ayye** \= ayyā \+ si (‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ga’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§114); ‘ā’ is elided (§83)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

“Ca” is for delimiting (*avadhāraṇa*). \[Here the meaning is\] fixity (*sanniṭṭhāna*).

---

**115\. 181\. Na ammādito (290).** \[Na \+ ammādito, 2\]

Tato **ammā**dito **ga**ssa **e**kārattaṃ na hoti. 

Bhoti ammā, bhoti annā, bhoti ambā, bhoti tātā. 

**Ammādito** ti kimatthaṃ? Bhoti kaññe.

---

After “ammā” and others, no \[change\].

After “ammā”, etc., ‘ga’ is not substituted by ‘e’.

---

Example:

1. **Ammā** \= ammā \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57) and is elided (§220); here ‘ga’ is not substituted by ‘e’ because there is “ammā” (§115)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘ammā’, etc.”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “ammā”, etc., such as in\] bhoti kaññe.

---

**116\. 197\. Akatarassā lato yv-ālapanassa ve-vo (291).** \[Akatarassa \+ lato \+ yo-ālapanassa \+ ve-vo, 4\]

Tasmā akatarassā **la**to **yvā**lapanassa **ve-vo-**ādesā honti. 

Bhikkhave, bhikkhavo; hetave, hetavo. 

**Akatarassā** ti kimatthaṃ? Sayambhuvo. 

**Lato** ti kimatthaṃ? Nāgiyo, dhenuyo, yāguyo. 

**Ālapanasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Te hetavo, te bhikkhavo.

---

After ‘la’ not shortened, ‘ve’ and ‘vo’ of the vocative ‘yo’.

After the letter ‘la’, which has not been shortened, the vocative ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ve’ and ‘vo’.

---

Example:

1. **Bhikkhave** \= bhikkhu \+ yo (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ve’ (§116); ‘u’, which has not been shortened, becomes ‘a’ (§97)).  
2. **Bhikkhavo** \= bhikkhu \+ yo (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘vo’ (§116); ‘u’, which has not been shortened, becomes ‘a’ (§97)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “not shortened”? \[This sutta does not apply when it has been shortened, such as in\] sayambhuvo. 

Example: 

1. **Sayambhuvo** \= sayambhū \+ yo (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘ū’ is shortened (§84); since ‘ū’ has been shortened, this sutta does not operate; ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘vo’ (§119)).

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘la’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘la’, such as in\] nāgiyo, dhenuyo, yāguyo.

What is the purpose of saying “of the vocative”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no vocative, such as in\] te hetavo, te bhikkhavo.

---

**117\. 124\. Jha-lato sassa no vā (292).** \[Jha-lato \+ sassa \+ no \+ vā, 4\]

Tasmā **jha-la**to **sa**ssa vibhattissa **no**-ādeso hoti vā. 

Aggino, aggissa; sakhino, sakhissa; daṇḍino, daṇḍissa; bhikkhuno, bhikkhussa; sayambhuno, sayambhussa. 

**Sasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Isinā, bhikkhunā. 

**Jha-lato** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisassa.

---

Optionally, after ‘jha’ and ‘la’, ‘no’ \[of\] ‘sa’.

Optionally, after ‘jha’ and ‘la’, the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’.

---

Examples:

1. **Aggino** \= aggi \+ sa (‘i’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).  
2. **Bhikkhuno** \= bhikkhu \+ sa (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘sa’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘sa’, such as in\] isinā, bhikkhunā.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘jha’ and ‘la’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘jha’ or ‘la’, such as in\] purisassa.

---

**118\. 146\. Gha-pato ca yonaṃ lopo (293).** \[Gha-pato \+ ca \+ yonaṃ \+ lopo, 4\]

Tehi **gha pa jha la** icc etehi **yo**naṃ lopo hoti vā. 

Kaññā, kaññāyo; rattī, rattiyo; itthī, itthiyo; yāgū, yāguyo; vadhū, vadhuyo. Aggī, aggayo; bhikkhū, bhikkhavo; sayambhū, sayambhuvo; aṭṭhī, aṭṭhīni; āyū, āyūni. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And after ‘gha’ and ‘pa’, \[there is\] elision of ‘yo’.

Optionally, after ‘gha’, ‘pa’, ‘jha’ and ‘la’, there is elision of ‘yo’.

---

Example:

1. **Kaññā** \= kaññā \+ yo (‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘yo’ is elided (§118)).  
2. **Rattī** \= ratti \+ yo (‘i’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘yo’ is elided (§118); ‘i’ is lengthened (§88)).  
3. **Itthī** \= itthī \+ yo (‘ī’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘yo’ is elided (§118)).  
4. **Yāgū** \= yāgu \+ yo (‘u’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘yo’ is elided (§118); ‘u’ is lengthened (§88)).  
5. **Vadhū** \= vadhū \+ yo (‘ū’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘yo’ is elided (§118)).

“Ca” is for dragging.

---

**119\. 155\. Lato vokāro ca (294).** \[Lato \+ vo-kāro \+ ca, 3\]

Tasmā **la**to **yo**naṃ **vo**kāro hoti vā. 

Bhikkhavo, bhikkhū; sayambhuvo, sayambhū. 

**Kāraggahaṇaṃ** kimatthaṃ? **Yo**naṃ **no** ca hoti. Jantuno. 

**Caggahaṇam** avadhāraṇatthaṃ. Amū purisā tiṭṭhanti. Amū purise passatha.

---

And after ‘la’, the letter ‘vo’.

Optionally, after ‘la’, ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘vo’.

---

Examples:

1. **Bhikkhavo** \= bhikkhu \+ yo (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘u’ becomes ‘a’ (§97); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘vo’ (§119)).

What is the purpose of taking “kāra” \[in the sutta\]? ‘Yo’ is also substituted by ‘no’. 

Example: 

1. **Jantuno** \= jantu \+ sa (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).

“Ca” is for preventing the operation in the following cases: Amū purisā tiṭṭhanti. Amū purise passatha. 

Examples: 

1. **Amū** \= amu \+ yo (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); by “ca”, ‘yo’ is not substituted by ‘no’ (§119); ‘yo’ is elided (§118); ‘u’ is lengthened (§88)).

---

**Iti nāma-kappe paṭhamo kaṇḍo**

**Thus ends the first section of the noun chapter**

**DUTIYA-KAṆḌA**

**SECOND SECTION**

**120\. 243\. Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se (295).** \[Amhassa \+ mamaṃ \+ sa-vibhattissa \+ se, 4\]

Sabbass’ eva **amha**saddassa savibhattissa **mamaṃ**-ādeso hoti **se** vibhattimhi. 

Mamaṃ dīyate purisena. Mamaṃ pariggaho.

---

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, “mamaṃ” \[of\] “amha”.

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mamaṃ”.

---

Example:

1. **Mamaṃ** \= amha \+ sa (“amha” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “mamaṃ” (§120)).

---

**121\. 233\. Mayaṃ yomhi paṭhame (296).** \[Mayaṃ \+ yomhi \+ paṭhame, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **amha**saddassa savibhattissa **mayaṃ**-ādeso hoti **yo**mhi paṭhame. 

Mayaṃ gacchāma. Mayaṃ dema. 

**Amhasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisā tiṭṭhanti. 

**Yomhi** kimatthaṃ? Ahaṃ gacchāmi. 

**Paṭhame** ti kimatthaṃ? Amhākaṃ passasi tvaṃ.

---

When the first ‘yo’ follows, “mayaṃ”.

When the first \[inflection\] ‘yo’ follows, the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mayaṃ”.

---

Example:

1. **Mayaṃ** \= amha \+ yo (“amha” together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “mayaṃ” (§121)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘amha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘amha’, such as in\] purisā tiṭṭhanti.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘yo’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ does not follow, such as in\] ahaṃ gacchāmi.

What is the purpose of saying “the first \[inflection\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ is not the first inflection, such as in\] amhākaṃ passasi tvaṃ.

---

**122\. 99\. Ntussa nto (297).** \[Ntussa \+ nto, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **nto**-ādeso hoti **yo**mhi paṭhame. 

Guṇavanto tiṭṭhanti. 

**Ntusse** ti kimatthaṃ? Sabbe sattā gacchanti. 

**Paṭhame** ti kimatthaṃ? Guṇavante passanti janā.

---

‘Nto’ of ‘ntu’.

When the first \[inflection\] ‘yo’ follows, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘nto’.

---

Example:

1. **Guṇavanto** \= guṇavantu \+ yo (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘nto’ (§122)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘ntu’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘ntu’, such as in\] sabbe sattā gacchanti.

What is the purpose of saying “the first \[inflection\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when it is not the first inflection, such as in\] guṇavante passanti janā.

---

**123\. 103\. Ntassa se vā (298).** \[Ntassa \+ se \+ vā, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **ntassā**deso hoti vā **se** vibhattimhi. 

Sīlavantassa jhāyino (Khu. i, 29), sīlavato jhāyino vā. 

**Se** ti kimatthaṃ? Sīlavā tiṭṭhati.

---

Optionally, when ‘sa’ follows, ‘ntassa’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘sa’ follows, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘ntassa’.

---

Example:

1. **Sīlavantassa** \= sīlavantu \+ sa (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘ntassa’ (§123)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘sa’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘sa’ does not follow, such as in\] sīlavā tiṭṭhati.

---

**124\. 98\. Ā simhi (299).** \[Ā \+ simhi, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **ā**-ādeso hoti **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Guṇavā, paññavā, sīlavā, balavā, dhanavā, mahimā, satimā (M. i, 70), dhitimā (S. i, 170). 

**Ntusse** ti kimatthaṃ? Puriso tiṭṭhati. 

**Simhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Sīlavanto tiṭṭhanti.

---

When ‘si’ follows, ‘ā’.

When the inflection ‘si’ follows, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘ā’.

---

Example:

1. **Guṇavā** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§124); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Satimā** \= satimantu \+ si (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§124); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘ntu’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘ntu’, such as in\] puriso tiṭṭhati.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in\] sīlavanto tiṭṭhanti.

---

**125\. 198\. Aṃ napuṃsake (300-1).** \[Aṃ \+ napuṃsake, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **aṃ**-ādeso hoti **si**mhi vibhattimhi napuṃsake vattamānassa. 

Guṇavaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati; rucimaṃ pupphaṃ virocati.

 **Simhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Vaṇṇavantaṃ agandhakaṃ virūḷhapupphaṃ passasi tvaṃ.

---

In the neuter, ‘aṃ’.

When the inflection ‘si’ follows and the gender is neuter, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘aṃ’.

---

Example:

1. **Guṇavaṃ** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§125); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in\] vaṇṇavantaṃ agandhakaṃ virūḷhapupphaṃ passasi tvaṃ.

---

**126\. 101\. Avaṇṇā ca ge (301-2).** \[Avaṇṇā \+ ca \+ ge, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **aṃ-a**vaṇṇā ca honti **ge** pare. 

Bho guṇavaṃ, bho guṇava, bho guṇavā. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And when ‘ga’ follows, \[there is ‘aṃ’\], ‘a’ and ‘ā’.

When ‘ga’ follows, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘aṃ’, ‘a’ and ‘ā’.

---

Example:

1. **Guṇavaṃ** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§126); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Guṇava** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘a’ (§126); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Guṇavā \=** guṇavantu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§126); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

“Ca” is for dragging.

---

**127\. 102\. To-ti-tā sa-smiṃ-nāsu (303).** \[To-ti-tā \+ sa-smiṃ-nāsu, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **to-ti-tā**desā honti vā **sa smiṃ nā** icc etesu yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Guṇavato, guṇavantassa; guṇavati, guṇavantasmiṃ; guṇavatā, guṇavantena; satimato, satimantassa; satimati, satimantasmiṃ; satimatā, satimantena. 

**Etesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Guṇavā. Satimā (M. i, 70).

---

When ‘sa’, ‘smiṃ’ and ‘nā’ follow, \[there are\] ‘to’, ‘ti’ and ‘tā’.

Optionally, when ‘sa’, ‘smiṃ’ and ‘nā’ follow, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘to’, ‘ti’ and ‘tā’ respectively.

---

Example:

1. **Guṇavato** \= guṇavantu \+ sa (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘to’ (§127)).  
2. **Guṇavati** \= guṇavantu \+ smiṃ (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ti’ (§127)).  
3. **Guṇavatā** \= guṇavantu \+ nā (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).  
4. **Satimato** \= satimantu \+ sa (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘to’ (§127)).  
5. **Satimati** \= satimantu \+ smiṃ (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ti’ (§127)).  
6. **Satimatā** \= satimantu \+ nā (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).

What is the purpose of saying “when these follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when these do not follow, such as in\] guṇavā, satimā.

---

**128\. 104\. Naṃmhi taṃ vā (304).** \[Naṃmhi \+ taṃ \+ vā, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa savibhattissa **taṃ**-ādeso hoti vā **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Guṇavataṃ, guṇavantānaṃ; satimataṃ, satimantānaṃ. 

**Naṃmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Guṇavanto tiṭṭhanti. Satimanto tiṭṭhanti.

---

Optionally, when ‘naṃ’ follows, ‘taṃ’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ together with the inflection is substituted by ‘taṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Guṇavataṃ** \= guṇavantu \+ naṃ (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘taṃ’ (§128)).  
2. **Satimataṃ** \= satimantu \+ naṃ (‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘taṃ’ (§128)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘naṃ’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘naṃ’ does not follow, such as in\] guṇavanto tiṭṭhanti, satimanto tiṭṭhanti.

---

**129\. 222\. Imass’ idaṃ aṃ-sisu napuṃsake (305).** \[Imassa \+ idaṃ \+ aṃ-sisu \+ napuṃsake, 4\]

Sabbass’ eva **ima**saddassa savibhattissa **idaṃ**-ādeso hoti vā **aṃ-si**su napuṃsake vattamānassa. 

Idaṃ cittaṃ passasi; idaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati; imaṃ cittaṃ passasi; imaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati. 

**Napuṃsake** ti kimatthaṃ? Imaṃ purisaṃ passasi. Ayaṃ puriso tiṭṭhati.

---

In the neuter, when ‘aṃ’ and ‘si’ follow, “idaṃ” \[of\] “ima”.

Optionally, in the neuter gender, when ‘aṃ’ and ‘si’ follow, the whole word “ima” together with the inflection is substituted by “idaṃ”.

---

Example:

1. **Idaṃ** \= ima \+ aṃ (“ima” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “idaṃ” (§129)).

What is the purpose of saying “in the neuter”? \[This sutta does not apply when it is not neuter, such as in\] imaṃ purisaṃ passasi; ayaṃ puriso tiṭṭhati.

---

**130\. 225\. Amuss’ āduṃ (308).** \[Amussa \+ āduṃ, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **amu**saddassa savibhattissa **aduṃ**-ādeso hoti **aṃ-si**su napuṃsake vattamānassa. 

Aduṃ (Khu. v, 34) pupphaṃ passasi; aduṃ (Khu. v, 34) pupphaṃ virocati. 

**Napuṃsake** ti kimatthaṃ? Amuṃ (M. i, 210) rājānaṃ passasi; asu (D. ii, 162) rājā tiṭṭhati.

---
