“Aduṃ” \[of\] “amu”.

In the neuter gender, when ‘aṃ’ and ‘si’ follow, the whole word “amu” together with the inflection is substituted by “aduṃ”.

---

Example:

1. **Aduṃ** \= amu \+ aṃ (“amu” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “aduṃ” (§130)).

What is the purpose of saying “in the neuter”? \[This sutta does not apply when it is not neuter, such as in\] amuṃ rājānaṃ passasi; asu rājā tiṭṭhati.

---

**131\. 0\. Itthi-puma-napuṃsaka-saṅkhyaṃ (309).** \[Itthi-puma-napuṃsaka-saṅkhyaṃ, 1\]

“Itthi-puma-napuṃsaka-saṅkhyaṃ” icc etaṃ adhikāratthaṃ veditabbaṃ.

---

A numeral that is feminine, masculine and neuter.

“A numeral that is feminine, masculine and neuter”, this should be understood as a governing aphorism (adhikāra-sutta).[^30] 

---

**132\. 228\. Yosu dvinnaṃ dve ca (310).** \[Yosu \+ dvinnaṃ \+ dve \+ ca, 4\]

**Dvinnaṃ** saṅkhyānaṃ itthi-puma-napuṃsake vattamānānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **dve** hoti **yo** icc etesu. 

Dve itthiyo, dve dhammā, dve rūpāni. 

**Yosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Dvīsu. 

**Caggahaṇena duve dvaya ubha ubhaya duvi** ca honti **yo nā aṃ naṃ** icc etesu. Duve (DA. i, 58) samaṇā. Duve (DA. i, 58) brāhmaṇā. Duve (DA. i, 58) janā. Dvayena, dvayaṃ (Vin. i, 24; VinA. i, 105). Ubhinnaṃ (Khu. v, 18). Ubhayesaṃ, duvinnaṃ.

---

And when ‘yo’ follow, “dve” \[of\] “dvi”.

When ‘yo’ follow, the numeral “dvi”, which is feminine, masculine and neuter, together with the inflection is substituted by “dve”.

---

Example:

1. **Dve** \= dvi \+ yo (“dvi” together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “dve” (§132)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘yo’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ does not follow, such as in\] dvīsu.

By taking “ca”, when ‘yo’, ‘nā’, ‘aṃ’ and ‘naṃ’ follow, there are also the substitutions by “duve”, “dvaya”, “ubha”, “ubhaya” and “duvi”.

Examples:

1. **Duve** samaṇā, **duve** brāhmaṇā, **duve** janā.  
2. **Dvayena**, **dvayaṃ**.  
3. **Ubhinnaṃ**.  
4. **Ubhayesaṃ**, **duvinnaṃ**.

---

**133\. 230\. Ti-catunnaṃ tisso catasso tayo cattāro tīṇi cattāri (311).** \[Ti-catunnaṃ \+ tisso-catasso-tayo-cattāro-tīṇi-cattāri, 2\]

**Ti-catu**nnaṃ saṅkhyānaṃ itthi-puma-napuṃsake vattamānānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tisso** catasso tayo cattāro tīṇi cattāri icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ yo icc etesu. 

Tisso vedanā (D. iii, 181); catasso disā; tayo janā (Khu. v, 196); cattāro purisā; tīṇi āyatanāni; cattāri ariyasaccāni (Khu. i, 3). 

**Yosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Tīsu, catūsu.

---

“Tisso”, “catasso”, “tayo”, “cattāro”, “tīṇi”, “cattāri” \[of\] “ti” and “catu”.

When ‘yo’ follows, the numerals “ti” and “catu”, which are feminine, masculine and neuter, together with the inflections are substituted by “tisso”, “catasso”, “tayo”, “cattāro”, “tīṇi” and “cattāri” respectively.

---

Example:

1. **Tisso** \= ti \+ yo (“ti”, which is feminine, together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “tisso” (§133)).  
2. **Catasso** \= catu \+ yo (“catu”, which is feminine, together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “catasso” (§133)).  
3. **Tayo** \= ti \+ yo (“ti”, which is masculine, together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “tayo” (§133)).  
4. **Cattāro** \= catu \+ yo (“catu”, which is masculine, together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “cattāro” (§133)).  
5. **Tīṇi** \= ti \+ yo (“ti”, which is neuter, together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “tīṇi” (§133)).   
6. **Cattāri** \= catu \+ yo (“catu”, which is neuter, together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “cattāri” (§133)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘yo’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ does not follow, such as in\] tīsu, catūsu.

---

**134\. 251\. Pañcādīnam akāro (247).** \[Pañca-ādīnaṃ \+ akāro, 2\]

Pañcādīnaṃ saṅkhyānaṃ itthi-puma-napuṃsake vattamānānaṃ savibhattissa antassa sarassa **a**kāro hoti **yo** icc etesu. 

Pañca, pañca; cha, cha; satta, satta; aṭṭha, aṭṭha; nava, nava; dasa, dasa. 

**Pañcādīnam** iti kimatthaṃ? Dve, tayo.

---

The letter ‘a’ of “pañca”, etc.

When ‘yo’ follows, the end vowel together with the inflection, of the numerals “pañca”, etc., which are feminine, masculine and neuter, is substituted by the letter ‘a’.[^31]

---

Example:

1. **Pañca** \= pañca \+ yo (‘a’ of “pañca” together with the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘a’ (§134)).  
2. The formation of the rest of the examples is similar to the previous one.

What is the purpose of saying “of the numerals ‘pañca’, etc.”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “pañca”, etc., such as in\] dve, tayo.

---

**135\. 118\. Rājassa rañño rājino se (314).** \[Rājassa \+ rañño-rājino \+ se, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **rāja**saddassa savibhattissa **rañño rājino** (Khu. i, 324) icc ete ādesā honti **se** vibhattimhi. 

Rañño, rājino. 

**Se** ti kimatthaṃ? Raññā.

---

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, “rañño” and “rājino” \[of\] “rāja”.

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, the whole word “rāja” together with the inflection is substituted by “rañño” and “rājino”.

---

Examples:

1. **Rañño** \= rāja \+ sa (“rāja” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “rañño” (§135)).  
2. **Rājino** \= rāja \+ sa (“rāja” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “rājino” (§135)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘sa’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘sa’ does not follow, such as in\] raññā.

---

**136\. 119\. Raññaṃ naṃmhi vā (315).** \[Raññaṃ \+ naṃmhi \+ vā, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **rāja**saddassa savibhattissa **raññaṃ**-ādeso hoti vā **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Raññaṃ, rājūnaṃ (Khu. i, 88) idaṃ raṭṭhaṃ.

---

Optionally, when ‘naṃ’ follows, “raññaṃ”.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows, the whole word “rāja” together with the inflection is substituted by “raññaṃ”.

---

Example:

1. **Raññaṃ** \= rāja \+ naṃ (“rāja” together with the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by “raññaṃ” (§136)).

---

**137\. 116\. Nāmhi raññā vā (316).** \[Nāmhi \+ raññā \+ vā, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **rāja**saddassa savibhattissa **raññā**-ādeso hoti vā **nā**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Tena raññā kataṃ, rājena vā kataṃ. 

**Nāmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Rañño santakaṃ.

---

Optionally, when ‘nā’ follows, “raññā”.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘nā’ follows, the whole word “rāja” together with the inflection is substituted by “raññā”.

---

Example:

1. **Raññā** \= rāja \+ nā (“rāja” together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by “raññā” (§137)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘nā’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘nā’ does not follow, such as in\] rañño santakaṃ.

---

**138\. 121\. Smiṃmhi raññe rājini (317).** \[Smiṃmhi \+ raññe-rājini, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **rāja**saddassa savibhattissa **raññe-rājini** icc ete ādesā honti **smiṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Raññe, rājini sīlaṃ tiṭṭhati.

---

When ‘smiṃ’ follows, “raññe” and “rājini”.

When the inflection ‘smiṃ’ follows, the whole word “rāja” together with the inflection is substituted by “raññe” and “rājini”.

---

Examples:

1. **Raññe** \= rāja \+ smiṃ (“rāja” together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by “raññe” (§138)).  
2. **Rājini** \= rāja \+ smiṃ (“rāja” together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by “rājini” (§138)).

---

**139\. 245\. Tumh’-āmhākaṃ tayi mayi (318).** \[Tumha-amhākaṃ \+ tayi-mayi, 2\]

Sabbessaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tayi mayi** icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ **smiṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Tayi, mayi. 

**Smiṃmhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Tvaṃ bhavasi. Ahaṃ bhavāmi.

---

“Tayi” and “mayi” \[of\] “tumha” and “amha”.

When the inflection ‘smiṃ’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayi”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mayi”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tayi** \= tumha \+ smiṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by “tayi” (§139)).  
2. **Mayi** \= amha \+ smiṃ (“amha” together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by “mayi” (§139)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘smiṃ’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘smiṃ’ does not follow, such as in\] tvaṃ bhavasi, ahaṃ bhavāmi.

---

**140\. 232\. Tvam-ahaṃ simhi ca (319-20).** \[Tvaṃ-ahaṃ \+ simhi \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tvaṃ-ahaṃ** icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Tvaṃ, ahaṃ. 

**Simhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Tayi, mayi. 

**Caggahaṇena tuvaṃ** ca hoti. Tuvaṃ satthā (M. ii, 354).

---

And when ‘si’ follows, “tvaṃ” and “ahaṃ”.

When the inflection ‘si’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tvaṃ”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “ahaṃ”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tvaṃ** \= tumha \+ si (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by “tvaṃ” (§140)).  
2. **Ahaṃ** \= amha \+ si (“amha” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by “ahaṃ” (§140)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in\] tayi, mayi.

By taking “ca”, there is also the substitution by “tuvaṃ”. 

Example:

1. **Tuvaṃ** \= tumha \+ si (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by “tuvaṃ” (§140)).

---

**141\. 241\. Tava-mama se.** \[Tava-mama \+ se, 2\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tava mama** icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ **se** vibhattimhi. 

Tava, mama. 

**Se** ti kimatthaṃ? Tayi, mayi.

---

When ‘sa’ follows, “tava” and “mama”.

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tava”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mama”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tava** \= tumha \+ sa (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “tava” (§141)).  
2. **Mama** \= amha \+ sa (“amha” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “mama” (§141)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘sa’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘sa’ does not follow, such as in\] tayi, mayi.

---

**142\. 242\. Tuyhaṃ mayhañ ca (321).** \[Tuyhaṃ-mayhaṃ \+ ca, 2\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tuyhaṃ mayhaṃ** icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ **se** vibhattimhi. 

Tuyhaṃ, mayhaṃ dhanaṃ dīyate. 

**Se** ti kimatthaṃ? Tayā, mayā.

---

And “tuyhaṃ” and “mayhaṃ”.

When the inflection ‘sa’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tuyhaṃ”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mayhaṃ”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tuyhaṃ** \= tumha \+ sa (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “tuyhaṃ” (§142)).  
2. **Mayhaṃ** \= amha \+ sa (“amha” together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “mayhaṃ” (§142)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘sa’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘sa’ does not follow, such as in\] tayā, mayā.

---

**143\. 235\. Taṃ-mam aṃmhi (322).** \[Taṃ-maṃ \+ aṃmhi, 2\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **taṃ maṃ** icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ aṃmhi vibhattimhi. 

Taṃ, maṃ. 

**Aṃmhi** ti kimatthaṃ? Tayā, mayā.

---

When ‘aṃ’ follows, “taṃ” and “maṃ”.

When the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “taṃ”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “maṃ”.

---

Examples:

1. **Taṃ** \= tumha \+ aṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “taṃ” (§143)).  
2. **Maṃ** \= amha \+ aṃ (“amha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “maṃ” (§143)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘aṃ’ does not follow, such as in\] tayā, mayā.

---

**144\. 234\. Tavaṃ mamañ ca navā (322).** \[Tavaṃ-mamaṃ \+ ca \+ navā, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tavaṃ-mamaṃ** icc ete ādesā honti navā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ **aṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Tavaṃ, mamaṃ passati. 

**Navā** ti kimatthaṃ? Taṃ, maṃ passati. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And occasionally “tavaṃ” and “mamaṃ”.

Occasionally, when the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tavaṃ”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mamaṃ”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tavaṃ** \= tumha \+ aṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “tavaṃ” (§144)).  
2. **Mamaṃ** \= amha \+ aṃ (“amha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “mamaṃ” (§144)).

What is the purpose of saying “occasionally”? \[To exclude the cases in which this rule does not apply, such as in\] taṃ, maṃ passati.

“Ca” is for dragging.[^32]

---

**145\. 238\. Nāmhi tayā mayā (323).** \[Nāmhi \+ tayā-mayā, 2\]

Sabbessaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ **tayā mayā** icc ete ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ **nā**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Tayā, mayā kataṃ. 

**Nāmhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Tumhehi, amhehi.

---

When ‘nā’ follows, “tayā” and “mayā”.

When the inflection ‘nā’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayā”, and the whole word “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mayā”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tayā** \= tumha \+ nā (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by “tayā” (§145)).  
2. **Mayā** \= amha \+ nā (“amha” together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by “mayā” (§145)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the inflection ‘nā’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘nā’ does not follow, such as in\] tumhehi, amhehi.

---

**146\. 236\. Tumhassa tuvaṃ-tvaṃ aṃmhi (324).** \[Tumhassa \+ tuvaṃ-tvaṃ \+ aṃmhi, 3\]

Sabbassa **tumha**saddassa savibhattissa **tuvaṃ tvaṃ** icc ete ādesā honti **aṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Kaliṅgarassa tuvaṃ maññe, kaṭṭhassa tvaṃ maññe.

---

When ‘aṃ’ follows, “tuvaṃ” and “tvaṃ” \[of\] “tumha”.

When the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows, the whole word “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tuvaṃ” and “tvaṃ”.

---

Examples:

1. **Tuvaṃ** \= tumha \+ aṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “tuvaṃ” (§146)).  
2. **Tvaṃ** \= tumha \+ aṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is substituted by “tvaṃ” (§146)).

---

**147\. 246\. Padato dutiyā-catutthī-chaṭṭhīsu vo-no (325).** \[Padato \+ dutiyā-catutthī-chaṭṭhīsu \+ vo-no, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ **vo-no**-ādesā honti navā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhī icc etesu bahuvacanesu. 

Pahāya vo bhikkhave gamissāmi (Khu. iv, 265); mā no ajja vikantiṃsu (Khu. vi, 93) rañño sūdā mahānase. Evaṃ **dutiyatthe**. 

Dhammaṃ vo bhikkhave desessāmi (M. iii, 86); saṃvibhajetha no rajjena (D. ii, 188). Evaṃ **catutthyatthe**. 

Tuṭṭho ’smi vo bhikkhave pakatiyā (Khu. vi, 89); satthā no Bhagavā anuppatto (M. i, 
266). Evaṃ chaṭṭhyatthe. 

**Navā** ti kimatthaṃ? Eso amhākaṃ satthā. 

**Tumha-amhākaṃ** iti kimatthaṃ? Ete isayo passasi. 

**Padato** ti kimatthaṃ? Tumhākaṃ satthā. 

**Etesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Gacchatha tumhe.

---

After a word, when the second, fourth and sixth \[inflections\] follow, “vo” and “no”.

Occasionally, when the second, fourth and sixth inflections in the plural follow, the whole words “tumha” and “amha”, when they come after a word, together with the inflections, are substituted by “vo” and “no” respectively.[^33]

---

Examples in the sense of the second \[inflection\]:

1. **Vo** \= tumha \+ yo (“tumha” together with the second plural inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “vo” (§147)).  
2. **No** \= amha \+ yo (“amha” together with the second plural inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by “no” (§147)).

“Pahāya vo bhikkhave gamissāmi” (having left you, O bhikkhus, I will go).

“Mā no ajja vikantiṃsu rañño sūdā mahānase” (may the king's cooks not cut us up today in the royal kitchen).

Examples in the sense of the fourth \[inflection\]:

1. **Vo** \= tumha \+ naṃ (“tumha” together with the fourth plural inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by “vo” (§147)).  
2. **No** \= amha \+ naṃ (“amha” together with the fourth plural inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by “no” (§147)).

“Dhammaṃ vo bhikkhave desessāmi” (O bhikkhus, I will teach you the Dhamma).

“Saṃvibhajetha no rajjena” (share the kingdom with us).

Examples in the sense of the sixth \[inflection\]:

1. **Vo** \= tumha \+ naṃ (“tumha” together with the sixth plural inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by “vo” (§147)).  
2. **No** \= amha \+ naṃ (“amha” together with the sixth plural inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by “no” (§147)).

“Tuṭṭho ’smi vo bhikkhave pakatiyā” (O bhikkhus, I am satisfied with your nature).

“Satthā no Bhagavā anuppatto” (the Blessed One, our Teacher, has arrived).

What is the purpose of saying “occasionally”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] eso amhākaṃ satthā.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘tumha’ and ‘amha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “tumha” or “amha”, such as in\] ete isayo passasi.

What is the purpose of saying “after a word”? \[This sutta does not apply when they do not come after a word, such as in\] tumhākaṃ satthā.

What is the purpose of saying “when the second, fourth and sixth \[inflections\] follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when these inflections do not follow, such as in\] gacchatha tumhe.

---

**148\. 247\. Te-me ’kavacanesu ca (326).** \[Te-me \+ ekavacanesu \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ **te me**-ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ catutthī chaṭṭhī icc etesu ekavacanesu. 

Dadāmi te gāmavarāni pañca (Khu. v, 229); dadāhi me gāmavaraṃ; idaṃ te raṭṭhaṃ (Khu. vi, 66, 131); ayaṃ me putto. 

**Padato** ti kimatthaṃ? Tava ñāti, mama ñāti.

---

And when \[those inflections\] follow in the singular, “te” and “me”.

When the fourth and sixth inflections in the singular follow, the whole words “tumha” and “amha”, when they come after a word, together with the inflections, are substituted by “te” and “me” respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Te** \= tumha \+ sa (“tumha” together with the fourth singular inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “te” (§148)).  
2. **Me** \= amha \+ sa (“amha” together with the fourth singular inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by “me” (§148)).

“Dadāmi te gāmavarāni pañca” (I give you five villages as a reward).

“Dadāhi me gāmavaraṃ” (give me a village as a reward).

“Idaṃ te raṭṭhaṃ” (this is your kingdom).

“Ayaṃ me putto” (this is my son).

What is the purpose of saying “after a word”? \[This sutta does not apply when they do not come after a word, such as in\] tava ñāti, mama ñāti.

---

**149\. 248\. Na aṃmhi (327).** \[Na \+ aṃmhi, 2\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ **te-me**-ādesā na honti aṃmhi vibhattimhi. 

Passeyya taṃ vassasataṃ arogaṃ (Khu. vi, 14); so maṃ bravīti.

---

When ‘aṃ’ follows, no \[change\].

When the inflection ‘aṃ’ follows, the whole words “tumha” and “amha”, when they come after a word, together with the inflections, are not substituted by “te” and “me”.

---

Examples:

1. **Taṃ** \= tumha \+ aṃ (here “tumha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is not substituted by “te”, but by “taṃ” (§143)).  
2. **Maṃ** \= amha \+ aṃ (here “amha” together with the inflection ‘aṃ’ is not substituted by “me”, but by “maṃ” (§143)).

“Passeyya taṃ vassasataṃ arogaṃ” (may you see him in health for a hundred years).

“So maṃ bravīti” (he speaks to me).

---

**150\. 249\. Vā tatiye ca (328).** \[Vā \+ tatiye \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ tumha-amhasaddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ te-me-ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ tatiy’-ekavacane pare. 

Kataṃ te pāpaṃ, kataṃ me pāpaṃ, kataṃ tayā pāpaṃ, kataṃ mayā pāpaṃ. 

**Padato** ti kimatthaṃ? Tayā kataṃ, mayā kataṃ.

---

And optionally, in the third \[singular inflection\].

Optionally, when the third singular inflection follows, the whole words “tumha” and “amha”, when they come after a word, together with the inflections, are substituted by “te” and “me” respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Te** \= tumha \+ nā (“tumha” together with the third singular inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by “te” (§150)).  
2. **Me** \= amha \+ nā (“amha” together with the third singular inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by “me” (§150)).

“Kataṃ te pāpaṃ” (the evil done by you).

“Kataṃ me pāpaṃ” (the evil done by me).

What is the purpose of saying “after a word”? \[This sutta does not apply when they do not come after a word, such as in\] tayā kataṃ, mayā kataṃ.

---

**151\. 250\. Bahuvacanesu vo no (329).** \[Bahuvacanesu \+ vo-no, 2\]

Sabbesaṃ **tumha-amha**saddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ **vo-no**-ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ tatiyābahuvacanesu paresu. 

Kataṃ vo kammaṃ, kataṃ no kammaṃ.

---

In the plural, “vo” and “no”.

When the third plural inflection follows, the whole words “tumha” and “amha”, when they come after a word, together with the inflections, are substituted by “vo” and “no” respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Vo** \= tumha \+ hi (“tumha” together with the third plural inflection ‘hi’ is substituted by “vo” (§151)).  
2. **No** \= amha \+ hi (“amha” together with the third plural inflection ‘hi’ is substituted by “no” (§151)).

“Kataṃ vo kammaṃ” (the action done by you).

“Kataṃ no kammaṃ” (the action done by us).

---

**152\. 136\. Pumantass’ ā simhi (331-2).** \[Puma-antassa \+ ā \+ simhi, 3\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa savibhattissa **ā**-ādeso hoti **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Pumā tiṭṭhati. 

**Simhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Pumāno tiṭṭhanti. 

Antaggahaṇena **maghava yuva** icc evam ādīnam antassa savibhattissa **ā-**ādeso hoti. Maghavā, yuvā.

---

When ‘si’ follows, ‘ā’ of the end of “puma”.

When the inflection ‘si’ follows, the end vowel of “puma” together with the inflection is substituted by ‘ā’.

---

Example:

1. **Pumā** \= puma \+ si (‘a’ of “puma” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§152)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in\] pumāno tiṭṭhanti.

Example:

1. **Pumāno** \= puma \+ yo (‘a’ of “puma” together with ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§155)).

By taking “anta”, the end vowel of “maghava”, “yuva”, etc., together with the inflections is also substituted by ‘ā’.

Examples:

1. **Maghavā** \= maghava \+ si (‘a’ of “maghava” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§152)).  
2. **Yuvā** \= yuva \+ si (‘a’ of “yuva” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§152)).

---

**153\. 138\. Am ālapanekavacane (333).** \[Aṃ \+ ālapana-ekavacane, 2\]

Puma icc evam antassa savibhattissa aṃ-ādeso hoti ālapanekavacane pare. 

He pumaṃ. 

**Ālapane** ti kimatthaṃ? Pumā. 

**Ekavacane** ti kimatthaṃ? He pumāno.

---

When the vocative singular follows, ‘aṃ’.

When the vocative singular follows, the end vowel of “puma” together with the inflection is substituted by ‘aṃ’.

---

Example:

1. **Pumaṃ** \= puma \+ si (‘a’ of “puma” together with the inflection ‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§153)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the vocative follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when it is not vocative, such as in\] pumā.

What is the purpose of saying “when the singular follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when it is not singular, such as in\] he pumāno.

---

**154\. 0\. Samāse ca vibhāsā (334).** \[Samāse \+ ca \+ vibhāsā, 3\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa samāse ca **aṃ**-ādeso hoti vibhāsā samāse kate. 

Itthī ca pumā ca napuṃsakaṃ ca itthipumannapuṃsakāni. Itthipumannapuṃsakānaṃ samūho itthipumannapuṃsakasamūho. 

**Vibhāsā** ti kimatthaṃ? Itthipumanapuṃsakāni.

---

And facultatively in a compound.

Facultatively, when a compound is formed, the end vowel of “puma” together with the inflection is substituted by ‘aṃ’.

---

Example:

1. **Itthipumannapuṃsakāni** \= itthī ca pumā ca napuṃsakaṃ ca (‘ā’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§154); ‘ṃ’ is substituted by ‘n’ (§31)).

What is the purpose of saying “facultatively”? \[This sutta does not always apply, such as in\] itthipumanapuṃsakāni.

---

**155\. 137\. Yosv āno (335).** \[Yosu \+ āno, 2\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa savibhattissa **āno**-ādeso hoti **yo**su vibhattīsu. 

Pumāno, he pumāno. 

**Yosv** iti kimatthaṃ? Pumā.

---

When ‘yo’ follow, ‘āno’.

When the inflections ‘yo’ follow, the end vowel of “puma” together with the inflection is substituted by ‘āno’.

---

Examples:

1. **Pumāno** \= puma \+ yo (‘a’ of “puma” together with ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§155)).  
2. **He pumāno** \= puma \+ yo (vocative plural; ‘a’ of “puma” together with ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§155)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘yo’ follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ does not follow, such as in\] pumā.

---

**156\. 142\. Āne smiṃmhi vā (336).** \[Āne \+ smiṃmhi \+ vā, 3\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa savibhattissa **āne**-ādeso hoti vā **smiṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Pumāne, pume vā.

---

Optionally, when ‘smiṃ’ follows, ‘āne’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘smiṃ’ follows, the end vowel of “puma” together with the inflection is substituted by ‘āne’.

---

Example:

1. **Pumāne** \= puma \+ smiṃ (‘a’ of “puma” together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘āne’ (§156)).

---

**157\. 140\. Hi-vibhattimhi ca (337-8).** \[Hi-vibhattimhi \+ ca, 2\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa **hi** vibhattimhi ca **āne**-ādeso hoti. 

Pumānehi, pumānebhi. 

Puna **vibhatti**ggahaṇaṃ kimatthaṃ? **Savibhattiggahaṇa-**nivattanatthaṃ. Pumānehi. 

**Ca**ggahaṇena **maghava yuva** icc evamādīnam antassa **āna**-ādeso hoti **si yo aṃ yo** icc etesu vibhattīsu. **Puma-kamma-thāma**ntassa c’ **u**kāro hoti **sa-smā**su vibhattīsu. Maghavāno. Maghavānā, maghavānaṃ, maghavāne. Yuvāno, yuvānā, yuvānaṃ, yuvāne; pumuno, pumunā. Kammuno, kammunā. Thāmuno, thāmunā.

---

And when the inflection ‘hi’ follows.

When the inflection ‘hi’ follows, the end vowel of “puma” is substituted by ‘āne’.

---

Example:

1. **Pumānehi** \= puma \+ hi (‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘āne’ (§157)).

What is the purpose of taking “vibhatti” again? To prevent the inclusion of “savibhatti”[^34] in this sutta. Example: pumānehi.

By taking “ca”, when the inflections ‘si’, ‘yo’, ‘aṃ’ and ‘yo’ follow, the end vowel of “maghava”, “yuva”, etc., is substituted by ‘āna’. And when the inflections ‘sa’ and ‘smā’ follow, the \[end\] vowel of “puma”, “kamma” and “thāma” is substituted by the letter ‘u’.

Examples:

1. **Maghavāno** \= maghava \+ si (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “maghava” is substituted by ‘āna’ (§157); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of ‘āna’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Maghavānā** \= maghava \+ yo (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “maghava” is substituted by ‘āna’ (§157); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§107); ‘a’ of ‘āna’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Maghavānaṃ** \= maghava \+ aṃ (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “maghava” is substituted by ‘āna’ (§157); ‘a’ of ‘āna’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Maghavāne** \= maghava \+ yo (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “maghava” is substituted by ‘āna’ (§157); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§107); ‘a’ of ‘āna’ is elided (§83)).  
5. The formation of **yuvāno**, **yuvānā**, **yuvānaṃ**, **yuvāne** is similar to the previous one.  
6. **Pumuno** \= puma \+ sa (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘u’ (§157); ‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).  
7. **Pumunā** \= puma \+ smā (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘u’ (§157); ‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘nā’ (§215)).  
8. The formation of **kammuno**, **kammunā**, **thāmuno**, **thāmunā** is similar to the previous one.

---

**158\. 143\. Susmim ā vā (339).** \[Susmiṃ \+ ā \+ vā, 3\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa **su** icc etasmiṃ vibhattimhi **ā**-ādeso hoti vā. 

Pumāsu, pumesu vā.

---

Optionally, when ‘su’ follows, \[there is\] ‘ā’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘su’ follows, the end vowel of “puma” is substituted by ‘ā’.

---

Example:

1. **Pumāsu** \= puma \+ su (‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘ā’ (§158)).  
2. **Pumesu[^35]** \= puma \+ su (‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).

---

**159\. 139\. U nāmhi ca (340).** \[U \+ nāmhi \+ ca, 3\]

**Puma** icc evam antassa **ā-u**-ādesā honti vā **nā**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Pumānā, pumunā, pumena vā. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And when ‘nā’ follows, \[there is\] ‘u’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘nā’ follows, the end vowel of “puma” is substituted by ‘ā’ or ‘u’.

---

Examples:

1. **Pumānā** \= puma \+ nā (‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘ā’ (§159)).  
2. **Pumunā** \= puma \+ nā (‘a’ of “puma” is substituted by ‘u’ (§159)).

The taking of “ca” is for dragging.[^36]

---

**160\. 197\. A kammantassa ca (341).** \[A \+ kamma-antassa \+ ca, 3\]

**Kamma** icc evam antassa ca **u-a**-ādesā honti vā **nā**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Kammunā (Khu. i, 299), kammanā (M. ii, 408), kammena vā.

**Ca**ggahaṇena **maghava yuva** icc evam antassa **ā-**ādeso hoti kvaci **nā su** icc etesu vibhattīsu. Maghavānā, maghavāsu, maghavesu, maghavena vā. Yuvānā, yuvāsu, yuvesu, yuvena vā.

---

And \[there is\] ‘a’ of the end \[vowel\] of “kamma”.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘nā’ follows, the end vowel of “kamma” is substituted by ‘u’ or ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Kammunā** \= kamma \+ nā (‘a’ of “kamma” is substituted by ‘u’ (§160)).  
2. **Kammanā** \= kamma \+ nā (‘a’ of “kamma” is substituted by ‘a’ (§160)).

By taking “ca”, sometimes, when the inflections ‘nā’ and ‘su’ follow, the end vowel of “maghava”, “yuva”, etc., is substituted by ‘ā’.

Example:

1. **Maghavānā** \= maghava \+ nā (by “ca”, ‘a’ of “maghava” is substituted by ‘ā’ (§160)).

---

**Iti nāma-kappe dutiyo kaṇḍo**

**Thus ends the second section of the noun chapter**

**TATIYA-KAṆḌA**  
**THIRD SECTION**

---

**161\. 244\. Tumh’-amhehi nam ākaṃ (344).** \[Tumha-amhehi \+ naṃ \+ ākaṃ, 3\]

Tehi **tumha-amhe**hi **naṃ**vacanassa **ākaṃ** hoti. 

Tumhākaṃ, amhākaṃ. 

**Naṃ** iti kimatthaṃ? Tumhehi, amhehi.

---

After “tumha” and “amha”, ‘naṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘ākaṃ’.

After \[the bases\] “tumha” and “amha”, there is ‘ākaṃ’ of the inflection ‘naṃ’.

---

Example:

1. **Tumhākaṃ** \= tumha \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘ākaṃ’ (§161); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “naṃ”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘naṃ’, such as in:\] Tumhehi, amhehi.

---

**162\. 237\. Vā yv appaṭhamo (345).** \[Vā \+ yo \+ a-ppaṭhamo, 3\]

Tehi **tumha-amhe**hi **yo** appaṭhamo **ākaṃ** hoti vā. 

Tumhākaṃ passāmi, tumhe passāmi vā. Amhākaṃ passasi, amhe passasi vā. 

**Yo** ti kimatthaṃ? Tumhehi, amhehi. 

**Appaṭhamo** ti kimatthaṃ? Gacchatha tumhe, gacchāma mayaṃ. 

**Vā** ti vikappanatthena **yo**naṃ **aṃ ānaṃ** honti. Tumhaṃ, tumhānaṃ. Amhaṃ, amhānaṃ.

---

Optionally, ‘yo’ which is not the first.

Optionally, after \[the bases\] “tumha” and “amha”, ‘yo’, which is not the first \[inflection\], becomes ‘ākaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tumhākaṃ** \= tumha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ākaṃ’ (§162); ‘a’ of “tumha” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Amhākaṃ** \= amha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ākaṃ’ (§162); ‘a’ of “amha” is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “yo”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘yo’, such as in:\] Tumhehi, amhehi.

What is the purpose of saying “which is not the first \[inflection\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when it is the first inflection, such as in:\] Gacchatha tumhe, gacchāma mayaṃ.

By ‘vā’, as an alternative, ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ or ‘ānaṃ’.

Examples:

1. **Tumhaṃ** \= tumha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§162); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Tumhānaṃ** \= tumha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ānaṃ’ (§162); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**163\. 240\. Sass’ aṃ (346).** \[Sassa \+ aṃ, 2\]

Tehi **tumha-amhe**hi **sa**ssa vibhattissa **aṃ**-ādeso hoti vā. 

Tumhaṃ dīyate, tava dīyate. Tumhaṃ pariggaho, tava pariggaho. Amhaṃ dīyate, mama dīyate. Amhaṃ pariggaho, mama pariggaho. 

**Sa**sse ti kimatthaṃ? Tumhesu, amhesu.

---

‘Aṃ’ of ‘sa’.

Optionally, after \[the bases\] “tumha” and “amha”, the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’.

---

Example:

1. **Tumhaṃ** \= tumha \+ sa (‘sa’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§163); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘sa’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘sa’, such as in:\] Tumhesu, amhesu.

---

**164\. 200\. Sabbanāmakārat’ e paṭhamo (347).** \[Sabbanāma-akārato \+ e \+ paṭhamo, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ sabbanāmānaṃ **a**kārato **yo** paṭhamo **e**ttam āpajjate. 

Sabbe, ye, te, ke, tumhe, amhe, ime. 

**Sabbanāmā** ti kimatthaṃ? Devā, asurā, nāgā, gandhabbā, manussā. 

**Akārato** ti kimatthaṃ? Amū purisā tiṭṭhanti. 

**Yo** ti kimatthaṃ? Sabbo, yo, so, ko, ayaṃ. 

**Paṭhama**ggahaṇaṃ uttarasuttatthaṃ.

---

After the letter ‘a’ of the pronouns, the first \[is substituted by\] ‘e’.

After the letter ‘a’ of all the pronouns, the first \[inflection\] ‘yo’ becomes ‘e’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbe** \= sabba \+ yo (‘yo’ becomes ‘e’ (§164); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Ye** \= ya \+ yo (‘yo’ becomes ‘e’ (§164); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Te** \= ta \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§164); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “all the pronouns”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no pronouns, such as in:\] Devā, asurā, nāgā, gandhabbā, manussā.

What is the purpose of saying “after the letter ‘a’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘a’, such as in:\] Amū purisā tiṭṭhanti.

What is the purpose of saying “\[the first inflection\] ‘yo’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘yo’, such as in:\] Sabbo, yo, so, ko, ayaṃ.

The taking of “paṭhama” is for the purpose of the following sutta.

---

**165\. 208\. Dvandaṭṭhā vā (348).** \[Dvanda-aṭṭhā \+ vā, 2\]

Tasmā sabbanām’-**a**kārato dvandaṭṭhā **yo** paṭhamo **e**ttam āpajjate vā. 

Katarakatame, katarakatamā vā. 

**Sabbanāmā** ti kimatthaṃ? Devāsuranāgagandhabbamanussā. 

**Dvandaṭṭhā** ti kimatthaṃ? Te, sabbe.

---

Optionally, in a copulative compound.

Optionally, in a copulative compound, after the \[end\] letter ‘a’ of all the pronouns, the first \[inflection\] ‘yo’ becomes ‘e’.

---

Example:

1. **Katarakatame** \= katarakatama \+ yo (‘yo’, which is the first inflection, becomes ‘e’ (§165); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “pronouns”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no pronouns, such as in:\] Devā-sura-nāga-gandhabba-manussā.

What is the purpose of saying “in a copulative compound”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no copulative compound, such as in:\] Te, sabbe.

---

**166\. 209\. Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ (349).** \[Na \+ aññaṃ \+ sabbanāmikaṃ, 3\]

Sabbanāmikānaṃ dvandaṭṭhe nāññaṃ kāriyaṃ hoti. 

Pubbāparānaṃ, pubbuttarānaṃ, adharuttarānaṃ.

---

No other pronominal \[operation\].

In a copulative compound of pronouns, \[except the substitution ‘e’ of the first \[inflection\] ‘yo’\] there is no other grammatical operation \[such as the other substitutions ‘saṃ’, ‘sānaṃ’, etc.\].

---

Example:

1. **Pubbāparānaṃ** \= pubbāpara \+ naṃ (here ‘naṃ’ does not become ‘saṃ’, ‘sānaṃ’, etc. (§166)).

---

**167\. 210\. Bahubbīhimhi ca (351-2).** \[Bahubbīhimhi \+ ca, 2\]

Bahubbīhimhi ca samāse sabbanāmavidhānañ ca nāññaṃ kāriyaṃ hoti. 

Piyapubbāya, piyapubbānaṃ, piyapubbe, piyapubbassa. 

**Ce** ti kimatthaṃ? Sabbanāmavidhānaṃ hoti. Dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ, dakkhiṇapubbassā, uttarapubbassaṃ, uttarapubbassā.

---

And in a relative compound.

And in a relative compound, \[except the substitution ‘e’ of the first \[inflection\] ‘yo’\] there is no other grammatical operation nor pronominal procedure \[such as the substitutions ‘saṃ’, ‘sānaṃ’, etc.\].

---

Example:

1. **Piyapubbāya** \= piyapubbā \+ smiṃ (here ‘smiṃ’ does not become ‘saṃ’ or ‘sā’; ‘ā’ of “pubbā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘āya’ (§111); ‘ā’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “ca”? \[To allow another\] pronominal procedure.

Example:

1. **Dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ** \= dakkhiṇapubba \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§63)).

---

**168\. 203\. Sabbato naṃ saṃ-sānaṃ (353, 368).** \[Sabbato \+ naṃ \+ saṃ-sānaṃ, 3\]

Sabbato sabbanāmato **naṃ**vacanassa **saṃ sānaṃ** icc ete ādesā honti. 

Sabbesaṃ, sabbesānaṃ, sabbāsaṃ, sabbāsānaṃ. Yesaṃ, yesānaṃ, yāsaṃ, yāsānaṃ. Tesaṃ, tesānaṃ, tāsaṃ, tāsānaṃ. Kesaṃ, kesānaṃ, kāsaṃ, kāsānaṃ. Imesaṃ, imesānaṃ, imāsaṃ, imāsānaṃ. Amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ.

**Nam** iti kimatthaṃ? Sabbassa, yassa, tassa, kassa. Evaṃ sabbattha.

---

After all \[the pronouns\], ‘naṃ’ \[is substituted by\] ‘saṃ’ and ‘sānaṃ’.

After all the pronouns, the inflection ‘naṃ’ is substituted by ‘saṃ’ and ‘sānaṃ’.

What is the purpose of saying ‘naṃ’? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘naṃ’, such as in:\] Sabbassa, yassa, tassa, kassa. Thus everywhere.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbesaṃ** \= sabba \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ becomes ‘saṃ’ (§168); ‘a’ of “sabba” is substituted by ‘e’ (§102)).  
2. **Sabbesānaṃ** \= sabba \+ naṃ (‘naṃ’ becomes ‘sānaṃ’ (§168); ‘a’ of “sabba” is substituted by ‘e’ (§102)).

---

**169\. 117\. Rājassa rāju su-naṃ-hisu ca (354).** \[Rājassa \+ rāju \+ su-naṃ-hisu \+ ca, 4\]

Sabbass’ eva **rāja**saddassa **rāju**-ādeso hoti **su naṃ hi** icc etesu. 

Rājūsu, rājūnaṃ, rājūhi, rājūbhi. 

**Su-naṃ-hi-sv** iti kimatthaṃ? Rājā. 

**Caggahaṇam** avadhāraṇatthaṃ. Rājesu, rājānaṃ, rājehi, rājebhi.

---

And when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow, \[there is\] “rāju” of “rāja”.

When the inflections ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow, the whole base “rāja” is substituted by “rāju”.

---

Example:

1. **Rājūsu** \= rāja \+ su (“rāja” is substituted by “rāju” (§169); ‘u’ is lengthened (§89)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ do not follow, such as in:\] Rājā.

The taking of “ca” is for restricting \[the scope of this sutta\]. Rājesu, rājānaṃ, rājehi, rājebhi.

---

**170\. 220\. Sabbass’ imass e vā (356).** \[Sabbassa \+ imassa \+ e \+ vā, 4\]

Sabbass’ eva **ima**saddassa **e**kāro hoti vā **su naṃ hi** icc etesu. 

Esu, imesu; esaṃ, imesaṃ; ehi, ebhi, imehi, imebhi. 

**Imasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Etesu, etesaṃ, etehi, etebhi.

---

Optionally, \[there is\] ‘e’ of the whole \[base\] “ima”.

Optionally, when the inflections ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘hi’ follow, the whole base “ima” becomes “e”.

---

Example:

1. **Esu** \= ima \+ su (“ima” becomes “e” (§170)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘ima’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “ima”, such as in:\] Etesu, etesaṃ, etehi, etebhi.

---

**171\. 219\. An’-imi nāmhi ca (357).** \[Ana-imi \+ nāmhi \+ ca, 3\]

**Ima**saddassa sabbass’ eva **ana imi** icc ete ādesā honti **nā**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Anena dhammadānena. Sukhitā hotu sā pajā. Iminā Buddhapūjena patvā amataṃ padaṃ. 

**Nā**mhī ti kimatthaṃ? Imesu, imesaṃ, imehi, imebhi.

---

And when ‘nā’ follows, \[there are\] ‘ana’ and ‘imi’.

When the inflection ‘nā’ follows, the whole base “ima” is substituted by “ana” or “imi”.

---

Examples:

1. **Anena** \= ima \+ nā (“ima” is substituted by “ana” (§171); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ena’ (§103); ‘a’ of “ana” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Iminā** \= ima \+ nā (“ima” is substituted by “imi” (§171)).

Examples in context:

1. *Anena dhammadānena. Sukhitā hotu sā pajā.* (By this gift of the Dhamma, may that community \[of beings\] be happy.)  
2. *Iminā Buddhapūjena patvā amataṃ padaṃ.* (Having reached the deathless state \[of Nibbāna\] by means of this veneration of the Buddha.)

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘nā’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘nā’ does not follow, such as in:\] Imesu, imesaṃ, imehi, imebhi.

---

**172\. 218\. Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi (306-7, 358).** \[Anapuṃsakassa \+ ayaṃ \+ simhi, 3\]

**Ima**saddassa sabbass’ eva anapuṃsakassa **ayaṃ**-ādeso hoti **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Ayaṃ puriso, ayaṃ itthī. 

**Anapuṃsakasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Idaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati. 

**Simhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Imaṃ purisaṃ passasi tvaṃ.

---

When ‘si’ follows, \[there is\] ‘ayaṃ’ of the non-neuter \[base\].

When the inflection ‘si’ follows, the whole base “ima”, which is not neuter, is substituted by “ayaṃ”.

---

Example:

1. **Ayaṃ** \= ima \+ si (“ima” is substituted by “ayaṃ” (§172); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

Examples in context:

1. **Ayaṃ puriso** (This man.)  
2. **Ayaṃ itthī** (This woman.)

What is the purpose of saying “of the non-neuter \[base\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when the \[base\] is neuter, such as in:\] Idaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati (This mind remains.)

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in:\] Imaṃ purisaṃ passasi tvaṃ (You see this man.)

---

**173\. 223\. Amussa mo saṃ (359).** \[Amussa \+ mo \+ saṃ, 3\]

**Amu**saddassa anapuṃsakassa **ma**kāro **sa**kāram āpajjate vā **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Asu (M. ii, 175) rājā; asu itthī; amuko rājā; amukā itthī. 

**Anapuṃsakasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Aduṃ (Khu. v, 34) pupphaṃ virocati. 

**Amusse** ti kimatthaṃ? Ayaṃ puriso tiṭṭhati. 

**Simhī** ti kimatthaṃ? Amhaṃ (M. i, 211) purisaṃ passasi.

---

‘M’ of “amu” \[becomes\] ‘s’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘si’ follows, ‘m’ of the base “amu”, which is not neuter, becomes ‘s’.

---

Example:

1. **Asu** \= amu \+ si (‘m’ of “amu” becomes ‘s’ (§173); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

Examples in context:

1. **Asu rājā** (Such a king.)  
2. **Asu itthī** (Such a woman.)  
3. **Amuko** rājā (Such a king.)  
4. **Amukā** itthī (Such a woman.)

What is the purpose of saying “of the non-neuter \[base\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when the \[base\] is neuter, such as in:\] Aduṃ pupphaṃ virocati (such a flower shines.)

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘amu’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “amu”, such as in:\] Ayaṃ puriso tiṭṭhati (This man remains.)

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in:\] Amhaṃ purisaṃ passasi (You see our man.)

---

**174\. 211\. Eta-tesaṃ to (360).** \[Eta-tesaṃ \+ to, 2\]

**Eta ta** icc etesaṃ anapuṃsakānaṃ **ta**kāro **sa**kāram āpajjate **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Eso puriso, esā itthī; so puriso, sā itthī. 

**Eta-tesam** iti kimatthaṃ? Itaro puriso, itarā itthī. 

**Anapuṃsakānam** iti kimatthaṃ? Etaṃ cittaṃ, etaṃ rūpaṃ; taṃ cittaṃ, taṃ rūpaṃ.

---

‘T’ of “eta” and “ta”.

When the inflection ‘si’ follows, ‘t’ of “eta” and “ta”, which are not neuter, becomes ‘s’.

---

Example:

1. **Eso** \= eta \+ si (‘t’ of “eta” becomes ‘s’ (§174); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of “esa” is elided (§83)).  
2. **So** \= ta \+ si (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘s’ (§174); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of “sa” is elided (§83)).

Examples in context:

1. **Eso puriso** (That man.)  
2. **Esā itthī** (That woman.)  
3. **So puriso** (That man.)  
4. **Sā itthī** (That woman.)

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘eta’ and ‘ta’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “eta” or “ta”, such as in:\] Itaro puriso, itarā itthī. (Another man, another woman.)

What is the purpose of saying “which are not neuter”? \[This sutta does not apply when they are neuter, such as in:\] Etaṃ cittaṃ, etaṃ rūpaṃ; taṃ cittaṃ, taṃ rūpaṃ. (That mind, that form; this mind, this form.)

---

**175\. 212\. Tassa vā nattaṃ sabbattha (361).** \[Tassa \+ vā \+ nattaṃ \+ sabbattha, 4\]

**Ta**ssa sabbanāmassa **ta**kārassa **na**ttaṃ hoti vā sabbattha liṅgesu. 

Nāya, tāya; naṃ (Khu. i, 308), taṃ; ne (DhA. i, 6), te; nesu, tesu; namhi, tamhi; nāhi, tāhi; nābhi, tābhi.

---

Optionally, everywhere, \[there is\] ‘n’ of ‘t’.

Optionally, in all the genders, the letter ‘t’ of the pronoun “ta” becomes ‘n’.

---

Example:

1. **Nāya** \= tā \+ nā (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘n’ (§175); ‘nā’ becomes ‘āya’ (§111); ‘ā’ of “nā” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Naṃ** \= ta \+ aṃ (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘n’ (§175); ‘a’ of ‘na’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Ne** \= ta \+ yo (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘n’ (§175); ‘yo’ becomes ‘e’ (§164); ‘a’ of “na” is elided (§83)).  
4. **Nesu** \= ta \+ su (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘n’ (§175); ‘a’ of “na” becomes ‘e’ (§101)).  
5. **Namhi** \= ta \+ smiṃ (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘n’ (§175); ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘mhi’ (§99)).  
6. **Nāhi** \= tā \+ hi (‘t’ of “tā” becomes ‘n’ (§175)).  
7. **Nābhi** \= tā \+ hi (‘t’ of “tā” becomes ‘n’ (§175); ‘hi’ becomes ‘bhi’ (§99)).

Parallel forms (with §175 applied / not applied):

1. Nāya, tāya  
2. Naṃ, taṃ  
3. Ne, te  
4. Nesu, tesu  
5. Namhi, tamhi  
6. Nāhi, tāhi  
7. Nābhi, tābhi

---

**176\. 213\. Sa-smā-smiṃ-saṃ-sāsv attaṃ (362, 368).** \[Sa-smā-smiṃ-saṃ-sāsu \+ attaṃ, 2\]

**Ta**ssa sabbanāmassa **ta**kārassa sabbass’ eva **a**ttaṃ hoti vā **sa smā smiṃ saṃ sā** icc etesu sabbattha liṅgesu. 

Assa, tassa; asmā, tasmā; asmiṃ, tasmiṃ; assaṃ, tassaṃ; assā, tassā. 

**Takāra**sse ti kimatthaṃ? Amussaṃ, amussā. 

**Etesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Nesu, tesu.

---

When ‘sa’, ‘smā’, ‘smiṃ’, ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’ follow, \[there is\] ‘a’.

Optionally, in all the genders, when the inflections ‘sa’, ‘smā’, ‘smiṃ’, ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’ follow, the whole \[base\] “ta” of the pronoun “ta” becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Assa** \= ta \+ sa (“ta” becomes “a” (§176); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).  
2. **Asmā** \= ta \+ smā (“ta” becomes “a” (§176)).  
3. **Asmiṃ** \= ta \+ smiṃ (“ta” becomes “a” (§176)).  
4. **Assaṃ** \= tā \+ smiṃ (‘ā’ of “tā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ of “tā” is shortened (§66); “ta” becomes “a” (§176)).  
5. **Assā** \= tā \+ sa (‘ā’ of “tā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘sa’ becomes ‘sā’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ of “tā” is shortened (§66); “ta” becomes “a” (§176)).

Parallel forms (with §176 applied / not applied):

1. Assa, tassa  
2. Asmā, tasmā  
3. Asmiṃ, tasmiṃ  
4. Assaṃ, tassaṃ  
5. Assā, tassā

What is the purpose of saying “the letter ‘t’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “t”, such as in:\] Amussaṃ, amussā.

What is the purpose of saying “when these \[inflections\] follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when these inflections do not follow, such as in:\] Nesu, tesu.

---

**177\. 221\. Imasaddassa ca (363).** \[Imasaddassa \+ ca, 2\]

**Ima**saddassa ca sabbass’ eva **a**ttaṃ hoti vā **sa smā smiṃ saṃ sā** icc etesu sabbattha liṅgesu. 

Assa, imassa; asmā, imasmā; asmiṃ, imasmiṃ; assaṃ, imissaṃ; assā, imissā. 

**Imasadda**sse ti kimatthaṃ? Etissaṃ, etissā.

---

And of the \[base\] “ima”.

Optionally, in all the genders, when the inflections ‘sa’, ‘smā’, ‘smiṃ’, ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’ follow, the whole \[base\] “ima” also becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Assa** \= ima \+ sa (“ima” becomes “a” (§177); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).  
2. **Asmā** \= ima \+ smā (“ima” becomes “a” (§177)).  
3. **Asmiṃ** \= ima \+ smiṃ (“ima” becomes “a” (§177)).  
4. **Assaṃ** \= imā \+ smiṃ (‘ā’ of “imā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); “ima” becomes “a” (§177)).  
5. **Assā** \= imā \+ sa (‘ā’ of “imā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘sa’ becomes ‘sā’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); “ima” becomes “a” (§177)).

Parallel forms (with §177 applied / not applied):

1. Assa, imassa  
2. Asmā, imasmā  
3. Asmiṃ, imasmiṃ  
4. Assaṃ, imissaṃ  
5. Assā, imissā

What is the purpose of saying “of the \[base\] ‘ima’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “ima”, such as in:\] Etissaṃ, etissā.

---

**178\. 224\. Sabbato ko (364).** \[Sabbato \+ ko, 2\]

Sabbato sabbanāmato **ka**kārāgamo hoti vā **si**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Sabbako, yako, sako, amuko, asuko (A. i, 73). 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Sabbo, yo, so, ko. 

**Sabbanāmato** ti kimatthaṃ? Puriso. 

Puna **sabbato**ggahaṇena aññasmā pi **ka**kārāgamo hoti. Hīnako, potako.

---

After all, ‘ka’ \[is inserted\].

Optionally, when the inflection ‘si’ follows, after all the pronouns, ‘ka’ is inserted.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbako** \= sabba \+ si (‘ka’ is inserted (§178); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of ‘ka’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Yako** \= ya \+ si (‘ka’ is inserted (§178); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of ‘ka’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Sako** \= sa \+ si (‘ka’ is inserted (§178); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of ‘ka’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Amuko** \= amu \+ si (‘ka’ is inserted (§178); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of ‘ka’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Asuko** \= amu \+ si (‘m’ of “amu” becomes ‘s’ (§173); ‘ka’ is inserted (§178); ‘si’ becomes ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ of ‘ka’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[To allow exceptions to this rule, such as in:\] Sabbo, yo, so, ko.

What is the purpose of saying “after the pronouns”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no pronouns, such as in:\] Puriso.

By taking “sabbato” again, ‘ka’ is also inserted in other \[places\]. Examples: Hīnako, potako.

---

**179\. 204\. Gha-pato smiṃ-sānaṃ saṃ-sā (365).** \[Gha-pato \+ smiṃ-sānaṃ \+ saṃ-sā, 3\]

Sabbato sabbanāmato **gha-pa**saññato **smiṃ sa** icc etesaṃ **saṃ-sā**-ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Sabbassaṃ, sabbassā; sabbāyaṃ, sabbāya. Imissaṃ, imissā (Abhi. ii, 254); imāyaṃ, imāya. Amussaṃ, amussā (M. iii, 10); amuyaṃ, amuyā. 

**Sabbanāmato** ti kimatthaṃ? Itthiyaṃ, itthiyā. 

**Smiṃ-sā**nam iti kimatthaṃ? Amuyo.

---

‘Saṃ’ and ‘sā’ of ‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’ after ‘gha’ and ‘pa’.

Optionally, after all the pronouns named ‘gha’ and ‘pa’, ‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’ become ‘saṃ’ and ‘sā’ respectively.

---

Example:

1. **Sabbassaṃ** \= sabbā \+ smiṃ (‘ā’ of “sabbā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ is shortened (§66)).

What is the purpose of saying “after the pronouns”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no pronouns, such as in:\] Itthiyaṃ, itthiyā.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘smiṃ’ or ‘sa’, such as in:\] Amuyo.

---

**180\. 207\. N’ etāhi smim āya-yā (369-70).** \[Na \+ etāhi \+ smim \+ āya-yā, 4\]

Etehi sabbanāmehi **gha-pa**saññehi **smiṃ**vacanassa n’ eva **āya-yā**desā honti. 

Etissaṃ, etāyaṃ; imissaṃ, imāyaṃ; amussaṃ, amuyaṃ. 

**Smin** ti kimatthaṃ? Tāya itthiyā mukhaṃ. 

**Etahī** ti kimatthaṃ? Kaññāya, vīṇāya, gaṅgāya, kapālikāya.

---

After these, ‘smiṃ’ does not \[become\] ‘āya’ or ‘yā’.

The inflection ‘smiṃ’, which follows the pronouns named ‘gha’ and ‘pa’, does not become ‘āya’ or ‘yā’.

---

Example:

1. **Etissaṃ** \= etā \+ smiṃ (‘ā’ of “etā” is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘smiṃ’ does not become ‘āya’ or ‘yā’ (§180); ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘saṃ’ (§179); ‘s’ is inserted (§62); ‘ā’ of “etā” becomes ‘i’ (§63)).

What is the purpose of saying “‘smiṃ’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘smiṃ’, such as in:\] Tāya itthiyā mukhaṃ. (The face of that woman.)

What is the purpose of saying “after these \[pronouns\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘smiṃ’ does not follow the pronouns, such as in:\] Kaññāya, vīṇāya, gaṅgāya, kapālikāya.

---

**181\. 95\. Manogaṇādito smiṃ-nānam i ā (373).** \[Manogaṇādito \+ smiṃ-nānaṃ \+ i-ā, 3\]

Tasmā manogaṇādito smiṃ nā icc etesaṃ ikāra-ākārādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Manasi (D. i, 12), manasmiṃ; sirasi, sirasmiṃ; manasā (Khu. i, 13), manena; vacasā (A. i, 504), vacena; sirasā (M. ii, 406), sirena; sarasā, sarena; tapasā (Khu. ii, 128), tapena; vayasā (D. ii, 125), vayena; yasasā (Khu. ii, 73), yasena; tejasā (Khu. ii, 135), tejena; urasā (M. ii, 409), urena; thāmasā, thāmena. 

**Smiṃ-nā**nam iti kimatthaṃ? Mano, siro, tamo, tapo, tejo. 

**Ādi**ggahaṇena aññāsmā pi **smiṃ-nā**naṃ **i**kāra-**ā**kārādesā honti. Bilasi, bilasā; padasi, padasā.

---

After the group “mano” and others, \[there are\] ‘i’ and ‘ā’ of ‘smiṃ’ and ‘nā’.

Optionally, after the group “mano” and others, the inflections ‘smiṃ’ and ‘nā’ are substituted by ‘i’ and ‘ā’ respectively.

---

Examples:

1. **Manasi** \= mana \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘i’ (§181); ‘s’ is inserted (§184)).  
2. **Manasā** \= mana \+ nā (‘nā’ becomes ‘ā’ (§181); ‘s’ is inserted (§184)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘smiṃ’ and ‘nā’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘smiṃ’ or ‘nā’, such as in:\] Mano, siro, tamo, tapo, tejo.

By taking “ādi”, also after other \[bases\], ‘smiṃ’ and ‘nā’ become ‘i’ and ‘ā’. Examples: Bilasi, bilasā; padasi, padasā.

---

**182\. 97\. Sassa c’ o (374).** \[Sassa \+ ca \+ o, 3\]

Tasmā **mano**gaṇādito **sa**ssa ca **o**kāro hoti. 

Manaso (Khu. ii, 14), thāmaso, tapaso.

---

And ‘o’ of ‘sa’.

After the group “mano” and others, the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by the letter ‘o’.

---

Example:

1. **Manaso** \= mana \+ sa (‘sa’ becomes ‘o’ (§182); ‘s’ is inserted (§184)).

---

**183\. 48\. Etesam o lope (375).** \[Etesaṃ \+ o \+ lope, 3\]

Etesaṃ **mano**gaṇādīnaṃ anto **o**ttam āpajjate vibhattilope kate. 

Manomayaṃ (D. i, 73), ayomayaṃ (Khu. i, 383), tejosamena, tapoguṇena, siroruhena. 

**Ādi**ggahaṇaṃ kimatthaṃ? Aññesam anto **o**ttam āpajjate. Āposamena, vāyosamena. 

**Lope** ti kimatthaṃ? Padasā, tapasā (Khu. ii, 121), yasasā (Khu. ii, 73), vacasā (A. i, 504), manasā (Khu. i, 13). Evaṃ aññe pi yojetabbā.

---

When there is elision, \[there is\] ‘o’ of these.

When the inflection is elided, the end \[vowel\] of the group “mano” and others becomes ‘o’.

This example will be given in Chapter 5.[^37]

What is the purpose of taking “ādi”? The end \[vowel\] of other words becomes ‘o’. Āposamena, vāyosamena.

What is the purpose of saying “when \[the inflection\] is elided”? \[This sutta does not apply when the inflection is not elided, such as in:\] Padasā, tapasā, yasasā, vacasā, manasā. Thus the others are also to be formed.

---

**184\. 96\. Sa sare v’ āgamo (376).** \[Sa \+ sare \+ vā \+ āgamo, 4\]

Eteh’ eva **mano**gaṇādīhi vibhattādese sare pare **sa**kārāgamo hoti vā.

Manasā, vacasā; manasi, vacasi.

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Manena, tejena, yasena.

**Sare** ti kimatthaṃ? Mano, tejo, yaso.

Puna **ādi**ggahaṇena aññasmim pi paccaye pare **s**akārāgamo hoti. Mānasikaṃ, vācasikaṃ (Abhi. ii, 255).

---

Optionally, when there is a vowel, \[there is\] insertion of ‘s’.

Optionally, after the group “mano” and others, when the vowel substitution of the inflection takes place, ‘s’ is inserted.

---

Examples:

1. **Manasā** \= mana \+ nā (‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§181); ‘s’ is inserted (§184)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[To allow exceptions to this sutta, such as in:\] Manena, tejena, yasena.

What is the purpose of saying “when there is a vowel”? \[This sutta does not apply when the vowel substitution of the inflection does not take place, such as in:\] Mano, tejo, yaso.[^38]

By taking “ādi” again, ‘s’ is also inserted when other suffixes follow. Mānasikaṃ, vācasikaṃ.

---

**185\. 112\. Santasaddassa so bhe bo c’ ante (378).** \[Santasaddassa \+ so \+ bhe \+ bo \+ ca \+ ante, 6\]

Sabbassa **santa**saddassa **sa**kārādeso hoti **bh**akāre pare, ante ca **b**akārāgamo hoti.

Sabbhir eva samāsetha,   
Sabbhi kubbetha santhavaṃ,   
Sataṃ saddhammam aññāya,  
Seyyo hoti na pāpiyo (S. i, 16). 

One should associate only with the good, one should cultivate intimacy with the good; having understood the true Dhamma of the good, one becomes better, not worse.

Jīranti ve rājaratha sucittā,   
Atho sarīram pi jaraṃ upeti,  
Satañ ca dhammo na jaraṃ upeti,   
Santo have sabbhi pavedayanti (Khu. i, 36).

The well-adorned chariots of kings wear out, and the body too comes to old age. But the Dhamma of the good does not come to old age; this the good proclaim among the good.[^39]

Sabbhūto (being good), sabbhāvo (state/condition of the good). 

**Bhe** ti kimatthaṃ? Santehi pūjito Bhagavā.

**Ca**ggahaṇaṃ kvaci **s**akārass’ eva pasiddhatthaṃ. Sakkāro, sakkato.

---

When ‘bh’ follows, \[there is\] ‘sa’ of the word “santa”, and ‘b’ at the end.

When ‘bh’ follows, the whole word “santa” is substituted by ‘sa’, and at the end ‘b’ is inserted.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbhi** \= santa \+ hi (‘hi’ is substituted by ‘bhi’ (§99); “santa” is substituted by ‘sa’ (§185); ‘b’ is inserted (§185)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘bh’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘bh’ does not follow, such as in:\] Santehi pūjito Bhagavā.

The taking of “ca” is, sometimes, for the substitution by ‘sa’ alone to take place.[^40] Sakkāro, sakkato.

---

**186\. 107\. Simhi gacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ (382-4).** \[Simhi \+ gacchanta-ādīnaṃ \+ nta-saddo \+ aṃ, 4\]

**Si**mhi **gacchantā**dīnaṃ **nta**saddo **am** āpajjate vā.

Gacchaṃ, gacchanto; mahaṃ, mahanto; caraṃ, caranto; khādaṃ, khādanto.

**Gacchantādīnam** iti kimatthaṃ? Anto, danto, vanto, santo.

---

When ‘si’ follows, the suffix ‘nta’ of “gacchanta” and others \[becomes\] ‘aṃ’.

Optionally, when \[the inflection\] ‘si’ follows, the suffix ‘nta’ of “gacchanta” and others becomes ‘aṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Gacchaṃ** \= gacchanta \+ si (‘nta’ becomes ‘aṃ’ (§186); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Gacchanto** \= gacchanta \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Mahaṃ** \= mahanta \+ si (‘nta’ becomes ‘aṃ’ (§186); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Mahanto** \= mahanta \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Caraṃ** \= caranta \+ si (‘nta’ becomes ‘aṃ’ (§186); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Caranto** \= caranta \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
7. **Khādaṃ** \= khādanta \+ si (‘nta’ becomes ‘aṃ’ (§186); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
8. **Khādanto** \= khādanta \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘gacchanta’ and others”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “gacchanta” and others, such as in:\] Anto, danto, vanto, santo.

---

**187\. 108\. Sesesu ntu ’va (385-8).** \[Sesesu \+ ntu \+ iva, 3\]

**Gacchantā**dīnaṃ **nta**saddo **ntu**ppaccayo ’va daṭṭhabbo sesesu vibhattippaccayesu.

Gacchato, mahato; gacchati, mahati, gacchatā, mahatā.

**Sesesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Gacchaṃ, mahaṃ, caraṃ, khādaṃ.

---

When the remaining \[inflections\] follow, like ‘ntu’.

When the remaining inflections follow, the suffix ‘nta’ of “gacchanta” and others is to be regarded as ‘ntu’.

---

Examples:

1. **Gacchato** \= gacchanta \+ sa (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘to’ (§127)).  
2. **Mahato** \= mahanta \+ sa (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘to’ (§127)).  
3. **Gacchati** \= gacchanta \+ smiṃ (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ti’ (§127)).  
4. **Mahati** \= mahanta \+ smiṃ (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ti’ (§127)).  
5. **Gacchatā** \= gacchanta \+ nā (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).  
6. **Mahatā** \= mahanta \+ nā (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).  
7. **Carato** \= caranta \+ sa (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘to’ (§127)).  
8. **Carati** \= caranta \+ smiṃ (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ti’ (§127)).  
9. **Caratā** \= caranta \+ nā (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).  
10. **Khādato** \= khādanta \+ sa (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘to’ (§127)).  
11. **Khādati** \= khādanta \+ smiṃ (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ti’ (§127)).  
12. **Khādatā** \= khādanta \+ nā (‘nta’ is regarded as ‘ntu’ (§187); ‘ntu’ together with the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the remaining \[inflections\] follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ follows, such as in:\] Gacchaṃ, mahaṃ, caraṃ, khādaṃ.

---

**188\. 115\. Brahm’-atta-sakha-rājādito am ānaṃ (393).** \[Brahma-atta-sakha-rāja-ādito \+ aṃ \+ ānaṃ, 3\]

**Brahma atta sakha rāja** icc evamādito **aṃ**vacanassa **ānaṃ** hoti vā. 

Brahmānaṃ, brahmaṃ; attānaṃ, attaṃ; sakhānaṃ, sakhaṃ; rājānaṃ, rājaṃ. 

**Am** iti kimatthaṃ? Rājā.

---

‘Aṃ’ of “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha”, “rāja”, etc., \[becomes\] ‘ānaṃ’.

Optionally, after “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha”, “rāja”, etc., the inflection ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘ānaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Brahmānaṃ** \= brahma \+ aṃ (‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘ānaṃ’ (§188); ‘a’ of “brahma” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Brahmaṃ** \= brahma \+ aṃ (‘a’ of “brahma” is elided (§83)).  
3. **Attānaṃ** \= atta \+ aṃ (‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘ānaṃ’ (§188); ‘a’ of “atta” is elided (§83)).  
4. **Attaṃ** \= atta \+ aṃ (‘a’ of “atta” is elided (§83)).  
5. **Sakhānaṃ** \= sakha \+ aṃ (‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘ānaṃ’ (§188); ‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).  
6. **Sakhaṃ** \= sakha \+ aṃ (‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).  
7. **Rājānaṃ** \= rāja \+ aṃ (‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘ānaṃ’ (§188); ‘a’ of “rāja” is elided (§83)).  
8. **Rājaṃ** \= rāja \+ aṃ (‘a’ of “rāja” is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying ‘aṃ’? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘aṃ’ does not follow, such as in:\] rājā.

---

**189\. 113\. Sy ā ca (390-1).** \[Si \+ ā \+ ca, 3\]

**Brahma atta sakha rāja** icc evamādito **si**vacanassa **ā** ca hoti. 

Brahmā, attā, sakhā, rājā, ātumā.

---

And ‘si’ \[becomes\] ‘ā’.

And after “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha”, “rāja”, etc., the inflection ‘si’ becomes ‘ā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Brahmā** \= brahma \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§189); ‘a’ of “brahma” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Attā** \= atta \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§189); ‘a’ of “atta” is elided (§83)).  
3. **Sakhā** \= sakha \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§189); ‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).  
4. **Rājā** \= rāja \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§189); ‘a’ of “rāja” is elided (§83)).  
5. **Ātumā** \= ātuma \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§189); ‘a’ of “ātuma” is elided (§83)).

---

**190\. 114\. Yonam āno (392).** \[Yonaṃ \+ āno, 2\]

**Brahma atta sakha rāja** icc evamādito **yo**naṃ **āno**-ādeso hoti. 

Brahmāno, attāno, sakhāno, rājāno, ātumāno.

---

‘Āno’ of ‘yo’.

After “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha”, “rāja”, etc., the inflection ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’.

---

Examples:

1. **Brahmāno** \= brahma \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§190); ‘a’ of “brahma” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Attāno** \= atta \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§190); ‘a’ of “atta” is elided (§83)).  
3. **Sakhāno** \= sakha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§190); ‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).  
4. **Rājāno** \= rāja \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§190); ‘a’ of “rāja” is elided (§83)).  
5. **Ātumāno** \= ātuma \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āno’ (§190); ‘a’ of “ātuma” is elided (§83)).

---

**191\. 130\. Sakhato[^41] c’ āyo no (394).** \[Sakhato \+ ca \+ āyo-no, 3\]

Tasmā **sakha**to ca **yo**naṃ **āyo**-**no**-ādesā honti. 

Sakhāyo, sakhino. **Yonam** iti kimatthaṃ? Sakhā.

---

And after “sakha”, \[there are\] ‘āyo’ and ‘no’.

And after “sakha”, \[the inflection\] ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āyo’ and ‘no’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sakhāyo** \= sakha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘āyo’ (§191); ‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).  
2. **Sakhino** \= sakha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§191); ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘yo’”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘yo’ does not follow, such as in:\] sakhā.

---

**192\. 135\. Smim e.** \[Smiṃ \+ e, 2\]

Tasmā **sakha**to **smiṃ**vacanassa **e**kāro hoti. 

Sakhe.

---

‘Smiṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘e’.

After “sakha”, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ becomes the letter ‘e’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sakhe** \= sakha \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§192); ‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).

---

**193\. 122\. Brahmato gassa ca (287).** \[Brahmato \+ gassa \+ ca, 3\]

Tasmā **brahma**to **ga**ssa ca **e**kāro hoti. 

He brahme.[^42]

---

And after “brahma”, \[there is ‘e’\] of ‘ga’.

And after “brahma”, ‘ga’ becomes ‘e’.

---

Examples:

1. **Brahme** \= brahma \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ga’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§193); ‘a’ of “brahma” is elided (§83)).

---

**194\. 131\. Sakhantass’ i no-nā-naṃ-sesu (407).** \[Sakha-antassa \+ i \+ no-nā-naṃ-sesu, 3\]

Tassa **sakha**ntassa **i**kāro hoti **no nā naṃ sa** icc etesu. 

Sakhino, sakhinā, sakhīnaṃ, sakhissa. 

**Etesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Sakhārehi.

---

When ‘no’, ‘nā’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘sa’ follow, \[there is\] ‘i’ of the end of “sakha”.

When \[the inflections\] ‘no’, ‘nā’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘sa’ follow, the end \[vowel\] of “sakha” becomes the letter ‘i’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sakhino** \= sakha \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§191); the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194)).  
2. **Sakhinā** \= sakha \+ nā (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194)).  
3. **Sakhīnaṃ** \= sakha \+ naṃ (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194); ‘i’ is lengthened (§89)).  
4. **Sakhissa** \= sakha \+ sa (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).

What is the purpose of saying “when the \[inflections ‘no’, ‘nā’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘sa’\] follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when these inflections do not follow, such as in:\] sakhārehi.

---

**195\. 134\. Āro himhi vā (408).** \[Āro \+ himhi \+ vā, 3\]

Tassa **sakha**ntassa **āro** hoti vā **hi**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Sakhārehi, sakhehi.

---

Optionally, when ‘hi’ follows, \[there is\] ‘āra’.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘hi’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of “sakha” becomes ‘āra’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sakhārehi** \= sakha \+ hi (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘āra’ (§195); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
2. **Sakhehi** \= sakha \+ hi (‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).

---

**196\. 133\. Su-nam-aṃsu vā (409).** \[Su-naṃ-aṃsu \+ vā, 2\]

Tassa **sakha**ntassa **āro** hoti vā **su naṃ aṃ** icc etesu. 

Sakhāresu, sakhesu; sakhārānaṃ, sakhīnaṃ; sakhāraṃ, sakhaṃ.

---

Optionally, when ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘aṃ’ follow.

Optionally, when \[the inflections\] ‘su’, ‘naṃ’ and ‘aṃ’ follow, the end \[vowel\] of “sakha” becomes ‘āra’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sakhāresu** \= sakha \+ su (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘āra’ (§196); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
2. **Sakhesu** \= sakha \+ su (‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
3. **Sakhārānaṃ** \= sakha \+ naṃ (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘āra’ (§196); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is lengthened (§89)).  
4. **Sakhīnaṃ** \= sakha \+ naṃ (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194); ‘i’ is lengthened (§89)).  
5. **Sakhāraṃ** \= sakha \+ aṃ (the end ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘āra’ (§196); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Sakhaṃ** \= sakha \+ aṃ (‘a’ of “sakha” is elided (§83)).

---

**197\. 125\. Brahmato tu smiṃ ni (405).** \[Brahmato \+ tu \+ smiṃ \+ ni, 4\]

Tasmā **brahma**to **smiṃ**vacanassa **ni**-ādeso hoti. 

Brahmani. 

**Tu**ggahaṇena abrahmato pi **smiṃ**vacanassa **ni** hoti. Kammani, cammani, muddhani.

---

After “brahma”, ‘smiṃ’ \[is substituted by\] ‘ni’.

After “brahma”, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’.

---

Examples:

1. **Brahmani** \= brahma \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§197)).

By taking “tu”, also after words other than “brahma”, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘ni’.

Examples:

1. **Kammani** \= kamma \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (“tu” in §197)).  
2. **Cammani** \= camma \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (“tu” in §197)).  
3. **Muddhani** \= muddha \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (“tu” in §197)).

---

**198\. 123\. Uttaṃ sa-nāsu (410).** \[Uttaṃ \+ sa-nāsu, 2\]

Tassa **brahma** saddassa anto **u**ttam āpajjate **sa nā** icc etesu. 

Brahmuno, brahmunā. 

**Sa-nāsv** iti kimatthaṃ? Brahmā.[^43]

---

When ‘sa’ and ‘nā’ follow, \[there is\] ‘u’.

When \[the inflections\] ‘sa’ and ‘nā’ follow, the end \[vowel\] of the word “brahma” becomes ‘u’.

---

Examples:

1. **Brahmuno** \= brahma \+ sa (‘a’ of “brahma” becomes ‘u’ (§198); ‘u’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).  
2. **Brahmunā** \= brahma \+ nā (‘a’ of “brahma” becomes ‘u’ (§198)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘sa’ and ‘nā’ follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘sa’ or ‘nā’ do not follow, such as in:\] brahmā.

---

**199\. 158\. Satthu-pitādīnam ā sismiṃ silopo ca (411).** \[Satthu-pitu-ādīnaṃ \+ ā \+ sismiṃ \+ silopo \+ ca, 5\]

**Satthu-pitu**-ādīnam anto **ā**ttam āpajjate **si**smiṃ, **si**lopo ca hoti. 

Satthā, pitā, mātā, bhātā, kattā. 

**Sismim** iti kimatthaṃ? Satthussa, pitussa, mātussa, bhātussa, kattussa.

---

And when ‘si’ follows, \[there is\] ‘ā’ of “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., and the elision of ‘si’.

When \[the inflection\] ‘si’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., becomes ‘ā’; and ‘si’ is elided.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthā** \= satthu \+ si (‘u’ becomes ‘ā’ (§199); ‘si’ is elided (§199)).  
2. **Pitā** \= pitu \+ si (‘u’ becomes ‘ā’ (§199); ‘si’ is elided (§199)).  
3. **Mātā** \= mātu \+ si (‘u’ becomes ‘ā’ (§199); ‘si’ is elided (§199)).  
4. **Bhātā** \= bhātu \+ si (‘u’ becomes ‘ā’ (§199); ‘si’ is elided (§199)).  
5. **Kattā** \= kattu \+ si (‘u’ becomes ‘ā’ (§199); ‘si’ is elided (§199)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘si’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘si’ does not follow, such as in:\] satthussa, pitussa, mātussa, bhātussa, kattussa.

---

**200\. 159\. Aññesv ārattaṃ (412).** \[Aññesu \+ ārattaṃ, 2\]

**Satthu-pitu**-ādīnam anto aññesu vacanesu **āra**ttam āpajjate. 

Satthāraṃ, pitaraṃ, mātaraṃ, bhātaraṃ, kattāraṃ, satthārehi, pitarehi, mātarehi, bhātarehi, kattārehi. 

**Aññesv** iti kimatthaṃ? Satthā, pitā, mātā, bhātā, kattā.[^44]

---

When others follow, \[there is\] ‘āra’.

When other inflections follow, the end \[vowel\] of “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., becomes ‘āra’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthāraṃ** \= satthu \+ aṃ (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Pitaraṃ** \= pitu \+ aṃ (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Mātaraṃ** \= mātu \+ aṃ (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Bhātaraṃ** \= bhātu \+ aṃ (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Kattāraṃ** \= kattu \+ aṃ (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Satthārehi** \= satthu \+ hi (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
7. **Pitarehi** \= pitu \+ hi (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
8. **Mātarehi** \= mātu \+ hi (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
9. **Bhātarehi** \= bhātu \+ hi (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
10. **Kattārehi** \= kattu \+ hi (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).

What is the purpose of saying “when other \[inflections\] follow”? \[This sutta does not apply when other inflections do not follow, such as in:\] satthā, pitā, mātā, bhātā, kattā.

---

**201\. 163\. Vā naṃmhi (416).** \[Vā \+ naṃmhi, 2\]

**Satthu-pitu**-ādīnam anto **āra**ttam āpajjate vā **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Satthārānaṃ, pitarānaṃ, mātarānaṃ, bhātarānaṃ. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Satthānaṃ, pitūnaṃ, mātūnaṃ, bhātūnaṃ.

---

Optionally, when ‘naṃ’ follows.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., becomes ‘āra’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthārānaṃ** \= satthu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§201); ‘a’ of ‘āra’ is lengthened (§89)).  
2. **Pitarānaṃ** \= pitu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§201); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).  
3. **Mātarānaṃ** \= mātu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§201); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).  
4. **Bhātarānaṃ** \= bhātu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§201); ‘ā’ of ‘āra’ is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[To allow exceptions to this sutta, such as in:\] satthānaṃ, pitūnaṃ, mātūnaṃ, bhātūnaṃ.

---

**202\. 164\. Satthun’ attañ ca (417).** \[Satthunaṃ \+ attaṃ \+ ca, 3\]

Tassa **satthu**saddassa anto **a**ttam āpajjate vā **naṃ**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Satthānaṃ, pitānaṃ, mātānaṃ, bhātānaṃ, kattānaṃ. 

**Vā** ti kimatthaṃ? Satthārānaṃ, pitarānaṃ, mātarānaṃ, bhātarānaṃ, dhītarānaṃ. 

**Ca**ggahaṇam aññesam pi saṅgahaṇatthaṃ.

---

And ‘a’ of “satthu”.

Optionally, when the inflection ‘naṃ’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of the word “satthu” becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthānaṃ** \= satthu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘a’ (§202); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).  
2. **Pitānaṃ** \= pitu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘a’ (“ca” of §202); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).  
3. **Mātānaṃ** \= mātu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘a’ (“ca” of §202); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).  
4. **Bhātānaṃ** \= bhātu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘a’ (“ca” of §202); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).  
5. **Kattānaṃ** \= kattu \+ naṃ (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘a’ (“ca” of §202); ‘a’ is lengthened (§89)).

What is the purpose of saying “optionally”? \[To allow exceptions to this sutta, such as in:\] satthārānaṃ, pitarānaṃ, mātarānaṃ, bhātarānaṃ, dhītarānaṃ.

The taking of “ca” is for including the others too.

---

**203\. 162\. U sasmiṃ salopo ca (418).** \[U \+ sasmiṃ \+ salopo \+ ca, 4\]

**Satthu pitu** icc evamādīnam antassa **u**ttaṃ hoti vā **sa**smiṃ **sa**lopo ca. 

Satthu, satthussa, satthuno dīyate, pariggaho vā. Pitu, pitussa, pituno dīyate, pariggaho vā. Bhātu, bhātussa, bhātuno dīyate, pariggaho vā. **Ca**ggahaṇaṃ dutiyasampiṇḍanatthaṃ.

---

When ‘sa’ follows, \[there is\] ‘u’ and the elision of ‘sa’.

Optionally, when \[the inflection\] ‘sa’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., becomes ‘u’ and ‘sa’ is elided.

---

Examples:

1. Satthu, satthussa, satthuno dīyate, pariggaho vā (it is given to the teacher, or \[it is\] the possession of the teacher):

  * **Satthu** \= satthu \+ sa (‘u’ of “satthu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘sa’ is elided (§203)).  
  * **Satthussa** \= satthu \+ sa (‘u’ of “satthu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).  
  * **Satthuno** \= satthu \+ sa (‘u’ of “satthu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).

1. Pitu, pitussa, pituno dīyate, pariggaho vā (it is given to the father, or \[it is\] the possession of the father):

  * **Pitu** \= pitu \+ sa (‘u’ of “pitu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘sa’ is elided (§203)).  
  * **Pitussa** \= pitu \+ sa (‘u’ of “pitu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).  
  * **Pituno** \= pitu \+ sa (‘u’ of “pitu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).

1. Bhātu, bhātussa, bhātuno dīyate, pariggaho vā (it is given to the brother, or \[it is\] the possession of the brother):  
  * **Bhātu** \= bhātu \+ sa (‘u’ of “bhātu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘sa’ is elided (§203)).  
  * **Bhātussa** \= bhātu \+ sa (‘u’ of “bhātu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘s’ is inserted (§61)).  
  * **Bhātuno** \= bhātu \+ sa (‘u’ of “bhātu” is substituted by ‘u’ (§203); ‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§117)).

The taking of “ca” is for the inclusion of the second \[meaning\].

---

**204\. 167\. Sakkamandhātādīnañ ca (419).** \[Sakkamandhātu-ādīnaṃ \+ ca, 2\]

**Sakkamandhātu** icc evamādīnam anto **u**ttam āpajjate **sa**smiṃ, **sa**lopo ca hoti. 

Sakkamandhātu iva assa rājino vibhavo. Evaṃ kattu, gantu, dātu icc evamādī. 

**Pun’ ārambhaggahaṇam** kimatthaṃ? Niccadīpanatthaṃ. Sakkamandhātu. 

**Ca**ggahaṇaṃ dutiyasampiṇḍanatthaṃ.

---

And of “sakkamandhātu”, etc.

When \[the inflection\] ‘sa’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of “sakkamandhātu”, etc., becomes ‘u’; and ‘sa’ is elided.

---

Examples:

1. Sakkamandhātu iva assa rājino vibhavo (the wealth of that king is like that \[of king\] Sakkamandhātu):

  * **Sakkamandhātu** \= sakkamandhātu \+ sa (‘u’ of “sakkamandhātu” becomes ‘u’ (§204); ‘sa’ is elided (§204)).

1. Evaṃ kattu, gantu, dātu icc evamādī (thus also kattu, gantu, dātu, etc.):

  * **Kattu** \= kattu \+ sa (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘u’ (§204); ‘sa’ is elided (§204)).  
  * **Gantu** \= gantu \+ sa (‘u’ of “gantu” becomes ‘u’ (§204); ‘sa’ is elided (§204)).  
  * **Dātu** \= dātu \+ sa (‘u’ of “dātu” becomes ‘u’ (§204); ‘sa’ is elided (§204)).

What is the purpose of the repetition of this sutta? To fix the change of ‘u’ into ‘u’.[^45] Sakkamandhātu.

The taking of “ca” is for the inclusion of the second \[meaning\].

---

**205\. 160\. Tato yonam o tu (421).** \[Tato \+ yonaṃ \+ o \+ tu, 4\]

Tato **ārā**desato sabbesaṃ **yo**naṃ **o**kārādeso hoti. 

Satthāro, pitaro, mātaro, bhātaro, kattāro, vattāro. 

**Tu**ggahaṇena aññasmā pi **yo**naṃ **o**kāro hoti. Caturo janā (Khu. i, 336), gāvo, ubho, purisā.

---

After \[the substitution ‘āra’\], there is ‘o’ of ‘yo’.

After the substitution ‘āra’, all the \[inflections\] ‘yo’ are substituted by ‘o’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthāro** \= satthu \+ yo (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Pitaro** \= pitu \+ yo (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Mātaro** \= mātu \+ yo (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Bhātaro** \= bhātu \+ yo (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Kattāro** \= kattu \+ yo (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Vattāro** \= vattu \+ yo (‘u’ of “vattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

By taking “tu”, also after other words ‘yo’ becomes ‘o’. Caturo janā, gāvo, ubho, purisā.[^46]

---

**206\. 165\. Tato smim i (422).** \[Tato \+ smiṃ \+ i, 3\]

Tato **ārā**desato **smiṃ**vacanassa **i**kārādeso hoti. 

Satthari, pitari, mātari, dhītari, bhātari, kattari, vattari. 

Puna **tato**gahaṇena aññasmā pi **smiṃ**vacanassa **i**kāro hoti. Bhuvi.

---

After that, ‘smiṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘i’.

After the substitution ‘āra’, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthari** \= satthu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Pitari** \= pitu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Mātari** \= mātu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Dhītari** \= dhītu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “dhītu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Bhātari** \= bhātu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Kattari** \= kattu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
7. **Vattari** \= vattu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “vattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

By taking “tato” again, also after other words the inflection ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘i’. Bhuvi.

---

**207\. 161\. Nā ā (423).** \[Nā \+ ā, 2\]

Tato **ārā**desato **nā**vacanassa **ā**-ādeso hoti. 

Satthārā, pitarā, mātarā, bhātarā, dhītarā, kattārā, vattārā.

---
