‘Nā’ \[becomes\] ‘ā’.

After the substitution ‘āra’, the inflection ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthārā** \= satthu \+ nā (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Pitarā** \= pitu \+ nā (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Mātarā** \= mātu \+ nā (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Bhātarā** \= bhātu \+ nā (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Dhītarā** \= dhītu \+ nā (‘u’ of “dhītu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Kattārā** \= kattu \+ nā (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
7. **Vattārā** \= vattu \+ nā (‘u’ of “vattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**208\. 166\. Āro rassam ikāre (424).** \[Āro \+ rassaṃ \+ ikāre, 3\]

**Ārā**deso rassam āpajjate **i**kāre pare. 

Satthari, pitari, mātari, dhītari, kattari, vattari.

---

When ‘i’ follows, ‘āra’ \[becomes\] short.

When ‘i’ follows, the substitution ‘āra’ is shortened.

---

Examples:

1. **Satthari** \= satthu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Pitari** \= pitu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Mātari** \= mātu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Dhītari** \= dhītu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “dhītu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Kattari** \= kattu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “kattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Vattari** \= vattu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ of “vattu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘i’ (§206); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§208); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**209\. 168\. Pitādīnam asimhi (425).** \[Pitu-ādīnaṃ \+ asimhi, 2\]

**Pitā**dīnam **ārā**deso rassam āpajjate **asi**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Pitarā, mātarā, bhātarā, dhītarā, pitaro, mātaro, bhātaro, dhītaro. 

**Asimhi**ggahaṇam **to**mhi pare **i**kārādesañāpanatthaṃ. Mātito (D. i, 106), pitito (D. i, 106), bhātito, duhitito.

---

When \[an inflection\] other than ‘si’ follows, of “pitu”, etc.

When an inflection other than ‘si’ follows, the substitution ‘āra’ of “pitu”, etc., is shortened.

---

Examples:

1. **Pitarā** \= pitu \+ nā (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Mātarā** \= mātu \+ nā (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Bhātarā** \= bhātu \+ nā (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Dhītarā** \= dhītu \+ nā (‘u’ of “dhītu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Pitaro** \= pitu \+ yo (‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Mātaro** \= mātu \+ yo (‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
7. **Bhātaro** \= bhātu \+ yo (‘u’ of “bhātu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
8. **Dhītaro** \= dhītu \+ yo (‘u’ of “dhītu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§205); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

The taking of “asimhi” is for showing the substitution ‘i’ when ‘to’ follows. 

Examples:

1. **Mātito** \= mātu \+ to (‘u’ of “mātu” is substituted by ‘i’ (“asimhi” in §209)).  
2. **Pitito** \= pitu \+ to (‘u’ of “pitu” is substituted by ‘i’ (“asimhi” in §209)).  
3. **Bhātito** \= bhātu \+ to (‘u’ of “bhātu” is substituted by ‘i’ (“asimhi” in §209)).  
4. **Duhitito** \= duhitu \+ to (‘u’ of “duhitu” is substituted by ‘i’ (“asimhi” in §209)).

---

**210\. 239\. Tayā-tayīnaṃ takāro tvattaṃ vā (435).** \[Tayā-tayīnaṃ \+ takāro \+ tvattaṃ \+ vā, 4\]

**Tayā tayi** icc etesaṃ **ta**kāro **tva**ttam āpajjate vā. 

Tvayā, tayā; tvayi, tayi. 

**Etesam** iti kimatthaṃ? Tuvaṃ, tavaṃ.

---

Optionally, the letter ‘ta’ of “tayā” and “tayi” \[becomes\] ‘tva’.

Optionally, the letter ‘ta’ of \[the substitutions\] “tayā” and “tayi” becomes ‘tva’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tvayā** \= tumha \+ nā (“tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayā” (§145); ‘ta’ becomes ‘tva’ (§210)).  
2. **Tayā** \= tumha \+ nā (“tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayā” (§145)).  
3. **Tvayi** \= tumha \+ smiṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayi” (§139); ‘ta’ becomes ‘tva’ (§210)).  
4. **Tayi** \= tumha \+ smiṃ (“tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayi” (§139)).

What is the purpose of saying “of these \[substitutions\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “tayā” or “tayi”, such as in:\] tuvaṃ, tavaṃ.

**Iti nāma-kappe tatiyo kaṇḍo**

**Thus ends the third section of the noun chapter**

**CATUTTHA-KAṆḌA**  
**FOURTH SECTION**

---

**211\. 126\. Attanto hismim anattaṃ (439).** \[Atta-anto \+ hismiṃ \+ anattaṃ, 3\]

Tassa **atta**no anto **ana**ttam āpajjate **hi**mhi vibhattimhi. 

Attanehi, attanebhi. 

**Attanto** ti kimatthaṃ? Rājehi, rājebhi. 

**Hismin** iti kimatthaṃ? Attano**.** 

**Anattam** iti bhāvaniddesena **atta**saddassa **sakā**deso hoti sabbāsu vibhattīsu. Sako, sakā, sakaṃ, sake.

---

When ‘hi’ follows, \[there is\] ‘ana’ of the end of “atta”.

When the inflection ‘hi’ follows, the end \[vowel\] of “atta” becomes ‘ana’.

---

Examples:

1. **Attanehi** \= atta \+ hi (‘a’ of “atta” becomes ‘ana’ (§211); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101)).  
2. **Attanebhi** \= atta \+ hi (‘a’ of “atta” becomes ‘ana’ (§211); ‘a’ is substituted by ‘e’ (§101); ‘hi’ is substituted by ‘bhi’ (§99)).

What is the purpose of saying “the end of ‘atta’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “atta”, such as in:\] rājehi, rājebhi.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘hi’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘hi’ does not follow, such as in:\] attano.[^47]

By the expression that indicates a state, “anattaṃ”, the word “atta” is substituted by “saka” when all the inflections follow. Sako, sakā, sakaṃ, sake.[^48]

---

**212\. 129\. Tato smiṃ ni (405).** \[Tato \+ smiṃ \+ ni, 3\]

Tato **atta**to **smiṃ**vacanassa **ni** hoti. Attani.

---

After this, ‘smiṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘ni’.

After “atta”, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘ni’.

---

Examples:

1. **Attani** \= atta \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§212)).

---

**213\. 127\. Sassa no (440).** \[Sassa \+ no, 2\]

Tato attato sassa vibhattissa no hoti. Attano.

---

‘No’ of ‘sa’.

After “atta”, the inflection ‘sa’ becomes ‘no’.

---

Examples:

1. **Attano** \= atta \+ sa (‘sa’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§213)).

---

**214\. 128\. Smā nā (441).** \[Smā \+ nā, 2\]

Tato **atta**to **smā**vacanassa **nā** hoti. 

Attanā. 

Puna **tato**gahaṇena tassa **atta**no **ta**kārass’ eva **ra**kāro hoti sabbesu vacanesu. Atrajo, atrajaṃ.

---

‘Smā’ \[becomes\] ‘nā’.

After “atta”, the inflection ‘smā’ becomes ‘nā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Attanā** \= atta \+ smā (‘smā’ is substituted by ‘nā’ (§214)).

By taking “tato” again, ‘ta’ of “atta” becomes ‘ra’ when all the inflections follow. 

Examples:

1. **Atrajo** \= atta \+ jo (‘ta’ of “atta” is substituted by ‘ra’ (“tato” in §214)).  
2. **Atrajaṃ** \= atta \+ jaṃ (‘ta’ of “atta” is substituted by ‘ra’ (“tato” in §214)).

---

**215\. 141\. Jha-lato ca (442).**[^49] \[Jha-lato \+ ca, 2\]

**Jha la** icc etehi **smā**vacanassa **nā** hoti. 

Agginā, daṇḍinā, bhikkhunā, sayambhunā. 

**Smā** ti kimatthaṃ? Aggayo, munayo, isayo.

---

And after ‘jha’ and ‘la’.

After ‘jha’ and ‘la’, the inflection ‘smā’ becomes ‘nā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Agginā** \= aggi \+ smā (‘i’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘nā’ (§215)).  
2. **Daṇḍinā** \= daṇḍī \+ smā (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘nā’ (§215)).  
3. **Bhikkhunā** \= bhikkhu \+ smā (‘u’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘nā’ (§215)).  
4. **Sayambhunā** \= sayambhū \+ smā (‘ū’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘ū’ is shortened (§84); ‘smā’ is substituted by ‘nā’ (§215)).

What is the purpose of saying ‘smā’? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘smā’ does not follow, such as in:\] aggayo, munayo, isayo.

---

**216\. 180\. Gha-pato smiṃ yaṃ vā (443).** \[Gha-pato \+ smiṃ \+ yaṃ \+ vā, 4\]

Tasmā **gha-pa**to **smiṃ**vacanassa **yaṃ** hoti vā. 

Kaññāyaṃ, kaññāya; rattiyaṃ, rattiyā; itthiyaṃ, itthiyā; yāguyaṃ, yāguyā; vadhuyaṃ, vadhuyā.

---

Optionally, after ‘gha’ and ‘pa’, ‘smiṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘yaṃ’.

Optionally, after ‘gha’ and ‘pa’, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ becomes ‘yaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Kaññāyaṃ** \= kaññā \+ smiṃ (‘ā’ is named ‘gha’ (§60); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yaṃ’ (§216)).  
2. **Kaññāya** \= kaññā \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘āya’ (§111); ‘ā’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Rattiyaṃ** \= ratti \+ smiṃ (‘i’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yaṃ’ (§216)).  
4. **Rattiyā** \= ratti \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yā’ (§112)).  
5. **Itthiyaṃ** \= itthī \+ smiṃ (‘ī’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yaṃ’ (§216)).  
6. **Itthiyā** \= itthī \+ smiṃ (‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yā’ (§112)).  
7. **Yāguyaṃ** \= yāgu \+ smiṃ (‘u’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yaṃ’ (§216)).  
8. **Yāguyā** \= yāgu \+ smiṃ (‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yā’ (§112)).  
9. **Vadhuyaṃ** \= vadhū \+ smiṃ (‘ū’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘ū’ is shortened (§84); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yaṃ’ (§216)).  
10. **Vadhuyā** \= vadhū \+ smiṃ (‘ū’ is shortened (§84); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘yā’ (§112)).

---

**217\. 199\. Yonaṃ ni napuṃsakehi (444).** \[Yonaṃ \+ ni \+ napuṃsakehi, 3\]

Sabbesaṃ **yo**naṃ **ni** hoti vā napuṃsakehi liṅgehi. 

Aṭṭhīni, aṭṭhī; āyūni, āyū. **Napuṃsakehī** ti kimatthaṃ? Itthiyo.

---

After the neuter bases, \[there is\] ‘ni’ of ‘yo’.

Optionally, after the neuter bases, all the ‘yo’ become ‘ni’.

---

Examples:

1. **Aṭṭhīni** \= aṭṭhi \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§217); ‘i’ is lengthened (§88)).  
2. **Aṭṭhī** \= aṭṭhi \+ yo (‘yo’ is elided (§118); ‘i’ is lengthened (§88)).  
3. **Āyūni** \= āyu \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§217); ‘u’ is lengthened (§88)).  
4. **Āyū** \= āyu \+ yo (‘yo’ is elided (§118); ‘u’ is lengthened (§88)).

What is the purpose of saying “after the neuter \[bases\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no neuter bases, such as in:\] itthiyo.

---

**218\. 196\. Ato niccaṃ (445).** \[Ato \+ niccaṃ, 2\]

**A**kārantehi napuṃsakaliṅgehi **yo**naṃ **ni** hoti niccaṃ. 

Yāni, yāni; tāni, tāni; kāni, kāni; bhayāni, bhayāni; rūpāni, rūpāni.

---

Always after ‘a’.

After the neuter bases ending in ‘a’, ‘yo’ always becomes ‘ni’.

---

Examples:

1. **Yāni** \= ya \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§218); ‘a’ is lengthened (§88)).  
2. **Tāni** \= ta \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§218); ‘a’ is lengthened (§88)).  
3. **Kāni** \= ka \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§218); ‘a’ is lengthened (§88)).  
4. **Bhayāni** \= bhaya \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§218); ‘a’ is lengthened (§88)).  
5. **Rūpāni** \= rūpa \+ yo (‘yo’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§218); ‘a’ is lengthened (§88)).

---

**219\. 195\. Si ’ṃ (446).** \[Si \+ aṃ, 2\]

**A**kārantehi napuṃsakaliṅgehi **si**vacanassa **aṃ** hoti niccaṃ. 

Sabbaṃ, yaṃ, taṃ, kaṃ, rūpaṃ.

---

‘Si’ \[becomes\] ‘aṃ’.

After the neuter bases ending in ‘a’, the inflection ‘si’ always becomes ‘aṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbaṃ** \= sabba \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§219); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Yaṃ** \= ya \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§219); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Taṃ** \= ta \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§219); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Kaṃ** \= ka \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§219); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Rūpaṃ** \= rūpa \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§219); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**220\. 74\. Sesato lopaṃ ga si pi (447).** \[Sesato \+ lopaṃ \+ gasi \+ api, 4\]

Tato niddiṭṭhehi liṅgehi sesato **ga si** icc ete lopam āpajjante. 

Bhoti itthi, sā itthī. Bho daṇḍi, so daṇḍī. Bho sattha, so satthā. Bho rāja, so rājā. 

**Sesato** ti kimatthaṃ? Puriso gacchati. 

**Ga-sī** ti kimatthaṃ? Itthiyā; satthussa.

---

After the remaining ones, ‘ga’ and ‘si’ are also elided.

After the remaining bases that have been shown,[^50] ‘ga’ and ‘si’ are elided.

---

Examples:

1. **Itthi** \= itthī \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ī’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘ī’ is shortened (§245); ‘ga’ is elided (§220)).  
2. **Itthī** \= itthī \+ si (‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
3. **Daṇḍi** \= daṇḍī \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§245); ‘ga’ is elided (§220)).  
4. **Daṇḍī** \= daṇḍī \+ si (‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
5. **Sattha** \= satthu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘ā’ is shortened (§246); ‘ga’ is elided (§220)).  
6. **Satthā** \= satthu \+ si (‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘ā’ (§199); ‘si’ is elided (§199)).  
7. **Rāja** \= rāja \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘ā’ is shortened (§246); ‘ga’ is elided (§220)).  
8. **Rājā** \= rāja \+ si (‘si’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§189); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

What is the purpose of saying “after the remaining ones”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no remaining bases, such as in:\] Puriso gacchati.

What is the purpose of saying “‘ga’ and ‘si’”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘ga’ or ‘si’ do not follow, such as in:\] itthiyā; satthussa.

---

**221\. 282\. Sabbāsam āvuso-’pasagga-nipātādīhi ca (448).** \[Sabbāsaṃ \+ āvuso-upasagga-nipāta-ādīhi \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbāsaṃ vibhattīnaṃ ekavacanabahuvacanānaṃ paṭhamā-dutiyā-tatiyā-catutthī-pañcamī-chaṭṭhī-sattamīnaṃ lopo hoti **āvuso upasagga nipāta** icc evamādīhi ca. 

Tvaṃ pan’ āvuso (Vin. ii, 1); tumhe pan’ āvuso (Vin. ii, 161); padaso dhammaṃ vāceyya (Vin. ii, 25); vihāraṃ sve upagaccheyya. Pa, parā, ni, nī, u, du, saṃ, vi, ava, anu, pari, adhi, abhi, pati, su, ā, ati, api, apa, upa. Pahāro, parābhavo, nihāro, nīhāro, uhāro, duhāro, saṃhāro, vihāro, avahāro, anuhāro, parihāro, adhihāro, abhihāro, patihāro, suhāro, āhāro, atihāro, apihāro, apahāro, upahāro. Evaṃ vīsati **upasaggehi** ca, yathā, tathā, evaṃ, khalu, kho, tatra, atho, atha, hi, tu, ca, vā, vo, haṃ, ahaṃ, alaṃ, eva, ho, aho, he, ahe, re, are. Evamādīhi **nipātehi** ca yojetabbāni. 

Caggahaṇam avadhāraṇatthaṃ.

---

And after “āvuso”, the prefixes, the particles, etc., \[there is elision\] of all.

After “āvuso”, the prefixes, the particles, etc., all the inflections, singular and plural, are elided, namely: first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth and seventh.

---

Examples:

1. **Tvaṃ pan’ āvuso** (and you, friend?)  
2. **Tumhe pan’ āvuso** (and you, friends?)  
3. **Padaso dhammaṃ vāceyya** (he should have the Dhamma recited word by word)   
4. **Vihāraṃ sve upagaccheyya** (he should approach the monastery tomorrow).  
5. **Āvuso** \= āvuso \+ si (‘si’ is elided (§221)).[^51]

The twenty prefixes are the following: pa, parā, ni, nī, u, du, saṃ, vi, ava, anu, pari, adhi, abhi, pati, su, ā, ati, api, apa, upa. \[Examples:\] Pahāro, parābhavo, nihāro, nīhāro, uhāro, duhāro, saṃhāro, vihāro, avahāro, anuhāro, parihāro, adhihāro, abhihāro, patihāro, suhāro, āhāro, atihāro, apihāro, apahāro, upahāro. 

Thus with the twenty prefixes; and they should be employed \[likewise\] with the particles yathā, tathā, evaṃ, khalu, kho, tatra, atho, atha, hi, tu, ca, vā, vo, haṃ, ahaṃ, alaṃ, eva, ho, aho, he, ahe, re, are, etc.

The taking of “ca” is for restricting \[the scope of this sutta\].

---

**222\. 342\. Pumassa liṅgādīsu samāsesu (449).** \[Pumassa \+ liṅga-ādīsu \+ samāsesu, 3\]

**Puma** icc etassa anto lopam āpajjate **liṅgā**dīsu parapadesu samāsesu. 

Pulliṅgaṃ, pumbhāvo, puṅkokilo. 

**Pumasse** ti kimatthaṃ? Itthiliṅgaṃ, napuṃsakaliṅgaṃ. 

**Liṅgādīsv** iti kimatthaṃ? Pumitthī. 

**Samāse**su ti kimatthaṃ? Pumassa liṅgaṃ.

---

In the compounds of “puma”, when \[the word\] “liṅga”, etc.

In the compounds, when \[the words\] “liṅga”, etc., are the following member,[^52] the end \[vowel\] of “puma” is elided.

---

Examples:

1. **Pulliṅgaṃ** \= puma \+ liṅgaṃ (‘a’ of “puma” is elided (§222); ‘m’ is substituted by ‘ṃ’ (§82); ‘ṃ’ is substituted by ‘l’ (“vā” in §31)).  
2. **Pumbhāvo** \= puma \+ bhāvo (‘a’ of “puma” is elided (§222); ‘m’ is substituted by ‘ṃ’ (§82); ‘ṃ’ is substituted by ‘m’ (§31)).  
3. **Puṅkokilo** \= puma \+ kokilo (‘a’ of “puma” is elided (§222); ‘m’ is substituted by ‘ṃ’ (§82); ‘ṃ’ is substituted by ‘ṅ’ (§31)).

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘puma’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no “puma”, such as in:\] itthiliṅgaṃ, napuṃsakaliṅgaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘liṅga’, etc., \[are the following member\]”? \[This sutta does not apply when “liṅga”, etc., are not the following member, such as in:\] Pumitthī.

What is the purpose of saying “in the compounds”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are no compounds, such as in:\] Pumassa liṅgaṃ.

---

**223\. 188\. Aṃ yam īto pasaññato (450).** \[Aṃ \+ yaṃ \+ īto \+ pasaññato, 4\]

**Aṃ**vacanassa **yaṃ** hoti vā **ī**to **pa**saññato. Itthiyaṃ, itthiṃ. 

**Pasaññato** ti kimatthaṃ? Daṇḍinaṃ, bhoginaṃ. 

**Am** iti kimatthaṃ? Itthīhi.

---

After ‘ī’ named ‘pa’, ‘aṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘yaṃ’.

Optionally, after ‘ī’ named ‘pa’, ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘yaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Itthiyaṃ** \= itthī \+ aṃ (‘ī’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘yaṃ’ (§223); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84)).  
2. **Itthiṃ** \= itthī \+ aṃ (‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘a’ of ‘aṃ’ is elided (§13)).

What is the purpose of saying “named ‘pa’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘pa’, such as in:\] daṇḍinaṃ, bhoginaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying ‘aṃ’? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘aṃ’, such as in:\] itthīhi.

---

**224\. 153\. Naṃ jhato katarassā (451).** \[Naṃ \+ jhato \+ katarassā, 3\]

Tasmā **jha**to katarassā **aṃ**vacanassa **naṃ** hoti. 

Daṇḍinaṃ, bhoginaṃ. 

**Jhato** ti kimatthaṃ? Vessabhuṃ. 

**Katarassā** ti kimatthaṃ? Kucchiṃ.

---

After ‘jha’ that has been shortened, \[there is\] ‘naṃ’.

After ‘jha’ that has been shortened, the inflection ‘aṃ’ becomes ‘naṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Daṇḍinaṃ** \= daṇḍī \+ aṃ (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘naṃ’ (§224)).  
2. **Bhoginaṃ** \= bhogī \+ aṃ (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘aṃ’ is substituted by ‘naṃ’ (§224)).

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘jha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘jha’, such as in:\] Vessabhuṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “that has been shortened”? \[This sutta does not apply when it has not been shortened, such as in:\] Kucchiṃ.

---

**225\. 151\. Yonaṃ no (452).** \[Yonaṃ \+ no, 2\]

Sabbesaṃ **yo**naṃ **jha**to katarassā **no** hoti. 

Daṇḍino bhogino; he daṇḍino, he bhogino. 

**Katarassā** ti kimatthaṃ? Aggayo, munayo, isayo. 

**Jhato** ti kimatthaṃ? Sayambhuno. 

**Yonan** ti kimatthaṃ? Daṇḍinā, bhoginā.

---

‘No’ of ‘yo’.

After ‘jha’ that has been shortened, all the ‘yo’ become ‘no’.

---

Examples:

1. **Daṇḍino** \= daṇḍī \+ yo (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§225)).  
2. **Bhogino** \= bhogī \+ yo (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘yo’ is substituted by ‘no’ (§225)).  
3. **He daṇḍino**, **he bhogino**: \[vocatives, with the same sequence\].

What is the purpose of saying “that has been shortened”? \[This sutta does not apply when it has not been shortened, such as in:\] aggayo, munayo, isayo.

What is the purpose of saying “after ‘jha’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘jha’, such as in:\] Sayambhuno.

What is the purpose of saying “of ‘yo’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘yo’, such as in:\] daṇḍinā, bhoginā.

---

**226\. 154\. Smiṃ ni (406).** \[Smiṃ \+ ni, 2\]

Tasmā **jha**to katarassā **smiṃ**vacanassa **ni-**ādeso hoti. 

Daṇḍini, bhogini. 

**Katarassā** ti kimatthaṃ? Byādhimhi.

---

‘Smiṃ’ \[becomes\] ‘ni’.

After ‘jha’ that has been shortened, the inflection ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’.

---

Examples:

1. **Daṇḍini** \= daṇḍī \+ smiṃ (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§226)).  
2. **Bhogini** \= bhogī \+ smiṃ (‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§84); ‘smiṃ’ is substituted by ‘ni’ (§226)).

What is the purpose of saying “that has been shortened”? \[This sutta does not apply when it has not been shortened, such as in:\] Byādhimhi.

---

**227\. 270\. Kissa ka ve ca (456).** \[Kissa \+ ka \+ ve \+ ca, 4\]

**Kim** icc etassa **ko** ca hoti **va**paccaye pare. 

Kva gato ’si tvaṃ Devānaṃpiyatissa. 

**Ca**ggahaṇena a**va**paccaye pare pi **ko** ca hoti. Ko taṃ ninditum arahati (Khu. i, 47); kathaṃ bodhayituṃ dhammaṃ.[^53] 

**Ve** ti kimatthaṃ? Kuto āgato ’si tvaṃ.

---

And when ‘va’ follows, \[there is\] ‘ka’ of “kiṃ”.

And when the suffix ‘va’ follows, “kiṃ” becomes “ka”.

---

Examples:

1. **Kva gato ’si tvaṃ Devānaṃpiyatissa** (where have you gone, Devānaṃpiyatissa?):

  * **Kva** \= kiṃ \+ va (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (§227); ‘a’ is elided (§404)).

By taking “ca”, also when other suffixes which are not ‘va’ follow, there is “ka”.

Examples:

1. **Ko taṃ ninditum arahati** (who deserves to blame him?); kathaṃ bodhayituṃ dhammaṃ (how to make the Dhamma known?):

  * **Ko** \= kiṃ \+ si (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (“ca” of §227); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
  * **Kathaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ thaṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (“ca” of §227)).

What is the purpose of saying ‘va’? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘va’ does not follow, such as in:\] Kuto āgato ’si tvaṃ (where have you come from?).

---

**228\. 272\. Ku hiṃ-haṃsu ca (460).** \[Ku \+ hiṃ-haṃsu \+ ca, 3\]

**Kim** icc etassa **ku** hoti **hiṃ haṃ** icc etesu ca. 

Kuhiṃ gacchasi, kuhaṃ gacchasi. 

**Ca**ggahaṇena **hiñcanaṃ-dācanaṃ**paccayesu paresu aññatthāpi **ku** hoti. Kuhiñcanaṃ, kudācanaṃ.

---

And, when ‘hiṃ’ and ‘haṃ’ follow, \[there is\] “ku”.

And when ‘hiṃ’ and ‘haṃ’ follow, “kiṃ” becomes “ku”.

---

Examples:

1. **Kuhiṃ gacchasi**, **kuhaṃ gacchasi** (where are you going?):

  * **Kuhiṃ** \= kiṃ \+ hiṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§228)).  
  * **Kuhaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ haṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§228)).

By taking “ca”, when the suffixes ‘hiñcanaṃ’ and ‘dācanaṃ’ follow, there is “ku” in other places too.

Examples:

1. **Kuhiñcanaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ hiñcanaṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (“ca” of §228)).  
2. **Kudācanaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ dācanaṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (“ca” of §228)).

---

**229\. 226\. Sesesu ca (457).** \[Sesesu \+ ca, 2\]

**Kim** icc etassa **ko** hoti sesesu vibhattipaccayesu paresu. 

Ko pakāro, kathaṃ, kaṃ pakāraṃ, kathaṃ. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And when the remaining ones follow.

When the remaining inflections and suffixes follow, “kiṃ” becomes “ka”.

---

Examples:

1. **Ko pakāro** (what manner?), **kathaṃ** (how); **kaṃ pakāraṃ** (what manner), **kathaṃ** (how):

  * **Ko** \= kiṃ \+ si (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (§229); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
  * **Kaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ aṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (§229); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
  * **Kathaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ thaṃ (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (§229)).

The taking of “ca” is for dragging.[^54]

---

**230\. 262\. Tra-to-thesu ca (460).** \[Tra-to-thesu \+ ca, 2\]

**Kim** icc etassa **ku** hoti **tra to tha** icc etesu ca. 

Kutra, kuto, kuttha. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And when ‘tra’, ‘to’ and ‘tha’ follow.

When ‘tra’, ‘to’ and ‘tha’ follow, “kiṃ” becomes “ku”.

---

Examples:

1. **Kutra** \= kiṃ \+ tra (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§230)).  
2. **Kuto** \= kiṃ \+ to (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§230)).  
3. **Kuttha** \= kiṃ \+ tha (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§230); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).

The taking of “ca” is for dragging.[^55]

---

**231\. 263\. Sabbass’ etassākāro vā (461).** \[Sabbassa \+ etassa \+ akāro \+ vā, 4\]

Sabbassa **eta**saddassa **a**kāro hoti vā **to tha** icc etesu. 

Ato, attha; etto, ettha.

---

Optionally, \[there is\] ‘a’ of the whole “eta”.

Optionally, when ‘to’ and ‘tha’ follow, the whole word “eta” becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Ato** \= eta \+ to (“eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§231)).  
2. **Attha** \= eta \+ tha (“eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§231); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
3. **Etto** \= eta \+ to (“eta” is substituted by ‘e’ (§233); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
4. **Ettha** \= eta \+ tha (“eta” is substituted by ‘e’ (§233); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).

---

**232\. 267\. Tre niccaṃ (462).** \[Tre \+ niccaṃ, 2\]

Sabbassa **eta**saddassa **a**kāro hoti niccaṃ **tra-**paccaye pare. 

Atra.

---

Always when ‘tra’ follows.

When the suffix ‘tra’ follows, the whole word “eta” always becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Atra** \= eta \+ tra (“eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§232)).

---

**233\. 264\. E to-thesu ca.**[^56] \[E \+ to-thesu \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbassa **eta**saddassa **e**kāro hoti vā **to tha** icc etesu. 

Etto, ato; ettha, attha.

---

And when ‘to’ and ‘tha’ follow, \[there is\] ‘e’.

Optionally, when ‘to’ and ‘tha’ follow, the whole word “eta” becomes ‘e’.

---

Examples:

1. **Etto** \= eta \+ to (“eta” is substituted by ‘e’ (§233); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
2. **Ettha** \= eta \+ tha (“eta” is substituted by ‘e’ (§233); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
3. **Ato** \= eta \+ to (“eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§231)).  
4. **Attha** \= eta \+ tha (“eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§231); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).

---

**234\. 265\. Imass’ i thaṃ-dāni-ha-to-dhesu ca (463).** \[Imassa \+ i \+ thaṃ-dāni-ha-to-dhesu \+ ca, 4\]

**Ima**saddassa sabbass’ eva **i**kāro hoti **thaṃ dāni ha to dha** icc etesu. 

Itthaṃ, idāni, iha, ito, idha.[^57]

---

And when ‘thaṃ’, ‘dāni’, ‘ha’, ‘to’ and ‘dha’ follow, \[there is\] ‘i’ of “ima”.

When ‘thaṃ’, ‘dāni’, ‘ha’, ‘to’ and ‘dha’ follow, the whole word “ima” becomes ‘i’.

---

Examples:

1. **Itthaṃ** \= ima \+ thaṃ (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
2. **Idāni** \= ima \+ dāni (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).  
3. **Iha** \= ima \+ ha (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).  
4. **Ito** \= ima \+ to (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).  
5. **Idha** \= ima \+ dha (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).

---

**235\. 281\. A dhunāmhi ca (464).** \[A \+ dhunāmhi \+ ca, 3\]

**Ima**saddassa sabbass’ eva **a**kāro hoti **dhunā**mhi paccaye pare. 

Adhunā. 

Caggahaṇam avadhāraṇatthaṃ.

---

And when ‘dhunā’ follows, \[there is\] ‘a’.

When the suffix ‘dhunā’ follows, the whole word “ima” becomes ‘a’.

---

Examples:

1. **Adhunā** \= ima \+ dhunā (“ima” is substituted by ‘a’ (§235)).

The taking of “ca” is for restricting \[the scope of this sutta\].

---

**236\. 280\. Eta rahimhi (465).** \[Eta \+ rahimhi, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **ima**saddassa **etā**deso hoti **rahi**mhi paccaye pare. 

Etarahi.

---

When ‘rahi’ follows, \[there is\] “eta”.

When the suffix ‘rahi’ follows, the whole word “ima” is substituted by “eta”.

---

Examples:

1. **Etarahi** \= ima \+ rahi (“ima” is substituted by “eta” (§236)).

---

**237\. 176\. Itthiyam ato āpaccayo (466).** \[Itthiyaṃ \+ ato \+ āpaccayo, 3\]

Itthiyaṃ vattamānāya **a**kārato **ā**paccayo hoti. 

Sabbā, yā, sā, kā, katarā.

---

In the feminine, after ‘a’, \[there is\] the suffix ‘ā’.

In the feminine, after the letter ‘a’, there is the suffix ‘ā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbā** \= sabba \+ si (‘ā’ is inserted after “sabba” (§237); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Yā** \= ya \+ si (‘ā’ is inserted after “ya” (§237); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Sā** \= ta \+ si (‘t’ of “ta” becomes ‘s’ (§174); ‘ā’ is inserted after “sa” (§237); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Kā** \= ka \+ si (‘ā’ is inserted after “ka” (§237); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Katarā** \= katara \+ si (‘ā’ is inserted after “katara” (§237); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**238\. 187\. Nadādito vā ī (467).** \[Nada-ādito \+ vā \+ ī, 3\]

**Nadā**dito vā a**nadā**dito vā itthiyaṃ vattamānāya **ī-**paccayo hoti. 

Nadī, mahī, kumārī, taruṇī, sakhī, itthī.[^58]

---

Optionally, after “nada”, etc., \[there is\] ‘ī’.

Optionally, in the feminine, after “nada”, etc., and after the words which are not “nadādi”,[^59] there is the suffix ‘ī’.

---

Examples:

1. **Nadī** \= nada \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “nada” (§238); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Mahī** \= maha \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “maha” (§238); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Kumārī** \= kumāra \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “kumāra” (§238); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Taruṇī** \= taruṇa \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “taruṇa” (§238); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Sakhī** \= sakha \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “sakha” (§238); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Itthī** \= itthi \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “itthi” (§238); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘i’ is elided (§83)).

---

**239\. 190\. Ṇava-ṇika-ṇeyya-ṇa-ntūhi (468).** \[Ṇava-ṇika-ṇeyya-ṇa-ntūhi, 1\]

**Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntu** icc etehi[^60] itthiyaṃ vattamānehi **ī-**paccayo hoti. 

Māṇavī, paṇḍavī, nāvikī, venateyyī, kunteyyī, gotamī, guṇavatī, sāmāvatī.

---

After ‘ṇava’, ‘ṇika’, ‘ṇeyya’, ‘ṇa’ and ‘ntu’.

In the feminine, after \[the words ending in\] ‘ṇava’, ‘ṇika’, ‘ṇeyya’, ‘ṇa’ and ‘ntu’, there is the suffix ‘ī’.

---

Examples:

1. **Māṇavī** \= māṇava \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “māṇava” (§239); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Paṇḍavī** \= paṇḍava \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “paṇḍava” (§239); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
3. **Nāvikī** \= nāvika \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “nāvika” (§239); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Venateyyī** \= venateyya \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “venateyya” (§239); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
5. **Kunteyyī** \= kunteyya \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “kunteyya” (§239); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Gotamī** \= gotama \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “gotama” (§239); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
7. **Guṇavatī** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “guṇavantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
8. **Sāmāvatī** \= sāmāvantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “sāmāvantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**240\. 193\. Pati-bhikkhu-rāj’-īkārantehi inī (469-70).** \[Pati-bhikkhu-rāja-īkārantehi \+ inī, 2\]

**Pati-bhikkhu-rāj’-ī**kārantehi itthiyaṃ vattamānehi **inī-**paccayo hoti. 

Gahapatānī, bhikkhunī, rājinī, hatthinī, daṇḍinī, medhāvinī, tapassinī.

---

After “pati”, “bhikkhu”, “rāja” and \[the words\] ending in the letter ‘ī’, \[there is\] ‘inī’.

In the feminine, after “pati”, “bhikkhu”, “rāja” and \[the words\] ending in the letter ‘ī’, there is the suffix ‘inī’.

---

Examples:

1. **Gahapatānī** \= gahapati \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “gahapati” (§240); ‘i’ of “pati” is substituted by ‘a’ (§91); ‘i’ of ‘inī’ is elided (§13); ‘a’ is lengthened (§16); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
2. **Bhikkhunī** \= bhikkhu \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “bhikkhu” (§240); ‘i’ of ‘inī’ is elided (§13); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
3. **Rājinī** \= rāja \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “rāja” (§240); ‘a’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
4. **Hatthinī** \= hatthī \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “hatthī” (§240); ‘ī’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
5. **Daṇḍinī** \= daṇḍī \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “daṇḍī” (§240); ‘ī’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
6. **Medhāvinī** \= medhāvī \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “medhāvī” (§240); ‘ī’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
7. **Tapassinī** \= tapassī \+ si (‘inī’ is inserted after “tapassī” (§240); ‘ī’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

---

**241\. 191\. Ntussa tam īkāre** (471). \[Ntussa \+ taṃ \+ īkāre, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **ntu**paccayassa **ta**kāro[^61] hoti vā **ī**kāre pare. 

Guṇavatī, guṇavantī; kulavatī, kulavantī; satimatī, satimantī; mahatī, mahantī; gottamatī, gottamantī.

---

Optionally, when ‘ī’ follows, \[there is\] ‘ta’ of ‘ntu’.

Optionally, when the letter ‘ī’ follows, the whole suffix ‘ntu’ becomes ‘ta’.

---

Examples:

1. **Guṇavatī** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “guṇavantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
2. **Guṇavantī** \= guṇavantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “guṇavantu” (§239); ‘u’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
3. **Kulavatī** \= kulavantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “kulavantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
4. **Kulavantī** \= kulavantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “kulavantu” (§239); ‘u’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
5. **Satimatī** \= satimantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “satimantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
6. **Satimantī** \= satimantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “satimantu” (§239); ‘u’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
7. **Mahatī** \= mahantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “mahantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
8. **Mahantī** \= mahantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “mahantu” (§239); ‘u’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
9. **Gottamatī** \= gottamantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “gottamantu” (§239); ‘ntu’ is substituted by ‘ta’ (§241); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
10. **Gottamantī** \= gottamantu \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “gottamantu” (§239); ‘u’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

---

**242\. 192\. Bhavato bhoto** (472). \[Bhavato \+ bhoto, 2\]

Sabbass’ eva **bhavanta**saddassa **bhotā**deso hoti **ī**kāre itthigate[^62] pare. 

Bhoti ayye (Vin. ii, 280), bhoti kaññe, bhoti Kharādiye (Khu. v, 4).

---

“Bhota” of “bhavanta”.

In the feminine, when ‘ī’ follows, the whole word “bhavanta” is substituted by “bhota”.

---

Examples:

1. **Bhoti ayye** (O lady\!), **bhoti kaññe** (O girl\!), **bhoti Kharādiye** (O Kharādiyā\!):  
  * **Bhoti** \= bhavanta \+ si (‘ī’ is inserted after “bhavanta” (§239); “bhavanta” is substituted by “bhota” (§242); ‘si’ is elided (§220); ‘a’ is elided (§83); ‘ī’ is shortened (§245)).

---

**243\. 110\. Bho ge tu (473-84).** \[Bho \+ ge \+ tu, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **bhavanta**saddassa **bho-**ādeso hoti **ge** pare. 

Bho purisa, bho aggi, bho rāja, bho sattha, bho daṇḍi, bho sayambhu. 

**Ge** ti kimatthaṃ? Bhavatā, bhavaṃ.[^63] 

**Tu**ggahaṇena aññasmim pi vacane sabbassa **bhavanta**saddassa **bhonta bhante bhonto** **bhadde bhotā bhoto** icc ete ādesā honti. Bhonta, bhante, bhonto, bhadde, bhotā, bhoto.

---

When ‘ga’ follows, \[there is\] “bho”.

When ‘ga’ follows, the whole word “bhavanta” is substituted by “bho”.

---

Examples:

1. **Bho purisa**, **bho aggi**, **bho rāja**, **bho sattha**, **bho daṇḍi**, **bho sayambhu**:

  * **Bho** \= bhavanta \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); “bhavanta” is substituted by “bho” (§243); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

What is the purpose of saying “when ‘ga’ follows”? \[This sutta does not apply when ‘ga’ does not follow, such as in:\] bhavatā, bhavaṃ.

By taking “tu”, also when other inflections follow, the whole word “bhavanta” is substituted by “bhonta”, “bhante”, “bhonto”, “bhadde”, “bhotā” and “bhoto”. Bhonta, bhante, bhonto, bhadde, bhotā, bhoto.

---

**244\. 72\. Akāra-pitādyantānam ā (475).** \[Akāra-pitu-ādi-antānaṃ \+ ā, 2\]

**A**kāro ca **pitā**dīnam anto ca **ā**ttam āpajjate **ge** pare. 

Bho purisā, bho rājā, bho pitā, bho mātā, bho satthā.

---

‘Ā’ of the letter ‘a’ and of the end of “pitu”, etc.

When ‘ga’ follows, the letter ‘a’ and the end \[vowel\] of “pitu”, etc., become ‘ā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Purisā** \= purisa \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
2. **Rājā** \= rāja \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
3. **Pitā** \= pitu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
4. **Mātā** \= mātu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘u’ of “mātu” becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
5. **Satthā** \= satthu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

---

**245\. 152\. Jha-la-pā rassaṃ (477).** \[Jha-la-pā \+ rassaṃ, 2\]

**Jha la pa** icc ete rassam āpajjante **ge** pare. 

Bho daṇḍi, bho sayambhu, bhoti itthi, bhoti vadhu.

---

‘Jha’, ‘la’ and ‘pa’ \[become\] short.

When ‘ga’ follows, ‘jha’, ‘la’ and ‘pa’ are shortened.

---

Examples:

1. **Daṇḍi** \= daṇḍī \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ī’ is named ‘jha’ (§58); ‘ī’ is shortened (§245); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
2. **Sayambhu** \= sayambhū \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ū’ is named ‘la’ (§58); ‘ū’ is shortened (§245); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
3. **Itthi** \= itthī \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ī’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘ī’ is shortened (§245); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
4. **Vadhu** \= vadhū \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘ū’ is named ‘pa’ (§59); ‘ū’ is shortened (§245); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

---

**246\. 73\. Ākāro vā (476, 478-9).** \[Ākāro \+ vā, 2\]

**Ā**kāro rassam āpajjate vā **ge** pare. 

Bho rāja, bho rājā; bho atta, bho attā; bho sakha, bho sakhā; bho sattha, bho satthā.

---

Optionally, the letter ‘ā’.

Optionally, when ‘ga’ follows, ‘ā’ is shortened.

---

Examples:

1. **Rāja** \= rāja \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘ā’ is shortened (§246); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
2. **Rājā** \= rāja \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
3. **Atta** \= atta \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘ā’ is shortened (§246); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
4. **Attā** \= atta \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
5. **Sakha** \= sakha \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘ā’ is shortened (§246); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
6. **Sakhā** \= sakha \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘a’ becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
7. **Sattha** \= satthu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘ā’ is shortened (§246); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).  
8. **Satthā** \= satthu \+ si (‘si’ is named ‘ga’ (§57); ‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘ā’ (§244); ‘si’ is elided (§220)).

**Iti nāma-kappe catuttho kaṇḍo**

**Thus ends the fourth section of the noun chapter**

**PAÑCAMA-KAṆḌA**

**FIFTH SECTION**

---

**247\. 261\. Tvādayo vibhattisaññāyo (492).** \[To-ādayo \+ vibhattisaññāyo, 2\]

**To** ādi yesaṃ paccayānaṃ, te honti **tvā**dayo. Te paccayā **tvā**dayo vibhattisaññā ’va daṭṭhabbā. 

Sabbato, yato, tato, kuto, ato, ito, sabbadā, yadā, tadā, kadā, idha, idāni.

---

Those beginning with ‘tvā’ are named “inflections”.

The suffixes beginning with ‘to’ are \[called\] ‘tvādi’. These suffixes ‘tvādi’ are to be regarded as “inflections”.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbato** \= sabba \+ to.  
2. **Yato** \= ya \+ to.  
3. **Tato** \= ta \+ to.  
4. **Kuto** \= kiṃ \+ to (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§230)).  
5. **Ato** \= eta \+ to (“eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§231)).  
6. **Ito** \= ima \+ to (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).  
7. **Sabbadā** \= sabba \+ dā.  
8. **Yadā** \= ya \+ dā.  
9. **Tadā** \= ta \+ dā.  
10. **Kadā** \= kiṃ \+ dā (“kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (“ca” of §227)).  
11. **Idha** \= ima \+ dha (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).  
12. **Idāni** \= ima \+ dāni (“ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).[^64]

---

**248\. 260\. Kvaci to pañcamyatthe (493).** \[Kvaci \+ to \+ pañcamī-atthe, 3\]

Kvaci **to**paccayo hoti pañcamyatthe. 

Sabbato, yato, tato, kuto, ato, ito. 

**Kvacī** ti kimatthaṃ? Sabbasmā, imasmā.

---

Sometimes, \[there is\] ‘to’ in the meaning of the fifth.

Sometimes, in the meaning of the fifth \[inflection\], there is the suffix ‘to’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbato** \= sabba \+ to (‘to’ is used in the meaning of the fifth inflection (§248)).  
2. **Yato** \= ya \+ to (‘to’ is used in the meaning of the fifth inflection (§248)).  
3. **Tato** \= ta \+ to (‘to’ is used in the meaning of the fifth inflection (§248)).  
4. **Kuto** \= kiṃ \+ to (‘to’ is used in the meaning of the fifth inflection (§248); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§230)).  
5. **Ato** \= eta \+ to (‘to’ is used in the meaning of the fifth inflection (§248); “eta” is substituted by ‘a’ (§231)).  
6. **Ito** \= ima \+ to (‘to’ is used in the meaning of the fifth inflection (§248); “ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).

What is the purpose of saying “sometimes”? \[To allow exceptions to this sutta, such as in:\] sabbasmā, imasmā.


---

**249\. 266\. Tra-tha sattamiyā sabbanāmehi (494).** \[Tra-tha \+ sattamiyā \+ sabbanāmehi, 3\]

**Tra tha** icc ete paccayā honti sattamyatthe sabbanāmehi. 

Sabbatra, sabbattha; yatra, yattha; tatra, tattha.

---

After the pronouns, in the seventh, \[there are\] ‘tra’ and ‘tha’.

After the pronouns, in the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], there are the suffixes ‘tra’ and ‘tha’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbatra** \= sabba \+ tra (‘tra’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§249)).  
2. **Sabbattha** \= sabba \+ tha (‘tha’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§249); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
3. **Yatra** \= ya \+ tra (‘tra’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§249)).  
4. **Yattha** \= ya \+ tha (‘tha’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§249); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).  
5. **Tatra** \= ta \+ tra (‘tra’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§249)).  
6. **Tattha** \= ta \+ tha (‘tha’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§249); ‘t’ is doubled (§28)).

---

**250\. 268\. Sabbato dhi (502).** \[Sabbato \+ dhi, 2\]

**Sabba** icc etasmā **dhi**-paccayo hoti kvaci sattamyatthe. 

Sabbadhi, sabbasmiṃ.

---

After “sabba”, \[there is\] ‘dhi’.

Sometimes, in the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], after “sabba” there is the suffix ‘dhi’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sabbadhi** \= sabba \+ dhi (‘dhi’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§250)).  
2. **Sabbasmiṃ** \= sabba \+ smiṃ (form with the regular inflection, allowed by “sometimes”).

---

**251\. 269\. Kiṃsmā vo (499).** \[Kiṃsmā \+ vo, 2\]

**Kim** icc etasmā **va**-paccayo hoti sattamyatthe. 

Kva gato ’si tvaṃ devānaṃ piyatissa.

---

After “kiṃ” \[there is\] ‘va’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], after “kiṃ” there is the suffix ‘va’.

---

Examples:

1. **Kva gato ’si tvaṃ Devānaṃ Piyatissa** (where have you gone, Devānaṃ Piyatissa?):

  * **Kva** \= kiṃ \+ va (‘va’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§251); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (§227); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).

---

**252\. 271\. Hiṃ-haṃ-hiñcanaṃ (500).** \[Hiṃ-haṃ-hiñcanaṃ, 1\]

**Kim** icc etasmā **hiṃ haṃ hiñcanaṃ** icc ete paccayā honti sattamyatthe. 

Kuhiṃ, kuhaṃ, kuhiñcanaṃ.

---

‘Hiṃ’, ‘haṃ’ and ‘hiñcanaṃ’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], after “kiṃ” there are the suffixes ‘hiṃ’, ‘haṃ’ and ‘hiñcanaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Kuhiṃ** \= kiṃ \+ hiṃ (‘hiṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§252); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§228)).  
2. **Kuhaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ haṃ (‘haṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§252); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (§228)).  
3. **Kuhiñcanaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ hiñcanaṃ (‘hiñcanaṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§252); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (“ca” of §228)).

---

**253\. 273\. Tamhā ca (501).** \[Tamhā \+ ca, 2\]

**Ta**mhā ca **hiṃ haṃ** icc ete paccayā honti sattamyatthe. 

Tahiṃ, tahaṃ. 

**Ca**ggahaṇaṃ **hiñcana**ggahaṇa-nivattanatthaṃ.

---

And after “ta”.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], after “ta” there are the suffixes ‘hiṃ’ and ‘haṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tahiṃ** \= ta \+ hiṃ (‘hiṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§253)).  
2. **Tahaṃ** \= ta \+ haṃ (‘haṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§253)).

The taking of “ca” is for excluding the taking of ‘hiñcanaṃ’.

---

**254\. 274\. Imasmā ha-dhā ca (503).** \[Imasmā \+ ha-dhā \+ ca, 3\]

**Ima**smā **ha dha** icc ete paccayā honti sattamyatthe. 

Iha, idha. 

Caggahaṇam avadhāraṇatthaṃ.

---

And after “ima” \[there are\] ‘ha’ and ‘dha’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], after “ima” there are the suffixes ‘ha’ and ‘dha’.

---

Examples:

1. **Iha** \= ima \+ ha (‘ha’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§254); “ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).  
2. **Idha** \= ima \+ dha (‘dha’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§254); “ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).

The taking of “ca” is for restricting \[the scope of this sutta with respect to words other than “ima”\].

---

**255\. 275\. Yato hiṃ (504).** \[Yato \+ hiṃ, 2\]

Tasmā **ya**to **hiṃ**-paccayo hoti sattamyatthe. 

Yahiṃ.

---

After “ya” \[there is\] ‘hiṃ’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], after “ya” there is the suffix ‘hiṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Yahiṃ** \= ya \+ hiṃ (‘hiṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection (§255)).

---

**256\. 0\. Kāle.** \[Kāle, 1\]

“Kāle” icc etaṃ adhikāratthaṃ veditabbaṃ.

---

In time.

It should be understood that “kāle” has the value of a governing aphorism.[^65]

---

**257\. 276\. Kiṃ-sabb’-aññ’-eka-ya-kuhi dā-dācanaṃ (505).** \[Kiṃ-sabba-añña-eka-ya-kuhi \+ dā-dācanaṃ, 2\]

**Kiṃ sabba añña eka ya ku** icc etehi **dā dācanaṃ** icc ete paccayā honti kāle sattamyatthe. 

Kadā, sabbadā, aññadā, ekadā, yadā, kudācanaṃ.

---

After “kiṃ”, “sabba”, “añña”, “eka”, “ya” and “ku”, \[there are\] ‘dā’ and ‘dācanaṃ’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], in time, after “kiṃ”, “sabba”, “añña”, “eka”, “ya” and “ku” there are the suffixes ‘dā’ and ‘dācanaṃ’.

---

Examples:

1. **Kadā** \= kiṃ \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ka” (§227)).  
2. **Sabbadā** \= sabba \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257)).  
3. **Aññadā** \= añña \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257)).  
4. **Ekadā** \= eka \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257)).  
5. **Yadā** \= ya \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257)).  
6. **Kudācanaṃ** \= kiṃ \+ dācanaṃ (‘dācanaṃ’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257); “kiṃ” is substituted by “ku” (“ca” of §228)).

---

**258\. 278\. Tamhā dāni ca (506).** \[Tamhā \+ dāni \+ ca, 3\]

**Ta** icc etasmā **dāni dā** icc ete paccayā honti kāle sattamyatthe. 

Tadāni, tadā. Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And after “ta” \[there is\] ‘dāni’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], in time, after “ta” there are the suffixes ‘dāni’ and ‘dā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Tadāni** \= ta \+ dāni (‘dāni’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§258)).  
2. **Tadā** \= ta \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (“ca” of §258)).

The taking of “ca” is for the dragging of ‘dā’.

---

**259\. 279\. Imasmā rahi-dhunā-dāni ca (507).** \[Imasmā \+ rahi-dhunā-dāni \+ ca, 3\]

**Ima**smā **rahi dhunā dāni** icc ete paccayā honti kāle sattamyatthe. 

Etarahi, adhunā, idāni. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And after “ima” \[there are\] ‘rahi’, ‘dhunā’ and ‘dāni’.

In the meaning of the seventh \[inflection\], in time, after “ima” there are the suffixes ‘rahi’, ‘dhunā’ and ‘dāni’.

---

Examples:

1. **Etarahi** \= ima \+ rahi (‘rahi’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§259); “ima” is substituted by “eta” (§236)).  
2. **Adhunā** \= ima \+ dhunā (‘dhunā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§259); “ima” is substituted by ‘a’ (§235)).  
3. **Idāni** \= ima \+ dāni (‘dāni’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§259); “ima” is substituted by ‘i’ (§234)).

The taking of “ca” is for the dragging of ‘dāni’.

---

**260\. 277\. Sabbassa so dāmhi vā (508).** \[Sabbassa \+ so \+ dāmhi \+ vā, 4\]

**Sabba** icc etassa **sa**kārādeso hoti vā **dā**mhi paccaye pare. 

Sadā, sabbadā.

---

Optionally, when ‘dā’ follows, \[there is\] ‘sa’ of “sabba”.

Optionally, when the suffix ‘dā’ follows, “sabba” is substituted by ‘sa’.

---

Examples:

1. **Sadā** \= sabba \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257); “sabba” is substituted by ‘sa’ (§260)).  
2. **Sabbadā** \= sabba \+ dā (‘dā’ is used in the meaning of the seventh inflection to indicate time (§257)).

---

**261\. 369\. Avaṇṇo ye lopañ ca (509).** \[Avaṇṇo \+ ye \+ lopaṃ \+ ca, 4\]

**A**vaṇṇo **ye** paccaye pare lopam āpajjate. 

Bāhussaccaṃ (Khu. i, 4); paṇḍiccaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); vepullaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); kāruññaṃ; kosallaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); sāmaññaṃ (Khu. vi, 177); sohajjaṃ.

---

And when ‘ya’ follows, ‘a’ and ‘ā’ are elided.

When the suffix ‘ya’ follows, the letters ‘a’ and ‘ā’ are elided.[^66]

---

**262\. 391\. Vuḍḍhassa jo iy’-iṭṭhesu (513).** \[Vuḍḍhassa \+ jo \+ iya-iṭṭhesu, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **vuḍḍha**saddassa **jo**-ādeso hoti **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Jeyyo, jeṭṭho (D. ii, 13).

---

When ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, \[there is\] ‘jo’ of “vuḍḍha”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the whole word “vuḍḍha” is substituted by “jo”.[^67]

---

**263\. 392\. Pasatthassa so ca (512).** \[Pasatthassa \+ so \+ ca, 3\]

Sabbass’ eva **pasattha**saddassa **so**-ādeso hoti, **jā**deso ca **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Seyyo (A. i, 130), seṭṭho (D. ii, 13), jeyyo, jeṭṭho (D. ii, 13).

---

And \[there is\] ‘sa’ of “pasattha”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the whole word “pasattha” is substituted by “sa”, and \[there is\] also the substitution “ja”.[^68]

---

**264\. 393\. Antikassa nedo (514).** \[Antikassa \+ nedo, 2\]

Sabbassa **antika**saddassa **nedā**deso hoti **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Nediyo, nediṭṭho.

---

“Neda” of “antika”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the whole word “antika” is substituted by “neda”.[^69]

---

**265\. 394\. Bāḷhassa sādho (515).** \[Bāḷhassa \+ sādho, 2\]

Sabbassa **bāḷha**saddassa **sādhā**deso hoti **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Sādhiyo, sādhiṭṭho.

---

“Sādha” of “bāḷha”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the whole word “bāḷha” is substituted by “sādha”.[^70]

---

**266\. 395\. Appassa kaṇ (516).** \[Appassa \+ kaṇ, 2\]

Sabbassa **appa**saddassa **kaṇ**ādeso hoti **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Kaṇiyo, kaṇiṭṭho.

---

“Kaṇ” of “appa”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the whole word “appa” is substituted by “kaṇ”.[^71]

---

**267\. 396\. Yuvānañ ca (517).** \[Yuvānaṃ \+ ca, 2\]

Sabbassa **yuva**saddassa **kaṇ**ādeso hoti **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Kaniyo, kaniṭṭho. 

Caggahaṇam anukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

---

And of “yuva”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the whole word “yuva” is substituted by “kaṇ”.[^72]

The taking of “ca” is for the dragging of the word “kaṇ”.

---

**268\. 397\. Vantu-mantu-vīnañ ca lopo (518).** \[Vantu-mantu-vīnaṃ \+ ca \+ lopo, 3\]

**Vantu mantu vī** icc etesaṃ paccayānaṃ lopo hoti **iya iṭṭha** icc etesu paccayesu. 

Guṇiyo, guṇiṭṭho, satiyo, satiṭṭho, medhiyo, medhiṭṭho.

---

And there is elision of “vantu”, “mantu” and “vī”.

When the suffixes ‘iya’ and ‘iṭṭha’ follow, the suffixes “vantu”, “mantu” and “vī” are elided.

---

Examples:

1. **Guṇiyo** \= guṇavantu \+ iya \+ si (“vantu” is elided (§268); ‘a’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
2. **Guṇiṭṭho** \= guṇavantu \+ iṭṭha \+ si (“vantu” is elided (§268); ‘a’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
3. **Satiyo** \= satimantu \+ iya \+ si (“mantu” is elided (§268); ‘i’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
4. **Satiṭṭho** \= satimantu \+ iṭṭha \+ si (“mantu” is elided (§268); ‘i’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
5. **Medhiyo** \= medhāvī \+ iya \+ si (“vī” is elided (§268); ‘ā’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).  
6. **Medhiṭṭho** \= medhāvī \+ iṭṭha \+ si (“vī” is elided (§268); ‘ā’ is elided (§83); ‘si’ is substituted by ‘o’ (§104)).[^73]

---

**269\. 41\. Yavataṃ ta-la-ṇa-dakārānaṃ byañjanāni ca-la-ña-ja-kārattaṃ (104, 106, 119, 121-5).** \[Yavataṃ \+ ta-la-ṇa-da-kārānaṃ \+ byañjanāni \+ ca-la-ña-ja-kārattaṃ, 4\]

**Ya**kāravantānaṃ **ta-la-ṇa-da**kārānaṃ byañjanāni **ca-la-ña-ja**-kārattam āpajjante yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. 

Bāhussaccaṃ; paṇḍiccaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); vepullaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); kāruññaṃ (Khu. vi, 177); kosallaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); nepuññaṃ (Abhi. iii, 128); sāmaññaṃ (S. iii, 
20); sohajjaṃ. 

**Yavata**m ti kimatthaṃ? Tiṇadalaṃ. 

**Ta-la-ṇa-da-kārāna**m iti kimatthaṃ? Ālasyaṃ (Abhi. iii, 364); ārogyaṃ (Khu. i, 395). 

**Byañjanāni** iti kimatthaṃ? Maccunā. 

**Kāra**ggahaṇaṃ kimatthaṃ? **Ya**kārassa **ma**kārādesañāpanatthaṃ. Opammaṃ (M. ii, 42).

---

The consonants ‘t’, ‘l’, ‘ṇ’ and ‘d’ which have ‘y’ \[become\] the letters ‘c’, ‘l’, ‘ñ’ and ‘j’.

The consonants ‘t’, ‘l’, ‘ṇ’ and ‘d’ which have the letter ‘y’ become respectively the letters ‘c’, ‘l’, ‘ñ’ and ‘j’.

---

Examples:

1. **Bāhussaccaṃ** \= bāhussuta \+ ya (the last ‘a’ is elided (§261); ‘t’ together with ‘y’ becomes ‘c’ (§269); ‘c’ is doubled (§28); ‘u’ becomes ‘a’ (§404)).

What is the purpose of saying “which have ‘ya’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there is no ‘ya’, such as in:\] Tiṇadalaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “the letters ‘t’, ‘l’, ‘ṇ’ and ‘d’”? \[This sutta does not apply when there are none of these letters, such as in:\] Ālasyaṃ, ārogyaṃ.

What is the purpose of saying “consonants”?[^74] \[This sutta does not apply when there are no consonants, such as in:\] Maccunā.

What is the purpose of taking ‘kāra’? To make known the substitution of the letter ‘ya’ by the letter ‘ma’, such as in: Opammaṃ.[^75]

---

**270\. 120\. Amha-tumha-ntu-rāja-brahm’-atta-sakha-satthu-pitādihi smā nā ’va (542).** \[Amha-tumha-ntu-rāja-brahma-atta-sakha-satthu-pitu-ādīhi \+ smā \+ nā \+ iva, 4\]

**Amha tumha ntu rāja brahma atta sakha satthu pitu** icc evamādīhi **smā**vacanaṃ **nā** ’va daṭṭhabbaṃ. 

Mayā, tayā, guṇavatā, raññā, brahmunā, attanā, sakhinā, satthārā, pitarā, mātarā, bhātarā, dhītarā, kattārā, vattārā. 

**Etehī** ti kimatthaṃ? Purisā.

---

After “amha”, “tumha”, “ntu”, “rāja”, “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha”, “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., ‘smā’ is like ‘nā’.

After “amha”, “tumha”, “ntu”, “rāja”, “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha”, “satthu”, “pitu”, etc., the inflection ‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’.

---

Examples:

1. **Mayā** \= amha \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); “amha” together with the inflection is substituted by “mayā” (§145)).  
2. **Tayā** \= tumha \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); “tumha” together with the inflection is substituted by “tayā” (§145)).  
3. **Guṇavatā** \= guṇavantu \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); “ntu” together with the inflection is substituted by ‘tā’ (§127)).  
4. **Raññā** \= rāja \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); “rāja” together with the inflection is substituted by “raññā” (§137)).  
5. **Brahmunā** \= brahma \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); ‘a’ of “brahma” becomes ‘u’ (§198)).  
6. **Attanā** \= atta \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); see also §214).  
7. **Sakhinā** \= sakha \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); ‘a’ of “sakha” is substituted by ‘i’ (§194)).  
8. **Satthārā** \= satthu \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); ‘u’ of “satthu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
9. **Pitarā** \= pitu \+ smā (‘smā’ is to be regarded as ‘nā’ (§270); ‘u’ of “pitu” becomes ‘āra’ (§200); ‘nā’ is substituted by ‘ā’ (§207); ‘ā’ of “āra” is shortened (§209); ‘a’ is elided (§83)).  
10. **Mātarā**, **Bhātarā**, **Dhītarā**: \[like pitarā\].  
11. **Kattārā**, **Vattārā**: \[like satthārā\].

What is the purpose of saying “after these”? \[This sutta does not apply when they are not these, such as in:\] Purisā.

**Iti nāmakappe pañcamo kaṇḍo**

**Thus ends the fifth section of the noun chapter**

**Nāmakappo Niṭṭhito**

**End of the Noun Chapter.**

[^1]:  This is an *adhikāra-sutta*, a governing aphorism of general application that rules the whole work. There are three kinds of *adhikāra*: (1) *sīha-gatika* (“lion's movement”), which affects all the preceding and following suttas; (2) *maṇḍūka-gatika* (“frog's movement”), which affects only certain suttas related to one another; and (3) *yathānupubbika*, which affects in sequential order, especially the following suttas. The present sutta is of the first kind. Everything mentioned in this text is in accordance with the word of the Buddha, that is, with the language used by him.

[^2]:  The particle “ca” in the sutta serves to include the roots (*dhātu*) as well; these too should be established in conformity with the Word of the Victorious One. The term *liṅga* can be translated in this context as “base” or “stem”; in other grammatical contexts it means gender. The real examples of this sutta are the bases “esa”, “amha”, “satthu”, “brahma”, “atta”, “sakha” and “rāja”; since it is impossible to show the bases without their inflections (*nāma-vibhatti*), they are presented here as completed words –base plus inflection–.

[^3]:  The particle “ca” in the sutta indicates that the inflections are also employed after the indeclinables that end with the suffixes ‘tave’, ‘tunā’, etc.

[^4]:  The inflections are divided into eight pairs: first (*paṭhamā*), second (*dutiyā*), third (*tatiyā*), fourth (*catutthī*), fifth (*pañcamī*), sixth (*chaṭṭhī*), seventh (*sattamī*) and eighth (*aṭṭhamī*). *Aṭṭhamī* is the name of the endings of the vocative case and, in the order of the declensions, it is placed after the first. The first of each pair is singular and the second is plural. Pāḷi has no dual number.

[^5]:  The particle “ca” in the sutta indicates that the verbs (ākhyāta) should also be formed in conformity with the Word of the Victorious One. The difference between this sutta and §53 is that by §53 the base should be established in conformity with the Word of the Victorious One, while by this sutta the completed form should be established in conformity with the Word of the Victorious One. Example: according to §53, the base of the word “puriso” (nominative singular) should be put down as “purisa” and not as “puruṣa” (as in Sanskrit); when the word is formed, it should be “puriso” and not “puruṣaḥ”.

[^6]:  Both “bhoti” and “ayye”, etc., are examples. When forming these words, the inflection ‘si’ is employed; then, by this sutta, ‘si’ is named ‘ga’, and the application of the other suttas follows to complete the word.

[^7]:  There are two inflections ‘si’: (1) the ‘si’ of the nominative singular (paṭhamā) and (2) the ‘si’ of the vocative singular (ālapana), to which this sutta gives the name ‘ga’. The purpose of this name is to allow the application of the morphological procedures prescribed by the pertinent suttas, and to distinguish precisely between both inflections ‘si’. This explanation was taken from the book of the Venerable A. Thitzana. 

[^8]:  Both “bhoti” and “ayye”, etc., are examples. When forming these words, the inflection ‘si’ is employed; then, by this sutta, ‘si’ is named ‘ga’, and the application of the other suttas follows to complete the word.

[^9]:  When forming “rattiyā” (by night), first the base “ratti” is put down, then the inflection ‘nā’ is employed. Then, by this sutta, ‘i’ of the feminine form “ratti” is named ‘pa’. Then the suttas “Pato yā”, etc., are applied.

[^10]:  ‘Saṃ’ and ‘sā’ are substitutions of the inflections ‘smiṃ’ and ‘sa’ respectively, after pronouns named ‘gha’ and ‘pa’ (see §179). This sutta prescribes the additional insertion of ‘s’ before those substitutions.

[^11]:  “Ca” means here *sampiṇḍana* (collecting), since both this sutta and §70 have a similar function: in §70, ‘jha’ and ‘la’ are substituted by ‘iya’ and ‘uva’; in this sutta, by ‘ya’ and ‘va’. The “ca” gathers both operations as a set. Ref. Ven. A. Thitzana.

[^12]:  First plural inflection, second plural inflection and vocative plural.

[^13]:  ‘Ava’ is one of the twenty prefixes (*upasagga*). The term “viparīta” –change, reversion; transformation– is the name given to the letter ‘o’ which is the transformation of the prefix ‘ava’, and to the letter ‘u’ which is the transformation of the letter ‘o’. E.g., in “ava-gacchati”, he goes down, the prefix ‘ava’ becomes ‘o’ (*o-gacchati*) and afterwards this becomes ‘u’ (*u-gacchati*); (Kac. §79, Rū. §45-46, Sad. iii §25-26).

[^14]:  Note: both changes, ‘ava’ → ‘o’ and ‘o’ → ‘u’, are called *viparīta* (transformation).

[^15]:  To prevent the application of the optional rule (*vibhāsa-nivattana*) here.

[^16]:  “Remains natural” (*pakati*) means that the vowel does not change.

[^75]:  Translator's note (Ven. A. Thitzana): These forms are abstract nouns formed with the taddhita suffix ṇya, “the state of being X”, and their full derivation is shared between this chapter and the taddhita one. Take paṇḍiccaṃ as a model. (1) ṇya is added to the base (Thitzana's sutta 360): paṇḍita \+ ṇya. (2) ‘ṇ’ of the suffix is elided (his sutta 396): paṇḍita \+ ya. (3) The end vowel of the base is elided before ‘ya’ (§261): paṇḍit \+ ya. (4) The consonant, together with ‘y’, becomes the corresponding one according to this sutta —‘t’ into ‘c’, ‘l’ into ‘l’, ‘ṇ’ into ‘ñ’, ‘d’ into ‘j’—: paṇḍic°. (5) The resulting consonant is doubled (§28): paṇḍicca. (6) The vowel of the base receives vuddhi (his sutta 400): ‘u’ becomes ‘o’ in kosallaṃ and sohajjaṃ; ‘i’ becomes ‘e’ in vepullaṃ and nepuññaṃ; ‘a’ is lengthened in kāruññaṃ and sāmaññaṃ (in bāhussaccaṃ, on the other hand, ‘u’ of “suta” becomes ‘a’; Nandisena cites §404). (7) Finally, being neuter, ‘si’ is substituted by ‘aṃ’ (§219) and the end ‘a’ is elided (§83): paṇḍiccaṃ. The bases of the eight examples: bāhussuta → bāhussaccaṃ; paṇḍita → paṇḍiccaṃ; vipula → vepullaṃ; karuṇā → kāruññaṃ; kusala → kosallaṃ; nipuṇa → nepuññaṃ; samaṇa —or samāna— → sāmaññaṃ; suhada → sohajjaṃ. In opammaṃ (upamā \+ ṇya) ‘y’ is substituted by ‘m’, according to the fourth kimatthaṃ of this sutta, with ‘u’ become ‘o’.
